# Progress

Live state cursor — repo-scoped, ~14-day rolling. The "where did I leave off, what just shipped, what's open" surface.

Sections:

- **Currently running** — 1-2 sentences on what's actively in flight
- **Recently shipped** — newest first; bullets older than 14 days are pruned automatically by the post-merge journal-sync
- **Open followups** — grouped by area; deferred work that's not yet issue-shaped or tech-debt-shaped

This file is updated through skills, not raw markdown edits:

- `/log followup "<text>" --area=<name>` to add an open followup
- `/done <slug-or-keyword>` to flip a followup → recently-shipped
- `/log decision` for decisions (ADR-grade lands in [`docs/adr/`](adr/README.md); sub-architectural in [decisions.md](decisions.md)) — not here

Drift is gated by `npm run lint:docs` — **FAILs** when `last-updated` is >7 days old AND there are commits since.

### scripts

#### `device:check` reports a device as free to test on while a live run holds its lock, and its advice line says so in as many words. `[fid: device-check-ignores-device-lock]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

[`scripts/device-check.js`](../scripts/device-check.js) probes ECP only and never reads the device lock, so it prints `hardware IS available, so run the tests` about a device another run is mid-suite on. Observed 2026-09-06: it said exactly that about `.178` while a suite from a second checkout held the lock, and the run launched on that advice died at `acquireDeviceLock` after a full `npm run build`. The lock behaved correctly — this is the ADVICE being wrong, and it is wrong at the one moment the script exists to be right. It matters more than it looks because `device:check` is what `AGENTS.md` tells an agent to run before claiming hardware is unavailable, so a wrong answer here is laundered into a confident claim. **Fix shape:** `device-lock.js` already exports the `status` read (it powers `npm run device:status`); call it per host and downgrade the verdict line to name the holder, rather than adding a second notion of availability. Note this is separate from the existing caveat that an answering ODC port is only a TCP connect.

#### Expand automated store screenshots from the 5 marketing languages to ALL ~99 locale files, to surface the default Roku OS font's blast radius — boxes/tofu for scripts the system font doesn't cover are EXPECTED and the point of capturing them. From #642. `[fid: screenshots-expand-to-all-locales]` `[captured 2026-06-10]`

#### `.device-runs/` has no operator-facing verb, and it now holds a secret at rest indefinitely. `[fid: device-runs-dir-no-operator-verb]` `[captured 2026-08-11]`

The registry snapshot (`.device-runs/registry-<host>.json`) is the device's WHOLE registry including `authToken` for the active server. Moving it out of `out/` fixed a real recovery bug, but it also removed the incidental cleanup a `npm run build` used to perform — so nothing deletes it now except a verified restore or `npm run rta:restore`, and the case that strands it is precisely a restore that never converged (the `authToken` re-mint that caused this is fixed — see [`restore-compares-credentials-by-presence`](decisions.md) — but any residual has the same shape). The ledger `.device-runs/<kind>/runs.jsonl` is likewise append-only with nothing pruning it. **Evidence, 2026-08-11 (friction event, not speculation):** during review of this branch a stranded snapshot on `.177` was destroyed by an `rm -rf .device-runs` issued against a misread path, and recovery needed hand-writing `JellyRock.active_user` + `server` back over ODC — there was no tooling for either half, and no way after the fact to know what the directory had held. The ledger history from the 2026-08-10/11 verification runs was lost with it. That is the friction: the recovery state has no verb, so operating on it means knowing which gitignored path to delete and which registry keys to hand-write. **Fix shape:** extend [`scripts/device-lock.js`](../scripts/device-lock.js) (which already owns `status` / `release`) with a `clean` subcommand that prunes ledgers and refuses to touch a snapshot without an explicit flag plus a warning naming the device it would strand; and add a `device:status` line reporting whether a snapshot is currently stranded — that alone would have surfaced this before anything was deleted.

#### `run-roku-tests.js` labels a never-ran run `failed`, so the device ledger repeats the conflation [#806](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/pull/806) fixed for RTA. `[fid: run-roku-tests-labels-never-ran-failed]` `[captured 2026-08-12]`

Its `catch` routes everything to `done(1)` -> `RUN_OUTCOMES.FAILED`, including a `deployToRoku()` throw — the same 401 shape that motivated `outcome` in the first place, and which the RTA entry point records as `crashed`. So one incident is a non-sample in `.device-runs/rta/` and a red sample in `.device-runs/device/`, while [`decisions.md`](decisions.md)'s `outcomes-partition-into-samples` presents the four values as one shared vocabulary and [`testing.md`](architecture/testing.md) documents them for both ledgers. `npm run flake-baseline -- --run run-roku-tests` therefore counts deploy failures as red runs. **Fix shape:** set a flag once `captureConsole()` starts, and have `done` pass `CRASHED` when the throw arrived before it. Raised in review of #806 and deliberately not folded in — the RTA side is what Phase 3 reads, and the device ledger has one line in it today.

#### Two Roku console readers now exist, and the one that matters is the newer one. `[fid: two-roku-console-readers]` `[captured 2026-08-12]`

[`scripts/run-roku-tests.js`](../scripts/run-roku-tests.js) has owned a line-buffered port-8085 tail with an idle timeout since long before this; `scripts/measure.js` added a second for the measurement series. They will drift, and the half worth sharing is the **replay-window handling**: a socket that connects and sits completely idle receives a stale `latest-rows run complete` line **10 ms later** reading `7241 ms`, against a live range of 1439–2654 ms that same session (measured on `.177`, 2026-08-12). `measure.js` defends against that twice — one socket held for the whole session, plus per-sample timestamp selection — while the Rooibos runner reconnects per invocation and has no equivalent (it reads `[Rooibos Result]` markers, so a replayed marker would report a stale verdict; whether that can actually happen is unverified and is the first thing to check). **Fix shape:** extract the tail into a shared reader and migrate `run-roku-tests.js` onto it. Deliberately not folded into the measurement-guard branch per `isolate-the-fix` — rewriting a working runner is not justified by the new consumer alone.

**A wrong verdict from another build has now happened.** PR #1296's `all-tests` failed on 2026-11-01 (run 41261833944) on `memoryMonitor.spec.bs`, reporting 5795 tests. That spec exists only on PR #1299's branch and was not among the files that run's build processed. Rerunning the job on the same commit passed 5813 of 5813. So the runner read a report from a different build; a replayed console is the likely source, not proven. This run's failure read as a real test failure until the file was looked for.

#### `npm run measure`'s ODC-precondition refusal has never run against real hardware. `[fid: measure-odc-refusal-untested-on-hardware]` `[captured 2026-08-12]`

The tool refuses before taking a sample when ODC does not answer (the device is not holding an RTA-deployed build) or when the app answers with no `serverUrl` (not signed in). Both paths are reasoned in the header and unit-tested, neither has been exercised on a device: all three local Roku devices happened to be carrying RTA builds when it was dogfooded, so the path could not be reached without deploying a plain `npm run build` and redeploying RTA afterwards. **Verification gap, not a design gap** — deliberately filed here rather than in `tech-debt.md` for that reason. Close it by pointing the tool at a device holding a non-RTA build, and at one that is RTA-deployed but signed out, and confirming both refuse with the documented messages.

#### Does a resident ODC component move a first-paint number? ANSWERED on all three tiers — and the answer is inverted. `[fid: resident-odc-first-paint-inverted]` `[captured 2026-08-13]`

`npm run measure` reads identity over ODC, and ODC exists only in an `ENABLE_RTA` build, so every number this tooling produces carried an unmeasured variable. **(1)(2)(3) DONE** — the sampling loop is extracted, [ADR 0030](adr/0030-non-odc-arm-identity-by-enclosure.md) settles identity-by-enclosure for the no-ODC arm, and [`npm run measure:calibrate`](../scripts/measure-calibration.js) is the harness (PR [#830](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/pull/830)). ⚠️ **ADR 0030's deploy mechanism was half wrong and is corrected there**: `injectTestingFiles: false` does NOT skip the staged-manifest `ENABLE_RTA` rewrite, so the plain arm flips the flag back to be the build the baselines were taken on. **THE RESULT — every figure below regenerated through `measure:compare`, none of it hand-arithmetic: 1 GB (+40.5 ms, p=0.67, n=30/arm) and 2 GB (+33.5 ms, p=0.053, n=30 rta vs n=25 plain — one block lost to the boot-race the harness now retries) show nothing above the ~120 ms floor. 512 MB shows the no-ODC build +130.5 ms (+4.0%) SLOWER, pooled n=60/arm across two independent runs, p=0.023; individually +153 and +101.5.** The `rta` baseline reproduced to 0.3% across three runs, which is what makes the pooling legitimate. ⚠️ **One figure here was wrong until review caught it, and the mechanism is worth more than the fix:** this entry read `+153` as `+136`, from an ad-hoc script that took `sorted(...)[len//2]` (the upper of two middle values, not a median) and filtered on `complete` rather than `indexInLaunch === 0` (so not cold-only either). It sat in a table beside a `+101.5` that HAD come from `measure:compare` — two different statistics in adjacent rows of one table, invisible because both looked plausible. Caught only by re-deriving from the ledger. **The rule that follows: every published figure comes out of `measure:compare`, including the pooled one — `--file` on a filtered copy of the ledger makes it do that too.** ⚠️ **Four caveats kept deliberately:** the pooling was decided AFTER seeing both runs (forking paths — "p=0.023, post-hoc"); the direction is opposite to the hypothesis; the decomposition arm (`no-component`: component gone, hooks kept) sits at +49.5 ms and so does NOT attribute the effect to the resident component; and no mechanism is established or named. **Practical consequence:** on the 512 MB tier a `measure` number reads ~4% faster than the same app measured without RTA, so the non-RTA baselines in [`home-first-paint-performance.md`](dev/home-first-paint-performance.md) still must not be compared against there — the `enableRta` refusal was protecting something real. Deltas between two RTA arms are unaffected. **What is left:** nothing required. A pre-registered n=60 run on 512 MB would settle the post-hoc caveat, and the mechanism is unexplained — both are optional follow-on curiosity, not blockers.

#### `checkout.agreesWithDevice` cannot detect stale CODE, only a stale FLAVOR — and it is null exactly when asked. `[fid: agrees-with-device-misses-stale-code]` `[captured 2026-08-14]`

It is derived from the `[debug=… perfTiming=…]` bracket carried BY A SAMPLE, so a zero-sample run records `null`, which is precisely the run that wants to know whether the device is holding this checkout. It also compares `bs_const` only, so a device running older code built with the same flags reads as agreeing. The stale-`build/` defect that motivated this is fixed (`--deploy` builds now), so the remaining exposure is the no-deploy mode — which is the default. **Fix shape:** have the app stamp an artifact identity into its own timing line (the commit, or a hash of the staged package) so the record compares artifacts rather than flags, and report it as unknown-with-a-reason rather than `null`.

#### An ADR's `related-files` claim is unenforced — nothing warns when you change a file an ADR says it governs. `[fid: adr-related-files-unenforced]` `[captured 2026-08-14]`

[`check-touched-related-files.cjs`](../scripts/lint/check-touched-related-files.cjs) scans `docs/architecture/` only (`ARCH_DIR`), and it reads `related-files` out of YAML frontmatter, while ADRs carry theirs as a body `**related-files**:` line — so ADRs are missed on both counts. **First instance already happened, not a predicted one:** the search-measurement branch changed `scripts/measure-selection.js`, `scripts/measure.js` and `tests/scripts/unit/measure-selection.test.js` — three of the six files [ADR 0028](adr/0028-mount-identity-component-and-variant.md) lists — and the end-of-turn hook reported only `system-shape.md`, matched via `docs/decisions.md`. The miss is quiet in exactly the way the architecture-doc reminder exists to prevent: the ADR that defines what `variant` means was never re-read by a branch that widened the meaning. **Fix shape:** teach the scanner the ADR line format and add `docs/adr/` to its scan set — soft reminder only, matching what it already does for architecture docs. NOT the CI gate ([`docs-stale-blocking.cjs`](../scripts/lint/docs-stale-blocking.cjs)), which keys on 120-day staleness and would fail PRs against day-old ADRs.

#### `measure:calibrate`'s remaining hardening — four small gaps, all found in review of the harness's own PR, none blocking. `[fid: measure-calibrate-remaining-hardening]` `[captured 2026-08-18]`

(a) **No early abort on a wrong `--server`.** The RTA blocks refuse in seconds, but the enclosed arm's tier 1 is `ok: true` by construction, so every plain block still takes its full n samples and only fails at the enclosure — one wrong URL costs the whole run (~30 min at n=30) to publish nothing. A "the first two blocks both produced no record → stop" check would pay for itself the first time. (b) **`--enclosed-server` without `--record-to` is not refused**, so a hand-typed `npm run measure -- --enclosed-server <url>` publishes an `identitySource: 'enclosed'` record straight to the ledger with no `enclosure` field, and `measure:compare`'s ADR 0030 exception would accept it (it checks `identitySource`, not `enclosure.ok`). Every other contradiction in that parser is a refusal; this one is a header comment saying "not for hand use". (c) **`bracketRead`'s retry policy lives in the delivery file, where no test can reach it.** Its give-up-by-RETURNING-the-empty-read contract is subtle and load-bearing — it is what lets a device with no server on it still fail the enclosure loudly — and it has no test, in a file whose own header says "everything here is a rule" belongs in `measure-arms.js`. (d) **`takeRecord` swallows JSON parse errors**, so a malformed record is reported as "wrote no record", which sends a reader to the child's exit code instead of to the file. Also worth a pass: the line-number citations into RTA's `RokuDevice.js` in `measure-arms.js`, `driver.js` and ADR 0030 point into `node_modules` and will drift on the next RTA bump — cite `createPackage` / `deploy` by name instead.

#### The cell-load ledger's counters reset when a screen REPLACES its content root `[fid: cell-load-ledger-resets-on-replace]` `[captured 2026-08-20]`

**The cell-load ledger's counters reset when a screen REPLACES its content root**, so a grid's line covers "since the last rebuild" rather than "since the screen opened". [`BaseGridView`](../components/ItemGrid/BaseGridView.bs) re-calls `initTextureManager(m.data)` at **five** call sites (refresh, filter change, …) and [`SearchRow`](../components/search/SearchRow.bs) at two; each fresh root starts a fresh set of counters and the previous root's counts are never published. Shipped that way deliberately in `4485d5fb` rather than fixed, because the fix needs an emit-before-replace at five call sites and the limit is **visible in the numbers** (a line whose `binds` is implausibly low against its `items`) rather than silent — it is documented in [`source/utils/cellLoad.bs`](../source/utils/cellLoad.bs)'s header. Worth closing once the cell workload is driven by a scripted RTA run: a scripted grid run that happens to include a filter change would otherwise under-report without saying so. **Fix shape:** call `cellLoad.emit(oldRoot)` immediately before assigning the new `m.data`.

#### The `defer-before` arm in the measurement ledger is a naming trap: its name promises a baseline and its numbers are the fixed build. `[fid: defer-before-arm-naming-trap]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

Left over from an abandoned pilot of the reload-guard defer experiment (2026-08-21) — one `blocked` series carrying no medians, and one `passed` series at n=5 reading `wipesReload` 6 / `loadsStarted` 43, which are the SHIPPED arm's numbers rather than the baseline's. Nothing published cites it (`grep defer-before docs/` is empty) and the three-arm campaign that settled #840 uses `preguard` / `guard` / `guarddefer`, so nothing is wrong today — the hazard is latent. `npm run measure:compare -- --a defer-before` hands a future reader post-fix numbers under a name promising the opposite, and the tool cannot catch it because an arm label is free text with no schema to violate. **Fix shape:** rename the passed series to `guard-pilot` rather than deleting the rows. The ledger is append-only by convention, and dropping a row to tidy a name is the class of provenance loss the record exists to prevent — the row is real data taken against a real build. Surfaced reviewing #840 for merge-readiness.

#### `/crash-report`'s occurrence threshold silently starves the architectural epics of evidence, because it fires before the disposition is knowable. `[fid: crash-report-threshold-starves-epics]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

[`applyThreshold()`](../scripts/crash-report.js) drops any signature below `min-devices=2` OR `min-dates=2`, and only surviving rows are offered for backtrace enrichment — but the exception code that routes a crash to `file` / `aggregate` / `watch` lives *only* in the backtrace. So a crash cannot be known to belong to an epic until after the gate that already excluded it. Observed 2026-08-21: the 08-15..08-20 report carried four `&hec` themed-init crashes (`JRLabel:5`, `JRPlaceholder:11`, `ScrollingLabelSmaller:2`, `VideoProgressBar:5`), each 1 date / 1 device, so all four were skipped and **epic [#103](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/issues/103) received nothing** — even though a manually-pulled backtrace confirmed `JRPlaceholder:11` as `&hec` + `init()` + `m.global.constants`, a textbook member of that class. The threshold's real purpose is to stop noisy *filing* (a new `[crash]` issue per singleton); an epic *record* comment carries no such risk, since records are upserted per `file·function·line·version` and accumulate. Net effect: the epics under-count exactly the long-tail classes they exist to aggregate, and a low-frequency-but-persistent architectural crash looks quieter than it is. **Fix shape (needs a design call, not a one-liner):** either exempt signatures whose `pkgPath`+`function` already appear on an epic's existing records from the threshold, or split the gate into `file-threshold` vs `aggregate-threshold` with the latter at 1/1. Interim workaround is `--min-dates 1` when the intent is epic evidence, which is a manual step nobody will remember. Surfaced running `/crash-report` while closing epics #728/#729.

#### `measure:compare` can't compare any A/B whose independent variable isn't code, so the repo's own comparison tool is unusable for a whole class of real experiments. `[fid: measure-compare-non-code-variable]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

It refuses two arms sharing a commit — a correct guard against the common mistake (an uncommitted change leaves both arms on the same SHA) — but that also rules out every experiment where the thing varied is *not* the checkout: server configuration, device state, network conditions, user settings. Hit directly 2026-08-21: the Home row-count campaign varied `LatestItemsExcludes` on the Jellyfin server with the build held **constant** across all 12 alternated blocks, which is exactly the design the guard forbids. The comparison had to be hand-rolled — reading `.device-runs/measure/measurements.jsonl` directly and implementing a Mann-Whitney in a throwaway script — so that campaign has no `measure:compare` provenance, no `seriesConsistency` check, and no reusable record of how it was decided, even though it produced the cost model the whole project now rests on. **Why it matters beyond one campaign:** arms that vary server configuration or device state are the natural shape for scaling questions (row count, library composition, connection speed), which are precisely what the measurement subsystem exists to answer. **Fix shape:** let an arm declare a non-code independent variable — `--variable=server-config`, or an `--allow-same-commit` that *records what differed* rather than refusing — so the guard keeps catching the accidental case while the deliberate one stays inside the tool. Surfaced running the Home row-count and `Limit` sweep campaigns.

#### Nothing asserts that a BSC plugin is actually REGISTERED, so deleting one line from a `bsconfig` plugins array silently disables it. `[fid: bsc-plugin-registration-unasserted]` `[captured 2026-08-23]`

[`scripts/bsc-plugins/`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/) holds seven convention plugins, three of them at **Error** severity ([`no-raw-run`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/no-raw-run.cjs), [`no-task-fanout`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/no-task-fanout.cjs), [`callfunc-interface`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/callfunc-interface.cjs)). Each is enforced only by its entry in [`bsconfig.json`](../bsconfig.json) / [`bsconfig-prod.json`](../bsconfig-prod.json), and nothing — no test, no lint step, no CI job — checks that the entry is still there. Removing it leaves the plugin file, its whole test suite, and every doc reference intact and passing, while the rule stops being enforced: the failure is total, silent, and green. Surfaced reviewing #846, where the same *class* of risk in `no-task-fanout` itself (a bare-only match on `launchTask`'s `callee`, which moving `tasks.bs` into a namespace would have disabled wholesale) was closed in that PR — this is the wider surface it pointed at. **Fix shape:** a Vitest case enumerating `scripts/bsc-plugins/*.cjs` against both configs, with an explicit allowlist for the deliberate asymmetries (`callfunc-interface` is registered in `bsconfig.json` only; `roku-log` / `translation-keys` are wired per build), so an intentional exclusion stays possible but an accidental one fails.

#### Nothing in the repo catches a journal LOSING content — the doc gates validate what is present, never what is absent. `[fid: journal-losing-content-undetected]` `[captured 2026-08-23]`

Proved the hard way 2026-08-23: a scripted edit truncated [`docs/progress.md`](progress.md) from **182 lines to 105** — five whole `###` sections and roughly sixty open followups — and `lint:docs`, `markdownlint` and `lint:spelling` **all passed**, because each validates frontmatter, schema, anchors, formatting and the prose that survived. It surfaced only by accident, when `main` moved and the merge forced a per-side diff; on a clean fast-forward it would have shipped silently and `/catchup` would have read the truncated file as authoritative. A spot-check of the lines around the insertion point does NOT substitute — the insertion was correct and the tail was gone. **Fix shape (two candidates, needs a call):** teach [`docs-check.cjs`](../scripts/lint/docs-check.cjs) a structural postcondition — the `###` heading set of `progress.md` / `signals-backlog.md` may gain sections but never lose one — or a cheaper pre-commit guard flagging any large line-count DROP in a journal for confirmation. The first is precise and journal-aware; the second is generic and catches classes the first does not. **Scoped OUT of `task-thread-budget` 2026-08-23** — the project surfaced it, but it is repo tooling work with no dependency on that project's phases.

#### `measure.js` throws away its console capture on a HEALTHY run, so any probe output a family pattern does not capture is unrecoverable. `[fid: measure-discards-healthy-console-capture]` `[captured 2026-08-25]`

`console-window.log` is written only inside the "nothing matched" branch of [`scripts/measure.js`](../scripts/measure.js) (beside the `the console carried N line(s) and none matched` error), and raw device lines never reach stdout either — nothing `console.log`s them, so redirecting the run to a file does not capture them. **Friction event, 2026-08-24, not speculation:** a new `#if perfTiming` probe emitted a section id on its log line, documented in the code as readable from the run log; a clean 20/20 arm kept the numeric counters the pattern captured and lost every section id behind them, costing a second 11-minute arm to recover. The general shape is worse than that one loss — it means an instrument is only as durable as the regex someone remembered to widen, and the failure is silent. **Fix shape:** write `console-window.log` unconditionally (the lines are already assembled in memory, `.device-runs/` is gitignored, and the run directory already exists), or gate it behind a flag that defaults on. That file's own header already argues it is "the only evidence of why" — which is just as true of a run that succeeded and surprised you as of one that emitted nothing.

#### The at-gate cell-load counters reach stdout but never the ledger, so every campaign that needs them hand-rolls the same join — three sessions running. `[fid: at-gate-counters-never-reach-ledger]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

Distinct from the entry above, and worth keeping apart from it: those are DEVICE lines that never reach stdout at all, while these are the harness's own `console.log` from [`reportSweep`](../tests/rta/lib/nav.js) — a redirect DOES capture them. What is missing is that nothing carries them into `.device-runs/measure/measurements.jsonl`. `readCellCounts` reads `binds`/`loadsStarted`/`unloads`/`appearances`/`popIns` over ODC at the sweep gate and `reportSweep` prints them into the `[nav] <name>:` line as `…, of which before the sweep (binds=… loadsStarted=…)`. **Why it matters:** the at-gate read is what CLASSIFIES a launch — Home's high/low bind mode is defined by it — while the ledger carries the mechanism fields, so any "which launches did the extra work, and why" question means joining a redirected console log to the ledger BY LAUNCH ORDER in a throwaway script, and a silent misalignment there looks exactly like a finding. Hit again 2026-08-25 taking the Home row-removal phase arms; that analyzer ran ~120 lines and had to assert the join itself (equal lengths, contiguous launch indices, every nav line carrying an at-gate group) because nothing else would catch a slip. **Fix shape:** have the nav emit the at-gate counters as a declared measurement line — the shape `latest-rows row removed` uses — so they land per-sample and no join exists; or have `measure.js` retain the matched nav lines beside the samples it already writes.

#### `docs-check.cjs` validates `related-files` in architecture-doc frontmatter but skips the `**related-files**:` line on `docs/decisions.md` notes, so a citation to a file that does not exist passes lint clean. `[fid: docs-check-skips-decisions-related-files]` `[captured 2026-08-27]`

Hit 2026-08-27 reviewing `fix/resolution-cap-ceiling`: the `resolution-cap-honors-user-choice` note cited `source/utils/transcodeCause.bs`, which exists nowhere in the repo, and `npm run lint:docs` still reported `101 file(s) clean`. The path escapes two passes at once — [`docs-check.cjs`](../scripts/lint/docs-check.cjs) lists `**related-files**` among the fields it explicitly does not validate (in `checkDecisionsSupersedeChain()`), and because the path sits in backticks rather than a markdown link, the link-resolution pass (`checkBodyLinks()`) never sees it either. **Why it matters:** `docs/decisions.md` is a surface `/catchup` and `/focus` read as authoritative, and a note's related-files is exactly what a later reader greps to find the code a decision governs; a rotted path sends them nowhere and nothing flags it. **Fix shape:** point the existing frontmatter related-files resolver at the bold-inline field on decisions.md notes too, reusing its path resolution rather than adding a second checker. **Adjacent, different script:** the `check-touched-related-files.cjs` followup above records the same bold-inline `**related-files**:` format being a blind spot there as well — same format, two scripts, worth one pass.

#### `device:check` reports "ODC answering" from a bare TCP connect, which can be true while ODC serves nothing. `[fid: device-check-odc-bare-tcp-connect]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

Both devices were observed on 2026-09-05 accepting a connection on port 9000 while every ODC *request* hung forever — the client's per-request `timeout` does not rescue that, because it wraps the request and not the handshake (`rta-odc-connect-hang`). Either issue a real request in the probe, or say the line only proves the port is open. A fresh deploy is the known fix, so `npm run test:rta` recovers it; restarting the channel by hand does NOT — on `.177` that loaded the last package deployed to it, which was a non-RTA build, and ODC then refused outright (a fast, clean error rather than the hang).

#### The `m.top` half of the observer-accumulation class is now gated; a control-flow-aware gate for CHILD-node toggles is still deferred. `[fid: observer-accumulation-child-toggle-gate]` `[captured 2026-09-13]`

ADR 0034's `top-observer-outside-init` / `top-unobserve-outside-ondestroy` close #896 (`position`) and #898 (`state`) by construction. What stays convention-only is the balanced toggle on a node the component owns — #899 (`bufferCheckTimer.fire`) and `playbackTimer.fire` — tracked with its sizing (143 naive sites, 16 narrowed, 3 of 3 spot-checked false positives) and direction in [`observer-balanced-toggle-ungated`](architecture/tech-debt.md#observer-balanced-toggle-ungated). Revisit if a third child-node instance lands.

#### `scripts/rta-run.js` deploys `build/` without rebuilding, so calling it directly right after `npm run test:tdd` sideloads the Rooibos test runner and waits forever for an ODC that build never starts. `[fid: rta-run-deploys-without-rebuild]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

Only the `npm run test:rta` script guards this, because it runs `npm run build` first; the direct invocation (`node scripts/rta-run.js <spec>`, the natural way to re-run one failing spec) does not. It presents as `Failed to connect to Roku ... on port 9000` and folds as `crashed`, not `failed` — so it is correctly excluded from the flake population, but the operator is left with no verdict and no obvious cause. **Cost three wasted runs and two wrong conclusions in one session** (2026-09-04, #750): a genuinely flaky RTA failure was being re-measured, and every re-measurement silently tested the test runner instead of the app. **Fix shape:** have `rta-run.js` assert `build/` is the app build before deploying — the manifest or the presence of the Rooibos runner entry point both distinguish them — and fail with the actual reason. Documenting it in [`docs/dev/rta-tests.md`](../docs/dev/rta-tests.md) is the cheaper half, but the guard is what stops the next person losing the time.

#### `measure` can lose the settle time of a slow `screen-load` launch, and the loss biases a comparison toward the slower build. `[fid: measure-loses-slow-screen-load-settle]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

Only the `paint` line is required for that family, so a paint-only sample already counts as complete and the 6 s quiet window (`QUIET_MS`) can close before `settled` arrives. Seen on 2 of ~560 Home launches on 2026-09-16, both in the slower width-3 arm; the run flags it only as `line coverage: settled 4/5`, and the per-launch "no complete sample" warning does not fire. Probable mechanism, unverified — no console capture exists for either launch, because the dump is written only on a zero-sample run. **Fix shape:** keep the quiet window open until the family's optional lines have had their chance (or until `settled` for families that declare one), and count a missing settle in the incomplete tally.

#### `rta:restore` cannot converge while a device's saved active user is on a server whose Home layout keeps changing. `[fid: rta-restore-cannot-converge-home-layout]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

The app re-derives that user's `homeSection0..6` from the server on every cold boot, and the public demo server resets hourly, so the snapshot's values go stale and every attempt fails on those keys; the 512 MB test device needed `--accept` three times on 2026-09-16. **Fix shape:** treat the server-derived `homeSection*` cache like the re-minted-credential class in `tests/rta/lib/registry.js` (presence counts, value does not).

#### There is no supported way to hold a device lock across a multi-step campaign. `[fid: no-lock-across-multi-step-campaign]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

`measure`, `measure-signin` and `rta:restore` each take and release the lock, so another session can deploy between two blocks of one comparison. Holding it from a wrapper means running the tools inside with `RTA_SKIP_LOCK=1`, which records `deviceKey: null` in `measurements.jsonl` and in the run ledger (seen on the 2026-09-16 pool-width confirmation runs). **Fix shape:** let a lock holder pass its device key and an "already held by my parent" mode to child tools.

#### `measure --component <name>` waits the full watch window on every launch when the named mount never completes — a typo in the component name costs ~n × 45 s before the run says so. `[fid: measure-component-typo-waits-full-window]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

Since #944 a launch ends early only on the NAMED mount's sample ([`scripts/measure-loop.js`](../scripts/measure-loop.js)), which is right for a slow screen (the old any-sample break cut Home off behind `preLogin`), but a typo now waits out `MAX_WINDOW_MS` + boot per launch instead of ~`QUIET_MS`, and only then reports `withoutNamedMount`. `--component` is only format-checked (`checkLabel` in [`scripts/measure-args.js`](../scripts/measure-args.js)). The per-launch line already names the mounts it did see, so an operator can abort after launch 1. Not hit in practice yet. **Fix shape:** validate `--component` up front against the `screenLoad.begin("…")` literals in `components/` + `source/` (7 today), scanned at startup rather than kept as a list, and fail naming the known components.

#### brighterscript `1.0.0-alpha.54` (the version `package-lock.json` pins) warns `deprecated-bsconfig-option` on every build: `[fid: brighterscript-deprecated-bsconfig-option]` `[captured 2026-09-17]`

`autoImportComponentScript` and `sourceMap` belong under `compilerOptions`. The top-level keys are in all eight tracked `bsconfig*.json` files (and the local `bsconfig-tdd.json`). Move them, then confirm `npx bsc --noEmit` and each `build:*` script print no warnings and the test runners still find source maps. A checkout whose `node_modules` still has `alpha.52` shows no warning, which is how this went unnoticed; run `npm ci` first. Found 2026-09-17 while verifying #955.

#### The four Task / dialog BSC plugins each hand-roll the same `bsc-disable-line` / `-next-line` regex pair, and the reason is a real constraint, not an oversight. `[fid: bsc-plugins-duplicate-disable-line-regex]` `[captured 2026-09-18]`

[`scripts/lib/bsc-rule.cjs`](../scripts/lib/bsc-rule.cjs)'s `isSuppressed` always honors `bsc-disable-file`, which an error-severity gate must NOT — a whole-file opt-out would remove the thread-budget bound from every launch in the file — so `no-raw-run`, `no-task-fanout`, `no-same-node-relaunch` and `no-hand-rolled-dialog` all opt out of the shared lifecycle and copy the markers instead. **Fix shape:** either an `isSuppressed(file, code, line, { allowFileLevel: false })` option, or a `createFileRule` factory for the single-file rule class those four belong to (every finding derived from and anchored in one file, which BSC already clears per re-validate — so there is no staleness for the existing factories to prevent). Either would let `scripts/CLAUDE.md`, [`build-and-tooling.md`](architecture/build-and-tooling.md) and [`scripts-development.md`](dev/scripts-development.md) drop the carve-out added 2026-09-17, which currently documents the exception in three places instead of encoding it once. **Evidence:** four copies today, and the same review needed three doc edits to explain why the directive does not apply — then a second round to add the program-hook clause to each. Kept out of that branch because it touches three green, well-tested plugins and the branch's job was landing the relaunch gate.

**Added 2026-11-13:** `no-namespace-const-call`, an error gate that needs the program scope (to tell a const from a variable), so it cannot take the single-file exception and is built on `createProgramRule`, which honors `bsc-disable-file`. The `allowFileLevel: false` option above would close that for it too; a `createFileRule` factory would not, since it is not a single-file rule. Until then its row in `build-and-tooling.md`'s marker table records the whole-file marker as honored.

#### `no-same-node-relaunch` follows same-file helpers by BARE name only, so class-based code is a blind spot — measured at zero cost today, which is why it was left. `[fid: no-same-node-relaunch-class-blind-spot]` `[captured 2026-09-18]`

A class reaches its own methods as `m.loadLogo()` and never bare (see [`MoviePresenter`](../source/GridView/MoviePresenter.bs), the one class in the app that calls `launchTask`), so a STOP inside a method-helper never crosses the hop. Measured 2026-09-17 before leaving it: a variant that also followed dotted `m.<name>()` calls found **the same 16 sites and no others**, so there is no present site to justify it — only a future one. **Fix shape:** closing it properly means resolving BOTH directions by scope — a bare call to free functions, `m.x()` to methods of the *enclosing class* — because the naive version lets a bare global call inherit a same-named method's STOPs, which is the same defect mirrored. Re-measure before building it; if class-based presenters grow and the count moves off zero, that is the trigger. Related: the rule also does not cross the hop in the other direction (a function that STOPs and then calls a helper which launches is missed) — both gaps are listed in the plugin header.

#### `scripts/measure.js` drives screens from the `SCREENS` registry but ignores their `requires` gates, so a measurement can silently profile the wrong thing. `[fid: measure-ignores-screen-requires-gates]` `[captured 2026-09-20]`

The registry's `requires` gates are honored by the functional suite and the screenshot orchestrator (both via `firstUnmetRequirement()`), not by `measure.js` — which documents `--nav osd` as an example, and `osd` now carries a `HERO_PRESENT` gate. Against a server lacking the configured film, `findMovie` answers a miss with tile 0, so the measurement runs against whatever sorts first and records the number under the nav name as though nothing were wrong. Pre-existing rather than new (`subtitlePanel` has had a gate all along and `measure.js` ignored that too), and adding the field regressed nothing — but `osd` is a *documented* measurement nav, which widens the surface. It matters because a ledger figure's whole value is that two records under one nav name are comparable. **Fix shape:** call the same `firstUnmetRequirement()` and refuse the run with the gate's reason, rather than teaching `measure.js` a second notion of readiness.

#### `observe-without-on-destroy` does not count `releaseTask(x, "field")` / `replaceTask(x, …, "field", …)` as releasing `x`'s observer `[fid: observe-without-on-destroy-release-task]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

**`observe-without-on-destroy` does not count `releaseTask(x, "field")` / `replaceTask(x, …, "field", …)` as releasing `x`'s observer**, so every migrated site (`FavoritesRows`, `BaseGridView`, `schedule`) carries an explicit `unobserveField` next to the helper that already does it. Teach the plugin the two helpers — `no-same-node-relaunch` already recognizes them as stopping their first argument. (`SearchResults`, once on this list, was retired; `SearchScreen` releases its task through `releaseTask` alone and the plugin does not flag it, so check why before generalizing from it.)

#### Nothing fails when a bare-array endpoint's builder is written with `validatedReq` instead of `listReq`. `[fid: bare-array-endpoint-validatedreq]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

[ADR 0039](adr/0039-list-endpoint-shape-in-pool.md) has the pool hold a list request's body to its shape, but only for builders that declare it; the "List requests" group in `ApiClient.spec.bs` pins the builders that exist and cannot see a new one. The first cut of that ADR's branch missed two of seven such readers, found only by joining the Jellyfin OpenAPI spec's array-typed 200 responses against the app's endpoints by hand (2026-09-22). **Fix shape:** have [`api-usage-manifest.js`](../scripts/generate/api-usage-manifest.js) record which builder helper each endpoint uses (it already reads `validatedReq`/`listReq` for the method), and a check join that against the spec's `type: array` 200 responses — reusing `scripts/lib/spec-fetch.cjs` rather than a second spec loader. Cost to weigh: CI needs the spec (a network fetch or the `api-watch` cache).

#### `callfunc-interface` could never have caught the `captionTask` teardown bug, and still can't: it checks `callFunc` targets program-wide `[fid: callfunc-interface-misses-caption-teardown]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

[`scripts/bsc-plugins/callfunc-interface.cjs`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/callfunc-interface.cjs) exists for exactly the "wrote the method, forgot the interface line" defect, but its rule is PROGRAM-WIDE: if any component declares `<function name="X" />`, no `callFunc("X")` is flagged. 11 components declare `onDestroy`, so `m.captionTask.callFunc("onDestroy")` against an interface-less `captionTask.xml` passed clean while the 100 ms caption timer survived every player exit. `onDestroy` is the most-declared name in the repo, which makes it the plugin's worst blind spot on the one call class whose failure is silent by construction. **Two halves, and neither works alone.** (1) *Receiver-awareness* — when the receiver's subtype is statically known, check THAT component's resolved interface rather than the program-wide set. Tractable: all 11 current production `callFunc("onDestroy")` receivers resolve, from either `CreateObject("roSGNode", "<literal>")` or `m.top.findNode("<id>")` against the same component's `<children>`, and [`field-observer-wiring`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/field-observer-wiring.cjs) already does union-find aliasing in-repo. It MUST walk the `extends` chain and accept an inherited declaration — measured 2026-09-23 on a Roku Ultra (OS 15.3.4) that `OSD.callFunc("onDestroy")` reaches the override on `OSD` itself through `JRScreen`'s declaration, pinned by [`callfunc-interface-inheritance.spec.bs`](../tests/source/unit/components/callfunc-interface-inheritance.spec.bs). (2) *Run it over the test bsconfigs* — `bsconfig.json` excludes `tests/**` and the `bsconfig-tests*.json` files load only rooibos, bslint and translation-keys, so no JellyRock guard reaches a spec; the old `captionTask.spec.bs` called `callFunc("updateCaption")` where `updateCaption` was declared by zero components repo-wide, which (2) would have caught. Doing (2) alone still passes on `onDestroy`; doing (1) alone leaves specs unguarded.

**Half (2) done 2026-11-13:** `callfunc-interface` now loads in every test config through `bsconfig-tests-base.json`, and `npm run validate` checks `bsconfig-tests.json`, so a spec's undeclared `callFunc` fails pre-push and CI. It found two on its first run: `ItemDetails.spec.bs` (a suite whose tests could not fail, replaced by `focusRowButton()` tests in `buttonOverflow.spec.bs`) and `OSDRepeatSteps.spec.bs` (now calls the declared `refreshFromQueue`). Half (1), receiver-awareness, is still open, so `callFunc("onDestroy")` on an interface-less component still passes.

#### `npm run measure` finds no Home first-paint sample when a launch drops two Home rows (found with #1043). `[fid: measure-home-sample-missing-dropped-rows]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

`HomeRows` logs `latest-rows row removed` once per row it drops mid-load, and `assembleSamples` (`scripts/measurements.js`) closes a sample whenever a line key repeats, so a second `row removed` splits the launch: sample #0 holds that one line and is incomplete, and the real paint lands as #1, which `selectColdSamples` (`scripts/measure-selection.js`) never picks. Seen 2026-09-25 on the 512 MB Stick against the demo server (Jellyfin 12.1.0): 5 of 5 launches painted 4 rows, 0 of 5 cold samples, run recorded `blocked`. Every Home first-paint A/B against the demo server fails until this is fixed. Fix shape: a repeated `rowRemoved` belongs to the same run rather than opening a new one, with a unit test built from that launch's line order.

#### `scripts/catchup-state.js` deletes old handoffs during a read `[fid: catchup-reader-writes-tracked-files]` `[captured 2026-10-03]`

A state reader should only read. Every run deletes handoffs in `.claude/handoffs/` older than 30 days. They are gitignored, so the tree stays clean, but a briefing still destroys local state as a side effect. Move the pruning to a command of its own; the `/catchup` `## This repo` note then drops its write exception. (The signals half shipped in #1221: the reader no longer writes `docs/signals-backlog.md`.)

#### Check whether the seeded vi and fa `LabelProfile` values mean a codec profile or a personal profile `[fid: labelprofile-vi-fa-meaning-check]` `[captured 2026-10-04]`

The seeded `LabelProfile` values for `vi` (`Hồ sơ`) and `fa` (`نمایه`) came from the `jellyfin-web` key `MediaInfoProfile` (a codec profile, e.g. H.264 High). Both words can also mean a personal profile, which is how that key reads in its `ja`, `ko`, `ar` and `ta` translations; those four were excluded in `locale/seed/keymap.yml` with reasons, but nobody on #931 could judge `vi` or `fa`. Close by having a Vietnamese and a Persian speaker check the two values. If either reads as a personal profile, delete it from `locale/custom/vi.json` / `fa.json` (or have a translator delete it in Weblate): the seed ledger (`locale/seed/seeded.json`) keeps the seeder from refilling it, and the next release's three-way merge carries the deletion to the other side. Also add it to that entry's `exclude:` with the reason, so the refusal is recorded next to the mapping.

#### Nothing builds with `debug=true` automatically, so a break in `#if debug` code goes unseen `[fid: debug-build-never-built-automatically]` `[captured 2026-10-09]`

Found 2026-10-09: every `debug=true` build had failed since the dead-code rule landed (#1073, 2026-09-29), because `printTaskThreads()` had no keep kind. Nobody noticed for ten days because no hook or CI workflow builds with `debug=true`; `.github/workflows/` never sets it. The fix (decision `dead-code-debug-console-keep`) unblocked the build but not the blind spot. Any later rule or change that breaks only `#if debug` code fails the same way, as does a `debug-console` marker that drifts inside a `#if`.

**Recommended:** a CI step (or pre-push step) that runs the BrighterScript validate with `debug` on, and makes no package. A manifest edit inside the build directory, or a `bsconfig` `manifest.bs_const` override, could do it; `bsc` honors that override for its own validation, though it never reaches the artifact (see `harden-prod-manifest.js`). Cost: one more full validate per run, which took about 10 s locally on 2026-10-09.

**Alternative the operator raised:** turn `debug` on by default in the committed manifest, with `harden-prod-manifest.js` (which already forces `debug`, `perfTiming` and `ENABLE_RTA` off) keeping releases clean. Every dev build, hook and CI run would then build the debug code. The conflict to settle first: `build-and-tooling.md` keeps `perfTiming` separate from `debug` because a debug build attaches `rawApiData` inside the `emit` timing, and a debug build painted Home 178 ms slower on a 512 MB Stick (measured 2026-08-04). So debug-by-default would make every dev build's perf numbers unrepresentative. Also not checked: whether `debug` raising the log level from 2 to 4 makes the everyday console too noisy.

#### `measure:devices` same-model refusal gives a false reason `[fid: measure-matrix-same-model-false-reason]` `[captured 2026-10-10]`

`sameDeviceRefusal()` in `scripts/measure-matrix.js` (its printed message and its doc comment, and the comment above the `'refuses two distinct devices of the same model…'` test in `tests/scripts/unit/measure-matrix.test.js`) says `measurements.jsonl` "identifies a device by model / model number / RAM tier and by nothing else". That has been false since 2026-08-11: every series carries `deviceKey` (run ledger since `8794b2873`; a `measure:compare` selector since #810), and `measure-report.js` lists `device` in `PROVENANCE_AXES`. The text came with #802 (2026-08-10), when it was true, and was not updated. The refusal itself may still be right: two same-model devices would land in one `measure:report` column and pool with only a disclosure.

Options weighed: `reword` (keep the refusal; state the true reason in the message, doc comment and test comment, name both escapes, `--arm` per device or `--select device=<key>` afterwards; then delete the warning box in `docs/dev/measuring-performance.md`, section "More than one device"), or `allow` (drop the same-model refusal and let `measure:report` disclose the pooling; a behavior change needing a design call, possibly re-keying columns on `device`). Recommended `reword`; read `measure-report.js` first to confirm the column claim. Ships as its own PR from `main`.

Not checked: whether `measure:report` really keys its columns by model and tier (taken from the old guide's wording, not read in `measure-report.js`); whether pooling two same-model devices in one cell is a problem worth refusing, or only a disclosure. Nobody has run the refusal on real hardware: you have one device of each model.

#### `language-coverage.cjs` check 2 and `translations.md` say a missing alias shows English; it usually shows the raw code `[fid: language-coverage-tier2-fallback-wording]` `[captured 2026-10-11]`

`scripts/lint/language-coverage.cjs` check 2's error message says a tier 1 language missing an alias for one of its 3-letter codes makes the app "use tier 2's English string in all UI locales", and `docs/architecture/translations.md`'s check table (row 2) says the same. `resolveLanguageName()` in `source/utils/mediaDisplayTitle.bs` misses tier 1 and then shows tier 2's English name only if tier 2 holds that 3-letter code; otherwise it shows the raw code. A check of the tables during the phase G comment pass (2026-10-11) found most aliased codes have no tier 2 entry (`slv`, for example), so the usual result is the raw code. The script's header comment is already corrected. Closes when the message and the doc row say "the English name if tier 2 has the code, else the raw code", checked against the tables.

#### `rta-wait-inventory.js` keeps its own copy of `_shared.js`'s `calleeName` `[fid: rta-wait-inventory-duplicate-calleename]` `[captured 2026-10-11]`

`scripts/lint/rta-wait-inventory.js` defines its own `calleeName()`, marked as mirroring `scripts/lint/eslint-rules/_shared.js`'s exported `calleeName` "for one local use". Two copies of the same AST helper drift; `_shared.js` exists to stop exactly that. Closes when `rta-wait-inventory.js` imports it from `./eslint-rules/_shared.js` and `npm run test:scripts` passes. Found in the phase G comment pass (2026-10-11).

#### `no-raw-run` and `no-direct-sdk` messages name only `bsc-disable-line`, not the next-line form they also honor `[fid: no-raw-run-message-omits-next-line-marker]` `[captured 2026-10-11]`

The `no-raw-run` and `no-direct-sdk` BSC plugins honor both `bsc-disable-line` and `bsc-disable-next-line` (their `DISABLE_NEXT_LINE_MARKER` checks the previous line), but each diagnostic message tells the developer only about `' bsc-disable-line <rule>`. Their header comments were corrected in the phase G comment pass (2026-10-11); the message strings were out of that pass's scope. Closes when both messages name both forms and the plugin tests that assert the text pass.

#### `session-start-nudge` can nudge with "0 commit(s) since" when only `progress.md` commits landed `[fid: session-start-nudge-zero-commits-message]` `[captured 2026-10-11]`

`checkProgressStale()` in `scripts/lint/session-start-nudge.cjs` decides to nudge when any commit landed since `progress.md`'s `last-updated`, but reports the count of commits other than `progress.md` ones. When every commit since is a `progress.md` commit, the nudge fires and says "0 commit(s) since". Read from the code during the phase G comment pass (2026-10-11), not seen in practice. Closes when the guard uses the same count it reports, with a test for the all-journal case.

The same function also counts differently from the other staleness checks (found in the phase G batch 4 pass, 2026-10-11, read from the code): it counts every commit since `last-updated` except `progress.md` ones, while `checkStale()` in `scripts/lint/progress-cursor-nudge.cjs` (which the pre-push and Stop-hook nudges and the weekly `docs-stale-tracker.yml` run) counts only non-maintenance commits. So the session-start nudge can call `progress.md` stale when the others call it fresh. Closing both means having `checkProgressStale()` use `checkStale()`'s count.

#### `acquireDeviceLock` throws on a network error during its lock read instead of degrading, which can fail a CI device run. `[fid: acquire-lock-unguarded-read-throws]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

[`scripts/device-lock.js`](../scripts/device-lock.js)'s `acquireDeviceLock` documents that it "throws only when exclusivity was required and could not be had", and every other GitHub failure returns `degraded(...)`. Two calls break that: after a 422 conflict, and on the read-only path (`cannotWrite`, which in CI is every run, under `contents: read`), it calls `readDeviceLock` outside any `try`. `readDeviceLock` goes through `api()`, which rejects on a network error or timeout, so a GitHub blip at that moment throws out of `acquireDeviceLock` instead of degrading: in CI that fails a device run the lock was never meant to block (CI does not set `RTA_REQUIRE_LOCK`). After a 422 the throw is mostly harmless (the run would be refused anyway), but its message names a network error instead of the holder. Found reading the code in phase G batch 3 (2026-10-11); not reproduced. **Close it by** wrapping both reads: on the read-only path a failed read returns `degraded(...)`, and after a 422 it keeps the CAS verdict with the generic contender, which the code already falls back to for an empty read. Needs a unit test through the `setTransport` seam, so it was left out of the comment pass.

#### The `roku-deploy` smoke step checks functions the repo never calls, and skips `zipPackage`, `publish` and `getDevId` `[fid: smoke-test-checks-unused-roku-deploy-api]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

The `roku-deploy` step in `.github/workflows/_smoke-test-deps.yml` checks that `deploy`, `createPackage` and `deployAndSignPackage` are exported functions. The repo calls `zipPackage` (`scripts/create-package.cjs`, `scripts/run-roku-tests.js`), `publish` (`scripts/run-roku-tests.js`), `getDevId` and `deployAndSignPackage` (`scripts/create-signed-package.cjs`), and never `deploy` or `createPackage`. So a dependency bump that breaks `zipPackage` passes the smoke step that exists to catch it. Found in the phase G batch 4 comment pass (2026-10-11) by grepping the call sites; not seen fail. Closes when the step checks the functions the repo calls; a test that derives the list from the call sites would keep it from drifting.

#### `docs-stale-tracker.yml`'s close comment promises the issue will reopen; the job opens a new one `[fid: docs-stale-tracker-reopen-promise]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

`.github/workflows/docs-stale-tracker.yml` closes its tracker issue with a comment saying it "will reopen if it goes stale again", but its "Find existing tracker issue" step lists only open issues, so a later stale week opens a new issue instead. The message is a string, which phase G of the docs-voice pass leaves alone (found 2026-10-11, read from the workflow, not seen on a run). Closes when the comment says what happens, or when the job reopens the closed issue.

#### The `roku-log` plugin injects `m.__le = m.log.enabled` even when `guard` is off `[fid: roku-log-guard-line-ignores-config]` `[captured 2026-10-13]` `[prompt: /snag The roku-log plugin injects m.__le = m.log.enabled after every logger creation even when guard is off]`

In `scripts/bsc-plugins/roku-log.cjs`, the visitor that finds `m.log = new log.Logger(...)` (and the factory form) adds `createGuardSetStatement()` without testing `config.guard`; only the call-site wrapping checks it. No bsconfig sets `guard`, so every logger creation gains a line nothing reads. Confirmed in the code 2026-10-13. A `/snag` candidate: gate the injection on `config.guard` and add a plugin test.

#### `server-upgrade.js executePlan` rewrites the body of a digest that is closed or already being triaged `[fid: server-upgrade-rewrites-closed-digest]` `[captured 2026-10-13]` `[prompt: /snag server-upgrade.js executePlan rewrites a closed or already-triaging digest body, erasing its earlier sub-issue lines]`

`executePlan` runs `gh issue edit <digest> --body … --add-label server-upgrade:triaging` with no check of the digest's state or labels, so a rerun against a closed digest (such as #897) erases its filed sub-issue lines. The skill's Step 0.3 warns; the script has no guard. Read in the code 2026-10-13. A `/snag` candidate.

#### `journal-sync.yml` never installs dependencies, so its spell-check always skips `[fid: journal-sync-spellcheck-always-skips]` `[captured 2026-10-13]` `[prompt: /snag journal-sync.yml has no npm ci, so the ship spell-check in scripts/journal-sync.js always skips in production]`

`.github/workflows/journal-sync.yml` checks out and sets up Node but never runs `npm ci`. `defaultSpellRunner` in `scripts/journal-sync.js` returns "skipped: spellchecker binary not found" when `node_modules/.bin/spellchecker` is missing. Only `journal-sync-precheck.yml` (which does run `npm ci`) guards `progress.md` titles. Read in the workflow and script 2026-10-13, not seen in a run log. A `/snag` candidate.

#### `server-upgrade.js` links a reused or reopened issue without `replace_parent` `[fid: subissue-attach-without-replace-parent]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

`attachSubIssue` POSTs only `sub_issue_id`, and `executePlan` calls it for `comment` and `reopen` as well as `file`. An issue that already has a parent may then fail to link; the error is stored as `subIssueError`, not fatal. Unverified: whether GitHub refuses without `replace_parent` needs a throwaway issue pair. Read in the code 2026-10-13.

#### `renovate.json` says "CLI smoke-tested", but the smoke workflow only loads the modules `[fid: renovate-cli-smoke-tested-wording]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

The `roku-deploy` and `rooibos-roku` group and the husky and lint-staged group descriptions in `renovate.json` say "CLI smoke-tested by `_smoke-test-deps.yml`". That workflow's header says it loads modules and does not run the CLIs, and no step covers `rooibos-roku`. Related: `smoke-test-checks-unused-roku-deploy-api`. Fix the wording (or add the coverage). Checked 2026-10-13.

#### Server-upgrade triage ignores endpoints newly marked deprecated, so it reacts only at removal `[fid: server-upgrade-flag-deprecated-endpoints]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

Jellyfin 12.0 marked `GET /Artists`, `GET /Artists/{name}` and `GET /Artists/AlbumArtists` deprecated (`deprecated: true` in the 12.0 OpenAPI spec), and the app calls `/Artists` and `/Artists/AlbumArtists` (see `docs/architecture/api-usage-manifest.json`). The server-upgrade triage from 10.11.8 to 12.0 reported nothing about it: `scripts/generate/spec-diff.js` does not read the `deprecated` flag, so the pipeline only reacts when an endpoint is removed (`endpoint-removed`), at the release that breaks the app. Upstream deprecates at least one major release before removing, so reading the flag would give a release cycle of warning.

Close it by adding a deprecation change kind to `spec-diff.js` (an operation whose `deprecated` turns true), surfacing it from `scripts/generate/findings-candidates.js` only for endpoints the app uses, with tests beside the existing `endpoint-removed` ones. Then rerun the findings from 10.11.8 to 12.0 so the `/Artists` deprecation shows up, and decide how the app moves off those endpoints. Found while closing the Jellyfin 12 readiness project, 2026-10-13.

#### `comment-voice-ratchet.js` keeps hand-kept skip lists beside git's list of visible files `[fid: doc-citation-ratchet-scans-gitignored]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

The push-blocking half is fixed: #1236 made the ratchet read only files git tracks or would track (`gitVisiblePaths()`, `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`), with tests that a gitignored file is not counted. It is now [`comment-voice-ratchet.js`](../scripts/lint/comment-voice-ratchet.js) (`npm run lint:comment-voice`), which checks code comments only.

Left: the walk still keeps the hand-kept skip lists `EPHEMERAL_RELS` and `ROOT_ONLY_SKIP_RELS` beside git's list. Closes when they are removed, or each kept with a comment saying what git's list does not already cover.

#### Upstream watch: `brighterscript-formatter` multi-line-AA indent regression `[fid: bsfmt-multiline-indent-regression]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: `brighterscript-formatter` releases after 1.7.28 that fix the [#140](https://github.com/rokucommunity/brighterscript-formatter/pull/140) "Multi-line Function parameters" indent regression

Last upstream version seen: 1.7.28 (checked 2026-06-28). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `bsfmt-multiline-indent-regression` row.

Where JellyRock stands: pinned to 1.7.27. 1.7.28 over-indents every statement after a multi-line associative-array call argument (for example `fn("...", {\n ... \n})`): the indent level is never popped, so the over-indent cascades past `end sub`/`end function` into the next top-level declaration. Cosmetic only (BrightScript blocks are keyword-delimited, so it still compiles) but `bsfmt --check` enforces the corruption. Renovate re-proposes 1.7.28 as a patch (no version hold in `renovate.json` by choice); close that PR without merging until a fixed release ships

When it moves: when a release after 1.7.28 ships, format the multi-line-AA reproduction snippet under it (`npx bsfmt --write` on a `fn("x", { k: v })`-across-lines snippet followed by a top-level function); if indentation is correct, merge the Renovate bump and drop the 1.7.27 pin in `package.json`. Link the upstream issue once filed

#### The hardware sync's GitLab fetch has not run in CI yet `[fid: roku-hardware-sync-gitlab-first-ci-run]` `[captured 2026-10-21]`

`15f0377e` changed `.github/workflows/roku-hardware-sync.yml`: the fetch step no longer has `GH_TOKEN` and now reads GitLab through `npm run roku-hardware:fetch`, and the sync PR body builds a GitLab commit link from `UPSTREAM_FULL`. The fetch ran locally against GitLab (data checksum unchanged), but the workflow's shell was never run in CI, and the PR-body step only runs when device data changes. Close it after the first scheduled run following the merge: confirm the fetch step passes (`gh run list --workflow roku-hardware-sync.yml`), and trigger it with `workflow_dispatch` if no device change shows up soon. The committed dataset's provenance (`upstreamCommit` `c685ceb7`) is behind the current GitLab commit `8abc84b1` with identical data, so the next data change will also refresh it.

#### Nothing fails a tracked file that cites a gitignored path as its source `[fid: no-check-tracked-cites-gitignored-path]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

`CLAUDE.md` says never to cite a gitignored path (`tasks/`, `docs/projects/`, `.claude/handoffs/`, `.claude/plans/`) in tracked content, source comments included, and names comments as where it keeps slipping. Nothing checks it. PR #1253 is the latest case: two source comments cited the project's `SPIKES.md`, a spec cited a `.device-runs/` file, and `syncPlayState.bs` cited the project Charter and a decision recorded only in the project folder. Every CI check passed, and the citations were caught only by a review and replaced by hand.

Closes with a lint in `npm run lint` and its CI home that fails a tracked code comment or Markdown file naming one of those folders, `.device-runs/`, or a project file (`PLAN.md`, `SPIKES.md`, a project's `DECISIONS.md`) as a source. A path named as a destination, such as a skill telling an agent where to write a handoff, stays allowed, so the check needs an allowlist for that case. Not now: it needs a design choice (extend `comment-voice-ratchet.js` or `docs-check.cjs`, or a new check) and tests, including a run against the PR #1253 text before its fix to see it fail.

#### The RTA runner captures no device console, so an app crash during a spec reads as a wait timeout `[fid: rta-run-hides-device-crash]` `[captured 2026-10-24]`

A BrightScript runtime error during an RTA spec suspends the app in the micro debugger, and every later wait in the run times out with `last={}` and no hint of the crash. [`scripts/rta-run.js`](../scripts/rta-run.js) and [`tests/rta/lib/driver.js`](../tests/rta/lib/driver.js) open no console tail; `driver.js` only names the telnet command in its deploy-timeout message. **Evidence:** SyncPlay phase C (2026-10-23, Streaming Stick 4K): a type mismatch in `syncPlayQueue.isStaleQueueUpdate` failed seven of eight specs as plain timeouts, and the backtrace surfaced only after a rerun with `nc` on port 8085 held open by hand. Not done in that PR: it is a runner change with its own tests. **Close:** have `rta-run.js` hold a console tail for the run (share the reader `two-roku-console-readers` proposes rather than writing a third), surface any `runtime error` or `Backtrace:` lines seen since the spec started in the failed wait's diagnosis, and save the tail beside the run record.

**Update 2026-11-06:** since #1305 the runner does save the console (`console-capture.js`), but it still never reads it. On PR #1329's dev build the app stopped at launch with `runtime error &hf3` before ODC answered, and the run printed `registry.js`'s `readTimedOut()` message (blaming the `setupClientSocket` defect of the RTA library, followup `rta-odc-connect-hang`). The `uncaughtException` handler exits without printing the console path, and the run closes as `crashed`, which the ledger drops from the pass/fail record, so a build that crashes at launch is never counted as failing. Recommended fix (`scan`): on the read timeout or any `uncaughtException`, read the lines captured since the deploy; if they hold `runtime error` or `Backtrace:`, report the app crash with its error line, print the console path, and close the run `failed`. Rejected: printing the path only (keeps the wrong verdict and the dropped record). Not checked: whether a Vitest worker's own mid-suite ODC calls mislabel a crash the same way.

#### An RTA run whose named specs skip every case looks like a pass `[fid: rta-run-silent-skips]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

With no `RTA_SERVER_*` set, `npm run test:rta` targets the public demo, which has no push channel, so `syncplay-controls` and `syncplay-correction` skip every case and the run exits 0 (10 of 10 skipped, 2026-10-31). A reader takes that for a pass, and CI runs no RTA. Not fixed now: it is a change to `scripts/rta-run.js` with its own tests, unrelated to PR #1296. Closes when the run's summary names how many cases skipped and why, or fails when a named spec ran nothing.

#### The Rooibos runner prints a stack trace when the device lock refuses `[fid: test-runner-lock-refusal-trace]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

`main()` in `scripts/run-roku-tests.js` calls `acquireDeviceLock()` outside its `try`, so a busy device ends the run in an unhandled stack trace, with no run record. Seen 2026-11-01 while other sessions' RTA runs held the lock. The lock itself worked: nothing deployed.

**Fix shape:** catch the refusal and print its message with a non-zero exit, using `refusalLine()` from `scripts/device-lock.js`.

#### A change to the Rooibos runner skips the device tests `[fid: device-tests-skip-runner-changes]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

The `Detect test-relevant changes` pattern in `device-unit-tests.yml` leaves out `scripts/`, so a change to the runner skips the device suite. #1301 changed `scripts/run-roku-tests.js` and its device job reported success in 13 s without running a test.

**Fix shape:** add `scripts/run-roku-tests.js`, `scripts/lib/rooibos-summary.cjs` and the patches the run applies to the pattern.

#### `screenshots:capture` and `demo` lose their registry restore when an agent stops the task `[fid: capture-demo-restore-cut-by-task-stop]` `[captured 2026-11-08]`

`scripts/capture-screenshots.js` and `tests/rta/demos/run.mjs` restore the registry inside their own process (`armRestoreOnInterrupt()` in `tests/rta/lib/registry.js`). An agent's task stop kills the whole process tree about a second after its first stop signal, so that restore is cut short and the device stays dirty until the next run repairs it from the snapshot. `scripts/rta-run.js` stopped having this problem in PR #1337 by handing its restore to `scripts/rta-restore.js --takeover`, started outside its tree by `spawnOutsideTree()`.

Not done there because it is a separate fix for separate entry points, and a store capture is a long device run to retest. To close: give the helper a mode that waits for the entry point's own process to exit instead of a Vitest group (`runTakeover()` in `scripts/lib/restore-takeover.js` takes its process calls as `deps`), make both entry points hand off to it on a signal, and check a task stop on a device leaves no snapshot and a free lock.

#### An RTA run stopped before the suite starts is recorded as crashed, not interrupted `[fid: rta-early-stop-folds-crashed]` `[captured 2026-11-08]`

The fix "Free the device when a run is stopped before the suite starts" added a handler in `scripts/rta-run.js` for a stop before the Vitest spawn (`onStopOnce`, released right after the spawn). It frees the lock and exits 130, but it never calls `run.close(RUN_OUTCOMES.INTERRUPTED)`, so `run-record.js`'s exit net labels a deliberate operator stop `crashed` (seen 2026-11-08 on the Stick 4K). The ledger then drops it as a non-sample for the wrong reason. The abandon path after the spawn shows the intended fold: it closes `interrupted` before it exits.

Not fixed now: the handler is installed before `const run = beginRun(...)`, so reading `run` from it throws while that `const` is in its temporal dead zone. A fix needs a `let` declared earlier, or a close that is installed once `beginRun` has run, plus a test that a stop at each stage folds `interrupted`. Close it when a stop before the spawn folds as `interrupted`.

#### Another checkout's test run deployed over an RTA run that held the device lock `[fid: deploy-over-held-device-lock]` `[captured 2026-11-10]`

An RTA run that held the device lock was interrupted by another checkout's deploy to the same Stick 4K (2026-11-10). The `syncplay-correction` run started at 16:33:57 UTC with `locked: true` in its ledger record. The device console shows the app exiting at 16:35:15 with no crash trace and a different build (`JellyRock run-312130b1`) compiling three seconds later; every later case failed with "Failed to connect to Roku at ... port 9000", and the run was recorded `blocked`. Right after, `npm run device:status` showed the lock held by `roku device tests` (`scripts/run-roku-tests.js`) from the main clone, building `bsconfig-tdd-tmp-migrations.json`, and the device left mid-restore.

Both runners take the lock through `acquireDeviceLock` (`scripts/rta-run.js`, `scripts/run-roku-tests.js`), so one of them went around it. Not checked: whether that run degraded (`RTA_SKIP_LOCK`, a read-only path, a failed write) or keyed the lock differently, and whether its own log says it ran unlocked. Related: `no-lock-across-multi-step-campaign`, `acquire-lock-unguarded-read-throws`.

Why it matters: a run that loses the device mid-way reads as an app failure until someone finds the other build in the console, and the registry restore it leaves owed holds an auth token.

Closes with: the cause found from the other run's record and the lock's history for that minute, and a runner that refuses to deploy over a held lock, or the reason it had to.

#### Upstream watch: BrighterScript compiling a member access after a const or enum member written with its namespace `[fid: bsc-namespace-const-inlining-upstream]` `[captured 2026-11-13]`

BrighterScript 1.0.0-alpha.56 (pinned) and alpha.57 compile it wrong when a namespaced const or enum member is written with its namespace and a member access follows it: the dotted chain is read as one name. `ns.PREFIX.len()` compiles to `ns_PREFIX_len()`, `ns.Kind.Movie.len()` to `ns_Kind_Movie_len()` and `ns.CFG.width` to `ns_CFG_width`, none of which exists, so the app crashes or reads garbage at run time with no build diagnostic. A top-level enum member (`TE.A.len()` compiles to `"aaa".len()`), the relative form inside the namespace and a local that shares the name of a const all compile correctly (measured 2026-11-13 with the `bsc` CLI and the repo's compiler options). Found 2026-11-13, when the library screens' `libraryTab.serverLandings` crashed the unit suite on a Roku Ultra.

The `no-namespace-const-call` BSC plugin fails the build on the broken shapes. Its Vitest test compiles every shape through BrighterScript and fails when the output changes, which is the retirement trigger: when it fails because a broken shape now compiles correctly, retire the plugin (the file, its test, its six `bsconfig` entries in `bsconfig.json`, `bsconfig-prod.json` and the four `bsconfig-tests*.json`, its two rows in `build-and-tooling.md` and the sentence there about the test configs). No upstream issue was found in `rokucommunity/brighterscript` (searched 2026-11-13); reporting one is a public post, so it waits for the operator.

#### translations:seed context clones source code into the repo's .cache, where other tools can walk into it `[fid: seed-code-checkouts-in-repo-cache]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

`fetchSourceCode()` in `scripts/translations-seed.js` clones each seed source's full code into the repo's `.cache/translation-seed/<id>-<sha>-code/`, so every checkout downloads its own copy, and other tools can walk into it. ESLint crashed on a cloned project's own config there, and now ignores `.cache/` (`eslint.config.js`). Prettier, markdownlint, the spellchecker and `scripts/lib/lint-excludes.cjs` still don't exclude `.cache/`, and only `.cache/translation-seed/` is gitignored.

To close: clone into the per-user cache folder (`${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-$HOME/.cache}/jellyrock/translation-seed/`, with a test and a line in `docs/dev/translations.md`), or keep the location and add `.cache/` to the other lint excludes. Not done in the PR that added `context`, because the ESLint ignore unblocked the push and moving the cache is a seeder change of its own.

#### `device:status` says `held` for a lease that has already expired, so a wait on `free` never ends `[fid: device-status-reports-expired-lease-held]` `[captured 2026-11-21]`

[`scripts/device-lock.js`](../scripts/device-lock.js)'s `status` command prints `held by …` for any lock ref that exists and never compares the ref's date with `DEFAULT_LEASE_MS`. Only `acquireDeviceLock` applies the lease, through `stealIfExpired()`. So after a failed release (followup `release-ref-swallows-failed-delete`) the status line keeps saying `held` for as long as nobody starts a run. Seen 2026-11-18 on a Streaming Stick 4K: a `test:rta` run ended with its lock leaked, and a background series that waited for `npm run device:status` to print `free` stalled about three hours, with no holder process alive. Whether a new run at that point would have taken the expired lease was not tried: `npm run device:release` cleared it. **Close it by** having `status` read the lease clock and print an expired lease as such (for example `expired lease of …; the next run takes it`), so a script or agent that waits on the status line is not blocked by a dead holder. Not fixed now: it changes what a shared command prints and needs a unit case through the `setTransport` seam.

### components

#### The `cell-load` counters are silently zeroed whenever a screen replaces its content root, so any measurement of a session that does so under-reports. `[fid: cell-load-counters-zeroed-by-root-swap]` `[captured 2026-08-22]`

The counters hang off the content root, and `BaseGridView` calls `initTextureManager` on a NEW root at four **mid-session** sites — a sort/filter change, the resume-time content-freshness check in `onScreenShown`, the alpha jump, and voice search — so a session that hits one discards every count up to that point and emits only the tail at hide. `SearchRow`'s two sites are harmless: the root it builds at init is empty, so nothing is lost unless a second query runs. **No recorded number is wrong** — each of the four scripted sweeps opens its screen once — which is exactly why this is invisible until someone measures a filter/sort/alpha/voice path and compares it to one that stayed put. Diagnosed 2026-08-22 from the call sites plus `.device-runs/measure/measurements.jsonl`; no fix designed. The two shapes are *carry the counters across the swap* (keeps one session per screen visit, but needs a call at each of the seven sites — the same missing-call-site class that produced three instrument defects in two sessions) or *emit before replacing* (no new call-site risk, but publishes two sessions per launch, so every consumer must name a `--component`).

#### Consolidate the `SceneManager` `reloadHomeRequested` Home-reload signal onto `JRScene` (alongside `contentVersion`), so both view-refresh signals live on the router host instead of split across `SceneManager`. Surfaced while cleaning up a stale `JRScene.xml` comment during the #550 sgRouter PR-hardening pass. `[fid: reload-home-requested-onto-jrscene]` `[captured 2026-06-28]`

#### A queued next episode always picks the device-best version, so a deliberately chosen alternate version is not carried forward (#933 follow-up). `[fid: queued-next-episode-loses-chosen-version]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`
