On Jellyfin 12.0 an item's alternate versions are selectable per item, but `LoadVideoContentTask` re-selects for each queued episode independently, so picking e.g. a black-and-white or director's-cut version for one episode is forgotten by the next. jellyfin-web solves it by matching the version NAME (jellyfin-web#7984, in v12.0), which also blocks a legitimate upgrade — an episode that only exists in 1080p followed by one that has a 4K version should use the 4K. **Fix shape:** carry the preference only when the user picked the version EXPLICITLY (an auto-selected version keeps re-selecting device-best per episode), matching the next episode's versions by what distinguishes the picked one. No longer blocked: the version-label work shipped in #953. Its `versionLabels` compares the versions of ONE item, so matching a version across episodes whose files carry unrelated release names still needs its own rule.

#### Normalize audio queue items to one transformed (camelCase) shape at queue-build time — `QuickPlayTask.doAlbum/doArtist/doPlaylist/doInstantMix` + the `set()` paths store RAW Jellyfin items while single-song taps store transformed ones, so `createQueueItem` reads both shapes with raw-key fallbacks (`albumName`←`Album`, `primaryImageTag`←`ImageTags.Primary`). Transforming at queue-build kills the duality so every consumer reads one predictable shape (also removes the latent `albumName`-vs-`Album` mismatch class). Deferred from the #550 audio now-playing contract fix (A+C+D shipped: `createQueueItem` carries the now-playing display fields + `QueueItem` interface + spec gate). Bigger blast radius (video queue paths) → needs a hardware verification pass. `[fid: audio-queue-items-one-camelcase-shape]` `[captured 2026-06-28]`

#### A failed latest-media row keeps its `type: "Loading"` skeleton placeholder indefinitely `[fid: failed-latest-row-keeps-loading-skeleton]` `[captured 2026-08-03]`

**A failed latest-media row keeps its `type: "Loading"` skeleton placeholder indefinitely**, so it reads as "still loading" when it has actually finished and failed. Deliberate as of PR #762: the alternative (removing the row) makes the next successful refresh re-insert it via `populateRowFromData`'s else-branch, which visibly pops the row in and shifts every row below it — measured on device with 8 libraries and five forced HTTP 500 responses, where the skipped rows held their single placeholder and nothing moved. Accepted cost: it only self-corrects on the next `Home.refresh()` (i.e. `onScreenShown`), so sitting on Home leaves the false loading state up. The better answer is a distinct failed/empty placeholder that keeps the row in place without lying — deferred because it needs a visual design, a translation key, and `JRRowItem` work to render a non-`Loading` placeholder type.

#### Home never removes the row of a library newly added to `latestItemsExcludes`. `[fid: home-keeps-row-of-excluded-library]` `[captured 2026-08-03]`

`HomeRows.onLibrariesLoaded` recomputes `m.filteredLatest` on every refresh, but that only decides which libraries get REQUESTED — nothing clears the row of a library that dropped OUT of the list, so its stale content sits on Home indefinitely. Note the setting has no JellyRock UI: it arrives from the Jellyfin server's user config (`SessionDataTransformer.bs` ← `configData.LatestItemsExcludes`), so it changes out from under the client and the app never sees an edit event. Fix shape: diff the previous `filteredLatest` against the new one in `onLibrariesLoaded` and remove the rows for dropped ids (`removeRowAtIndex` already exists). This is pre-existing and independent of the #728 orchestrator work — surfaced while reviewing PR #762, deliberately not folded in.

#### Genres view skeleton rows draw a single placeholder cell where the filled row shows up to 7 — cosmetic; flagged in PR #779 as deliberately not done. `[fid: genres-skeleton-single-placeholder-cell]` `[captured 2026-08-08]`

#### A Genres load whose per-genre sample fetches ALL fail renders titled rows with empty slots and no explanation. `[fid: genres-all-sample-fetches-fail-empty]` `[captured 2026-08-09]`

Measured on a Stick 4K by stubbing out `LoadItemsTask2.populateGenreRow`: 14 rows delivered, every one childless — row titles, row spacing and the focus ring all still draw, but nothing sits under them and `emptyText` stays hidden, so the screen reads as broken rather than as failed. Pre-existing and deliberate on the data side: `populateGenreRow` leaves a row in place with no children when its request never answered (pool down, run budget expired) or came back `ok = false`, on the reasoning that a genre that can't be sampled still belongs in the list. The state-reporting half is now fixed — `loadState` counts ITEMS not rows, so this reports `empty` instead of claiming `loaded` — but not the pixels. Fix shape needs a design call: show `emptyText` when no row could be filled, or hold the skeleton placeholder on rows that came back empty. The latter is the same distinct failed-vs-loading placeholder the failed-latest-media-row entry above already wants, so it is worth solving once for both.

#### The movie/TV Genres view silently truncates at its query limit (100) and reports the truncated load as complete. `[fid: genres-silently-truncates-at-100]` `[captured 2026-08-09]`

Proven on device 2026-08-09 (`.178`, `tasks/probes/genre-truncation.mjs`): with the genre query's `limit` forced to 3 against an 8-genre library, 3 rows drew, no further page arrived in 25 s, and `loadState` read `loaded` — the app does not know it truncated. Mechanism: the `Genres` branch of `BaseGridView.onItemDataLoaded()` `return`s before `m.loadedItems` is set and before the `loadMoreData()` block, so it never paginates. Music genres fall through to the grid path and DO paginate correctly, which matters because music is where genre counts actually explode. Exposure is unquantified — the two servers measured hold 8–23 movie genres, and anime/multi-language metadata is the untested hypothesis for crossing 100. Do NOT "fix" it by raising the limit: 1000 genres would fire ~334 `apiPipeline` waves against a 20 s run budget, trading a hidden ceiling for a view that times out. The correct fix is Phase 2 viewport-lazy hydration (fetch the full `{id, name}` list, hydrate only the visible rows) — record it as a NAMED requirement of that work, not a hoped-for side effect, since Phase 2's stated justification today is Home at 1000+ libraries. Nothing in RTA catches this: `assertGenreRowsOwnTheirItems` checks row/item pairing, not completeness.

#### `setServer`'s `savedOnly` variant fires when there were no saved servers EITHER, so it names a third workload as the second. `[fid: set-server-saved-only-variant-misnamed]` `[captured 2026-08-15]`

`SetServerScreen.onScanForServersComplete` stamps `discovered` when SSDP returned anything and `savedOnly` otherwise — but that else-branch covers two different runs: "discovery found nothing and the registry had the picker's content all along" (the measured case) and "there was nothing anywhere" — a first-run device, empty picker, focus falling through to the manual-entry field, and neither merge pass doing any work at all. By the variant's own justification (a run that cannot say which workload it was cannot be compared against one that can, per ADR 0028), that is a third population wearing the second's name. **Unreachable on any device that has ever been signed in**, because Change server clears `server` but preserves `saved_servers` — which is why all 18 launches of the n=15 series stamped `savedOnly` correctly, and why this is a latent naming bug rather than a wrong number. **Fix shape:** a three-way stamp (`discovered` / `savedOnly` / `empty`) keyed on the count of saved entries the picker was actually built from — `savedServers.serverList.Count()` is already in scope at the paint site. **Deliberately NOT folded into the `feat/measure-server-select` branch**: it is the only finding from that branch's review that touches app code, and merging it would mean the shipped build is no longer the build the n=15 numbers were taken on. Costs a re-measure to land honestly, so it belongs with the next `setServer` series rather than ahead of it.

#### A grid cell's failure-glyph wipe is counted in the BENIGN bucket, so `wipesReload` reads 0 on grids structurally. `[fid: grid-failure-glyph-wipe-benign-bucket]` `[captured 2026-08-20]`

`GridItem.unloadGridTexture` calls `resetPlaceholderToLoading`, which fires `cellLoad.glyphWipe(WIPE_BIND)` — but an unloading cell is not changing content, it is the same item scrolling out of the render window. [`cellLoad.bs`](../source/utils/cellLoad.bs)'s own constants define `WIPE_BIND` as "a cell whose content is changing anyway" and `WIPE_RELOAD` as "a cell SITTING STILL showing a stable glyph", which is the one that reads to a user as flicker. The `RowList` twin classifies the identical event the other way: [`JRRowItem`](../components/ui/rowitem/JRRowItem.bs)'s `unloadTexture` leaves the glyph alone and `reloadTexture` wipes it as `WIPE_RELOAD`. So the two geometries file the same user-visible flicker in opposite buckets, and a grid's `wipesReload 0` is not evidence that a grid does not flicker. Also note `reloadGridTexture` calls no `glyphWipe` at all, where its `RowList` twin does. Surfaced 2026-08-20 while scoping `cellSweepGrid`'s return leg — before it the grid's reload path was unreachable (`reloads` 1 across three launches), so the wrong-bucket filing could not be measured; it now can. **Fix shape:** decide which bucket a wipe from the texture lifecycle belongs in — probably a third `WIPE_*` source for an event in the texture lifecycle, since it is neither a bind nor a reload — and make `GridItem` and `JRRowItem` agree before either counter is used as evidence. Deliberately not folded into the workload-scripting change: it is an app-side instrumentation fix with its own device-test cost.

#### Home's Continue Watching row fetches UNBOUNDED — it is the only Home row with no limit at all. `[fid: continue-watching-fetch-unbounded]` `[captured 2026-08-24]`

The `continue` row in `loadItems()` ([`LoadItemsTask.bs`](../components/home/LoadItemsTask.bs)) builds `BuildGetResumeItemsRequest` with only `EnableTotalRecordCount` and never sets a limit, and nothing slices `data.Items` afterwards, so every resume item the server returns becomes a row child. `injectApiParams()` ([`misc.bs`](../source/utils/misc.bs)) merges image and `fields` defaults and adds no limit either — read, not assumed. Every other Home row carries an explicit bound: Next Up 69, On Now 16, Active Recordings 16, `channelPrograms` 20, Latest In 16. Surfaced 2026-08-24 while auditing which Home rows can cross `TEXTURE_BUFFER_THRESHOLD = 20` for `task-thread-budget` phase C — this one can, without ceiling, which is part of why that phase's "windowing never runs on Home" premise was wrong. **Not measured**: our fixture runs ~10.7 items/row, so it has never been large here, and a user with a long resume list is exactly the case nobody has looked at. **Fix shape:** decide whether the unbounded request is intentional (and say so in a comment) or give it a limit consistent with its neighbors.

#### Trim the library grid's alpha menu to only the letters that actually have media. `[fid: library-grid-alpha-menu-trim]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

Today the A-Z jump menu offers every letter whether or not the library holds anything under it, so a user can select `Q` and land on nothing — a dead control that looks live. **What it needs first:** the grid loads items lazily, so nothing on the client currently knows the full set of first letters up front. The open question is whether that set can be obtained cheaply — a `SortName`-ordered id-only query, an existing Jellyfin filters/letters endpoint, or a first-page-per-letter probe — rather than by loading all titles, which is the naive read and would defeat the lazy loading. **Scope:** UX win, `components/ItemGrid`, NOT part of `task-thread-budget`. Raised by the user 2026-08-25 while re-scoping that project.

#### Analyze and optimize the library grid's lazy loading (`components/ItemGrid`). `[fid: library-grid-lazy-loading-optimize]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

Home's loading path now has a validated cost model (render cost is per-row and saturates at the visible window; task-thread transform is ~1.6 ms per item, linear and uncapped — see `docs/dev/home-first-paint-performance.md`), and nothing equivalent exists for the grid. The grid is a different shape — `MarkupGrid` with no horizontal buffer, `updateGridTextureBufferRange` instead of the `RowList` path — so the Home model does NOT carry over and must be re-derived rather than assumed. The `item-grid` measurement family and the `cell-load` counters already exist for it. **Scope:** deliberately deferred by the user 2026-08-25 as NOT part of `task-thread-budget`; pick it up as its own piece of work once the Home limit/windowing work lands.

#### Both `remove-phase` arms sat at an at-gate `loadsStarted` band of 90-91 (29 of 40) against the 100-102 band published from the previous campaign, and nothing separates probe overhead from the app-version change `[fid: remove-phase-arms-loadsstarted-band]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

(2.26.0 here against 2.25.x then). A ~10% move in the number this project CLASSIFIES launches by, so it is not cosmetic — Home's high/low bind mode is defined at that gate. **The discriminating experiment is one n=20 arm on 2.26.0 WITHOUT the probe** (~11 min on `.177`): if the band stays at 90-91 the probe is exonerated and the shift belongs to the app version. ⚠️ **The old 100-102 band cannot be re-derived to compare against** — that campaign's at-gate values were console-only and `measure.js` discards the console on a healthy run, so the comparison arm has to be taken fresh rather than re-read. Surfaced 2026-08-25 reviewing `perf/home-row-removal-phase-probe`; [`home-first-paint-performance.md`](dev/home-first-paint-performance.md) marks it Open, but a dev-doc paragraph is not a surface `/catchup` reads, which is why it is here.

#### Next Up sends NO `limit`; its row size for a real user on a large library is unmeasured. `[fid: next-up-sends-no-limit]` `[captured 2026-09-17]`

Uncapped since 2026-08-26 (Continue Watching is the precedent). First episodes of series the user has not started are now dropped on every server version (`next-up-started-series-filter`, #973), so the row holds only series with a watched episode. Still unmeasured: its size for a real user with history on a large library (count `#homeRows.content.<nextUpRow>.getChildCount()`, or read `reportSweep`'s row widths); if it is in the hundreds, give Next Up a generous cap.

#### `JRDialog` caps its button row at 3 (`MAX_BUTTONS`) but only WARNS when exceeded — the same overflow family as #788, one surface over. `[fid: jrdialog-max-buttons-only-warns]` `[captured 2026-09-07]`

Enforcement was deliberately left out of #757 and the epic's own closing note flags it as wanting a consistent answer. #788 settled the row case with a derived cap plus a `More` menu (`source/utils/buttonOverflow.bs`); the dialog case is different enough not to reuse it directly — a button ROW has no menu to spill into and `showChoiceDialog` already falls back to `JRListDialog` when labels are too wide — so the open question is whether the warn should become an error, a fallback, or stay advisory.

#### A `parkPlayerAfterSupersede()` that originated in `onState`'s terminal `error` branch is not reversed by a voice/remote `play` — read from code, not reproduced. `[fid: park-player-after-supersede-not-reversed]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

Since ADR 0034 that branch raises `m.hasPlaybackFailed` instead of unobserving `state`; the flag clears only when a new stream loads, so `onState`'s `playing` branch (which reverses the park) stays gated. Unchanged from v2.28.0 for the player; the difference is that `PlayerHostView` now still hears a later `finished`. The other three `showPlaybackErrorDialog` routes keep `onState` running. Recorded with its reasoning in [`onstate-error-branch-not-reentrant`](architecture/tech-debt.md#onstate-error-branch-not-reentrant); still open whether an error-route park is even *meant* to resume rather than exit.

#### `playbackTimer`'s observer is dropped on `stopped` and never re-registered, so a voice or remote-control resume of a STOPPED (not finished) player reports no periodic progress. `[fid: playback-timer-observer-dropped-on-stopped]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

[`VideoPlayerView.bs`](../components/video/VideoPlayerView.bs) observes `playbackTimer.fire` exactly once, in `init()` (L118). The `stopped` (L1848) and `finished` (L1860) branches of `onState` both `unobserveField("fire")`, and the `playing` branch only does `control = "start"` (L1833) — nothing re-observes. **Reachability confirmed by trace 2026-09-09, not by runtime observation:** `handleTransport` is declared in [`VideoPlayerView.xml`](../components/video/VideoPlayerView.xml), delegated from [`PlayerHostView.bs`](../components/video/PlayerHostView.bs), and reached from `source/main.bs` (Roku voice) and [`remoteDispatch.bs`](../source/remotecontrol/remoteDispatch.bs) (remote control); on a non-paused, non-playing player it sets `m.top.control = "play"` on the EXISTING node rather than going through `PlayerHostView.mountPlayer()`, which is the only thing that creates a fresh `VideoPlayerView` and re-runs `init()`. So the instance is kept and the observer stays gone. **The `finished` half of this is NOT reachable and was ruled out:** `PlayerHostView.onPlayerStateChange` early-returns on anything but `finished`, so on `finished` it proceeds to the advance/exit logic, which either remounts a fresh `VideoPlayerView` (re-running `init()`) or navigates away. `stopped` is the reachable half precisely because that early return means nothing tears the player down — it stays mounted, which is the state `parkPlayerAfterSupersede` leaves it in and which the comment reversing the park in the `playing` branch names as resumable by voice or remote control. Consequence not yet measured: the explicit stop/finished reports still fire, so the expected symptom is a stale Continue Watching position mid-playback rather than a lost session. **Fix shape:** drop the two `unobserveField("fire")` calls rather than adding a re-observe — the timer is registered once in `init()` and torn down in `onDestroy`, so `control` alone should drive its lifecycle, which is the register-once shape documented in [`components/CLAUDE.md`](../components/CLAUDE.md). Same observer lifecycle family as #896 / #898 / #899, opposite direction — an observer lost rather than accumulated.

#### Subtitle download from the playback OSD `[fid: subtitle-download-from-osd]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

(#750 follow-up, low priority — contingent on #750 shipping first): extend the `ItemDetails` subtitle-management surface to the player OSD so a user can search/download subtitles mid-playback instead of backing out to the detail screen. Larger than the detail-screen case because it needs a mid-stream subtitle track reload after the server-side download lands: verified on a local 10.11.11 server that a downloaded external subtitle is inserted at stream index 0 and **renumbers every other stream** (video/audio indices shift), so the player's `selectedAudioStreamIndex` / `subtitleTrack` state must be re-synced from a fresh item fetch, not just appended to. Cheapest if the #750 panel is built self-contained (owns its own API calls + focus, exposes `itemId`/`isOpen`/`subtitlesChanged`) so the OSD can mount the same component. Deliberately out of scope for the initial #750 implementation.

#### `ItemDetails`' refresh takes focus from an open child panel, and the panel is the only thing that noticed. `[fid: item-details-refresh-steals-panel-focus]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

Setting `subtitlesChanged` makes `ItemDetails` re-fetch and rebuild, and the rebuild moves focus to its own button group even while a child panel is open and holding it. Measured on device during #750 Phase 4: after a subtitle delete, focus left the open `SubtitlePanel` and landed on `ItemDetails`' own `manageSubtitlesButton` BEHIND it, where neither UP nor DOWN recovered it — `ItemDetails.onKeyEvent()` (`if key = "down" and m.buttonGrp.isInFocusChain() and m.subtitlePanelOpen then return true`) swallows the key, so the remote simply appears dead. **Established by isolation, not argument**: with that one line removed from the delete path focus stayed in the panel across the identical sequence; with it restored it did not. Two earlier explanations were checked and are WRONG — the emptied list stealing focus, and `setupButtons`' focus restore (`isInitialLoad` is false while the panel is open and `wasButtonGroupFocused` is false), **so the exact stealing line is still unidentified**. Nothing is broken today: `SubtitlePanel` works around it by deferring `subtitlesChanged` to `closePanel()`, which is right on its own merits. The HOST behavior is unchanged, and any future child panel of `ItemDetails` that triggers a refresh while holding focus hits the same thing — the deferred OSD subtitle variant is exactly that shape. **Fix shape:** find the line, then guard the host's focus restore on `m.subtitlePanelOpen` (generally: don't take focus during a refresh when a child panel owns it). The instrument that cracked it is `odc.getFocusedNode({ includeNode: true })` on an RTA build — it names the focused node instead of inviting inference.

#### Audit `Poster.loadSync` app-wide, then fix only what needs it — do NOT convert every image. `[fid: poster-load-sync-audit]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

`loadSync` defaults to FALSE, which the Roku docs define as "loaded asynchronously in a background thread, and **may not appear immediately**"; with true "and the `uri` field specifies a local file (in the pkg:/images directory), the image is loaded synchronously, and appears immediately". **Measured in the subtitle panel (#750):** the async default was the badge icons visibly popping in AND the roughly 28-pixel rightward jump of whatever badge followed one, because a Poster with no bitmap yet contributes nothing to a `LayoutGroup` — 48 async `loadStatus` transitions during a single search, **0** after setting it. Fixed there for the badge icons, the row delete icon and `TextButton`'s icon; **every other local icon in the app still has the async default** (`IconButton`, `Toast`, `CommunityRating`, `CriticRating`, `JRRowItem`, the overhang icons…). **The work is an AUDIT, not a sweep.** `loadSync` pays the fetch on the RENDER THREAD — the docs' own caveat is that it "might cause brief glitches in the rendering while the image is being fetched and loaded" — so it is right for a handful of tiny local PNGs loaded once at construction and potentially wrong for anything large, numerous, or remote (a poster grid must never do this). Find the places where a local icon's late arrival is actually visible (pop-in, or the layout moving because it sits in a `LayoutGroup`) and convert those; leave the rest. **Then write the resulting rule down** — the durable form is something like "small local `pkg:/images` icon whose absence would move layout → `loadSync` = true; remote or large → never" — in `components/CLAUDE.md` beside the existing list/focus entry, so the next person does not have to re-derive the tradeoff. A lint could plausibly encode it (`loadSync` on a `pkg:/` path under some size, flagged on an `http` one), but decide that on the audit's evidence rather than up front.

#### `PlayerHostView.destroyPlayer` likely drops the player's stop report on paths that skip the Back key. `[fid: destroy-player-drops-stop-report]` `[captured 2026-09-13]`

It runs `m.view.unobserveField("state")` before `m.view.control = "stop"`, and [`ObserverRegistry.spec.bs`](../tests/source/unit/platform/ObserverRegistry.spec.bs) recorded a parent's plain `unobserveField` on a child's field removing the child's OWN observers too — so `VideoPlayerView.onState`'s `stopped` branch, the only `reportPlayback("stop")` for a non-finished end, would not run. #914 independently saw "the host's teardown stop adds no report". Back from the remote is unaffected (`onKeyEvent` stops first). Candidate paths, read from code and not reproduced: a deep link replacing playback (`teardownForDeepLink`), voice "stop", sign-out while playing. [`playback.md`](architecture/playback.md) step 5 claims the stop IS reported there. **Fix shape:** measure first (Jellyfin activity / session after a deep link mid-episode, as #914 did), then gate `onPlayerStateChange` with a teardown flag instead of unobserving first — the ADR 0034 pattern — minding the documented stop-surfaces-as-`finished` re-entry. Surfaced 2026-09-13 reviewing #913; kept out of it as pre-existing and a separate path.

#### Manage Subtitles is offered on disc-rip items where the search can never return anything `[fid: manage-subtitles-offered-on-disc-rips]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

(#750). Jellyfin's `SubtitleManager.SearchSubtitles(Video, …)` returns an empty list unless `video.VideoType` is `VideoFile` — so an ISO image or DVD / Blu-ray folder always gets "no subtitles found" — read at the `v10.7.0`, `v10.7.7`, `v10.8.13`, `v10.9.11`, `v10.10.7`, `v10.11.11` and `v12.0` tags, unchanged. `ItemDetails` does not gate the button on the targeted version's `VideoType` (its only `VideoType = "VideoFile"` check is `buildVideoDropdownItems`), so the user is told to check provider setup when setup is fine. Documented for users in [`manage-subtitles.md`](user/features/manage-subtitles.md). **Decide:** hide the button for a non-`VideoFile` version, or keep the doc as the answer. A code change, so it needs device testing — kept out of #750's docs pass for that reason.

#### Posters and Continue Watching can show a different version's progress than the one that would play (#933 follow-up; the DISPLAY half of the version-choice work, split out 2026-09-17 by user call). `[fid: posters-show-other-version-progress]` `[captured 2026-09-19]`

The playback half shipped: `versionResume.chooseResumeSource()` is the single chooser for the details screen, quick play and casts, so all of them resume the same version (most recently played, unless the in-progress versions sit within 30 s of each other, where the best version for the device among them wins — recorded as `version-resume-near-level`); episodes a queue arrives at start fresh instead (`episode-queue-starts-fresh`). What is still unresolved is DISPLAY: a library poster and the Continue Watching row bind the item's OWN `UserData`, so with an alternate in progress the bar can belong to a version other than the one Play would start, and with ONLY an alternate in progress a poster shows no bar at all. **Not a scope call to make in passing:** a per-tile `GET /UserItems/{id}/UserData` is one request per tile, exactly the crossing cost [`async.md`](architecture/async.md) warns about in a grid, and the cheap alternative — preferring the item's own version when positions are near-level — would AMEND `version-resume-near-level` rather than merely narrow it. **Also weigh (user idea, 2026-09-19): a visual cue that another version of an episode or movie is available**, on the tiles and episode rows where the choice otherwise stays hidden until the details screen; capture what those screens show today on 12.0 and 10.11 before designing it (UI screenshots work — only playback cannot be captured). **Fix shape:** weigh the options and MEASURE a per-tile read on a real grid before choosing; the signals available without any request are the item's own position and the server's source order.

#### The Cinema Mode pre-roll can play in front of an alternate version's resume on 12.0 (PR #958 follow-up; pre-existing). `[fid: cinema-pre-roll-before-alternate-resume]` `[captured 2026-09-19]`

`LoadVideoContentTask` decides the pre-roll before the version is chosen, so it reads `hasVersionInProgress` (the free source-order signal) as well as `startingPoint`, but only `quickplay.video` sets that field. `ItemDetails.resumeSeriesNextUp` arrives without it, so an item whose own version sits at 0 while an alternate is partway through gets the intro first. Pre-existing — before #958 no path set it — and kept out of it deliberately. Play All, shuffle and every item a queue arrives at are no longer part of this: they start fresh at an exact 0 (`nodeHelpers.startFresh`, which also clears `hasVersionInProgress`), so no resume follows the intro. **Agreed fix shape (user call, 2026-09-19):** compute it once in `nodeHelpers.createQueueItem`, which receives the source node and so covers every caller that passes one (quick play, chapter, Series Resume, Play from details). Unit-gate it on the `createQueueItem` contract.

#### The playback-info report renders a BLANK row for the transcode reason `AudioCodecNotSupported`, which its own contract forbids. `[fid: playback-info-blank-transcode-reason]` `[captured 2026-09-20]`

The `dialogs` RTA spec asserts "no row is ever a placeholder for missing data" and fails on `row reason.AudioCodecNotSupported rendered an empty value`, so the report is emitting a row it has no value for rather than omitting it. Measured 2026-09-20 on the local test servers with `The Dark Knight` as the hero: **byte-identical on 10.11.11, 12.0.0 and 12.1.0**, so this is version-independent and NOT v12 fallout. It has never surfaced on the public demo, whose fixture does not produce that reason — which is why three RTA baselines missed it and why it appeared the first time the suite was pointed at a local server. Note the nearby decision that transcode reasons keep the RAW server code (sentences were rejected) — so the question here is whether a reason with no displayable value should render label-only or be dropped, NOT whether to prose it. **Reproduce:** `RTA_SERVER_URL=http://192.168.1.3:8102 RTA_SERVER_USER=jellyrock-rta RTA_SERVER_PASS=jellyrock-rta RTA_HERO_MOVIE="The Dark Knight" node scripts/rta-run.js tests/rta/specs/dialogs.spec.js -t "osd info button"`.

#### JellyRock's own audio-language setting has no "Original language" choice (PR follow-up to the 12.0 original-language fix). `[fid: audio-language-no-original-choice]` `[captured 2026-09-21]`

`playbackAudioLanguagePreference` offers `webclient` / `roku` / `custom`, so a viewer can only get the original audio by setting it in the web client. Add an `original` option that `wantsOriginalAudio()` treats like the web client's sentinel, hidden on servers older than 12.0, where no item carries `OriginalLanguage`.

#### The TV guide's channel list is paged 25 at a time; larger later pages would shorten a big lineup's load — measure before changing. `[fid: tv-guide-channel-paging-25]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

A 2,000-channel lineup takes 18 s to finish loading idle (Stick 4K, 2026-09-21), ~180 ms per page of which the transform is now the smaller part, and while the user scrolls each page costs 3.6–4.7 s, mostly per-request overhead (see the API pool followup under `### api`). Keep page 1 small for first paint; bigger later pages trade fewer round trips for a larger single handoff, so compare both on a 512 MB device.

#### Dropping or cutting a TV guide program block blocks the render thread `[fid: tv-guide-block-drop-blocks-render]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

**Dropping or cutting a TV guide program block blocks the render thread**: a drop 108–256 ms on a 512 MB Roku Stick (typically ~150) and 45–137 ms on a Stick 4K; a cut back to the time window 50–235 ms and 33–83 ms (one `removeChildrenIndex` per channel row, measured 2026-09-21). Once `guidePrograms.CAP` blocks are held, scrolling costs one drop per 25 rows, and scrolling in time one cut per block per ~12 h; `reconcilePrograms` applies one trim per call, so they do not stack. Deferring them until the focus settles, or spreading a block's rows across turns, would move them out of the scroll — neither is measured yet.

#### A loading indicator for the TV guide's rows. `[fid: tv-guide-rows-loading-indicator]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

`TimeGrid` draws program cells itself and takes no custom cell component, so a spinner can only go (a) per row, in a custom `channelInfoComponentName` channel column, or (b) once for the guide, beside the channel column header, shown while `guidePrograms.spanState` reads `GuideSpanState.LOADING`. Try (b) first: one animation, and no takeover of the channel column's logo, name, focus and fallback-font rendering. Measure it on a 512 MB Stick while scrolling — an animating render thread is what slowed every Task crossing ([`threading.md`](architecture/threading.md#measured-findings)) — before considering (a).

#### Every focused TV guide program's details are fetched twice `[fid: tv-guide-program-details-fetched-twice]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

(its own PR, for its own changelog line). `Schedule.loadProgramDetails`'s callback calls `channel.ReplaceChild` before `m.fullyLoadedProgramIds[node.id] = true`, and replacing the focused cell re-enters `onProgramFocused()` synchronously — probe, 2026-09-21: `replaceStart`, then `focusIn`, then `replaceEnd` — which finds the program not yet loaded and fetches it again. Pre-existing on `main`. **Fix:** mark it loaded before the `ReplaceChild`, and check the probe's focus and replace lines show one fetch per focus.

#### The TV guide's other memory levers. `[fid: tv-guide-other-memory-levers]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

The per-block time window bounds program memory, but the 512 MB Stick still reported `generalMemoryLevel` low at 150–170 MB while swapping blocks a week ahead (2026-09-21). (a) A program cell is a full `JellyfinBaseItem` (167 fields, ~10 KB each): a lean `ContentNode` with the fields the grid and details pane read before full details load could cut that by an unmeasured factor — measure the node cost first. (b) `m.global.device.memoryLevel` is already tracked: the guide could drop its unwanted blocks when it reads `low`, as a safety valve. (c) A very dense guide (many short programs) multiplies a block's size; a program-count budget in `guidePrograms.evictions` would bound that — speculative until a lineup shows it.

#### Dialog headings cut long titles mid-word (found with the details-text fit; pre-existing). `[fid: dialog-headings-cut-mid-word]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

`JRDialogPanel`, the chrome every dialog shares, draws its title on one line truncated by character, so the description dialog cuts a long item name too ("…Stays at t…" for a 118-character episode title on the Ultra, 2026-09-22). With the details title now cut to one line, that dialog is where the full name should be readable. Candidate: let the heading wrap to two lines, and check every dialog in the family still lays out (the `JRDialogPanel.xml` ordering contract).

#### A "more info" view for the metadata the details screen has no room for (user idea, raised with the details-text fit). `[fid: details-more-info-view]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

Tags (already kept as `item.tags` by `JellyfinDataTransformer` but shown nowhere), all studios, writers and producers, external ids. The info block is full at title + three rows, so this needs its own surface and a UX design pass.

#### The caption fetch/render split left `CaptionRenderer`'s delivery path with no unit test, and there is no seam to add one. `[fid: caption-renderer-delivery-untested]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

`onCaptionDataReceived` — which starts the 100 ms timer, calls `applyFont()` and populates the cue list — cannot be driven from a spec: `m.fetchTask` is private and `isCurrentTaskEvent` compares node identity, so nothing can inject a delivery. Before the split the old spec could simply write `captionData` on the node. The two `url` specs in [`CaptionRenderer.spec.bs`](../tests/source/unit/components/video/CaptionRenderer.spec.bs) only cover the CLEAR path. That leaves the font decision untested, which is the one piece of this component that has already been shipped wrong once and reverted (`setFont()` reading the setting instead of the file on disk). **Fix shape:** extract `applyCaptionData(entries, fontAvailable)` as the declared `callFunc` and leave `onCaptionDataReceived` a thin event-unwrapping wrapper — the same pure-core / thin-shell split [`source/utils/tasks.bs`](../source/utils/tasks.bs) uses so Rooibos can reach the ledger arithmetic. Test-only interface declarations already have precedent here (8 exist, including this component's own `updateCaption`).

#### The Live TV restart's wiring has no automated test (deferred from the restart-loop fix). `[fid: live-tv-restart-wiring-untested]` `[captured 2026-09-24]`

`liveTv.bs` unit-tests the rule and the progress measure, but nothing checks that `PlayerHostView` passes `isLiveRestart = true` only from `restartLiveChannel()`, or that `VideoPlayerView.onPositionChanged` feeds `liveProgressOnPosition()` for a live item. A regression that restarts through `playCurrentQueueItem()` again resets the count on every restart and brings the endless loop back, and only a manual run would see it. An RTA spec needs a Live TV fixture the harness can start; today that is `hls-fixture.sh` outside the repo plus a tuner added to the test server by hand, so moving the fixture into the repo comes first.

#### `JRPlaceholder.onSizeChanged` crashed (`&hf3`, "Interface not a member" on `m.backdrop.setFields`) during a forced app exit (found with the Live TV stall fix, 2026-09-23; not investigated). `[fid: jr-placeholder-on-size-changed-crash]` `[captured 2026-09-24]`

Seen once on a Stick 4K when the RTA harness's hard relaunch exited the app (`EXIT_UNKNOWN`) mid-load. The same runs also hit `&hec` in `JRScrollingLabel.init()` / `LabelSmall.init()` reading `m.global.constants` at exit, which looks like the init/`m.global.constants` class `/crash-report` already tracks; the `&hf3` is a different code and may be its own class. Check the weekly crash CSVs for `JRPlaceholder.brs` before deciding whether it needs a fix.

#### Check `ItemDetails`, `PhotoDetails` and `settings` for the resume-focus trap. `[fid: resume-focus-trap-other-screens]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

Each sets focus in `onScreenShown()` but does not override `restoreScreenFocus`, which `JRScreen.handleFocus` runs right after and which restores `lastFocus` again. Home (the overhang-icon regression) and `BaseGridView` (#1033) were the two confirmed cases; for each of these three, compare what `onScreenShown()` focuses with what `lastFocus` would restore, and override `restoreScreenFocus` only where they differ. See [navigation.md → Focus management](architecture/navigation.md#focus-management). `SearchResults` was on this list and is retired; its replacement `SearchScreen` records `lastFocus` wherever it moves focus (the keyboard when its overlay opens, Try again after a failed submit), so `handleFocus` restores the same node: this check, done for it in `61e70ebe`.

#### A library grid's filter list still gives up at 10 s on a slow server (found with #1043). `[fid: grid-filter-list-10s-timeout]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

`BaseGridView.loadFilters` asks `/Items/Filters` through `fetchAsync`, and on the slow 8,643-movie fixture (Jellyfin 10.11.11, `sqlite_stat1` dropped) it timed out at 10.0 s both times it was measured (2026-09-25, 512 MB Stick) — `filter load failed`, and the options' filter lists come back empty while the page itself (60 s, `timeouts.GRID_PAGE_MS`) loads. **Not a one-line `timeoutMs`:** it is a render-thread request, which has no waiting Task for the coordinator to watch, so a 60 s filters request would hold a pool slot for up to a minute after Back — the thing #1043's 60 s is only safe without ([api.md → A long request already on a slot](architecture/api.md#a-long-request-already-on-a-slot)). **Design (agreed 2026-09-25):** for a long render-thread request, have the coordinator watch its own result node's `abandoned` field, which `abandonApiPromises()` already sets at teardown (the auto-abandon plugin injects it into `BaseGridView.onDestroy`). The in-flight stop then uses the same caller-gone rule as the queued skip (`apiRequestCallerGone`: the Task stopped, or the request was abandoned), and because the result node is per request it avoids the shared-subscription problem a Task watch has. Then give `/Items/Filters` a `timeoutMs`. Check what the user sees on a failed filter load first.

#### Find which screens other than the library grid outgrow the 10 s request limit on a slow server (from #1043). `[fid: screens-outgrowing-10s-request-limit]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

Measured over 10 s so far: a grid's first page (fixed in #1043), a quick play's whole-library read (17.2–35.0 s, measured 2026-09-29; fixed with #811 by `timeouts.QUICKPLAY_LIBRARY_MS`, which also covers the person and artist reads, not measured — no fixture has people — but the same whole-server query), `/Items/Filters` (followup above), live `PlaybackInfo` (followup under api), and every `PlaybackInfo` of some remote `.strm` items (10.4–12.0 s, measured 2026-10-12; pinned followup `strm-first-play-probe-outlasts-10s`). Drive each spinner screen against the slow 8,643-movie fixture and record which requests exceed `timeouts.HTTP_MS`. For each one that does, give it all three parts #1043 gave the grid page, or none: a limit sized from the measurement, staged "Still loading…" text before 10 s, and a stop on Back that frees its pool slot (ADR 0043). Don't raise `HTTP_MS` for the whole app: a server that never answers would take a minute to report on every screen. The staged text now comes from a screen wait (`screenWaits.begin`, ADR 0044), so a screen gets it by opening one rather than running its own timer.

#### A genre view's rows may still give up at 10 s on a slow server — unmeasured (from #1043). `[fid: genre-rows-10s-timeout-unmeasured]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

`LoadItemsTask2` fetches each genre row's sample items through `apiPipeline` with the default limit, inside one run budget (`timeouts.PIPELINE_RUN_MS`), so #1043's longer limit for a grid page does not reach them. The slow 8,643-movie fixture cannot show it: its movies have no metadata, so it has no genres (checked 2026-09-25). **First** measure the sample fetches against a slow server whose library has genres. **If they exceed `timeouts.HTTP_MS`:** give them a `timeoutMs` and pass `apiPipelineBegin` a larger `budgetMs`; the one-watch-per-Task stop #1043 added stops all of them on Back ([api.md → A long request already on a slot](architecture/api.md#a-long-request-already-on-a-slot)).

#### Move the remaining content loads onto screen waits (`screenWaits.begin` / `finish`, [ADR 0044](adr/0044-spinner-by-kind-of-wait.md)). `[fid: content-loads-onto-screen-waits]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

The library grid first: #1043 gave it its own stage timer (`BaseGridView.startSlowLoadText()`) and its own teardown stop (`m.ownsSpinner`), so delete both and open a named wait for the first page, so one rule shows the text and the screen's lifecycle ends it. Then `ItemDetails` (metadata, and the silent refresh on return, which today ends Play's spinner) and the TV guide, which block the remote and hide the screen today, so each needs a Back-mid-load check, and a longer request limit before the 30 s stage can ever show. The TV guide (`Schedule`) is a panel nested inside the grid view, not a routed screen: it passes its host screen to `screenWaits` rather than `m.top`, so decide how it gets that reference. Each move gets a case in an RTA spec the way `slow-search.spec.js` covers search.

#### Build the remaining app waits and loading kinds (ADR 0044, ADR 0046), then gate them. `[fid: remaining-app-waits-and-loading-kinds]` `[captured 2026-09-27]`

Playback start is built: a `PLAYBACK_START` app wait, one Promise per start (`appWaits`, `QueueManager.beginPlaybackStart`), kept by `AppWaitHost`, with a time limit that fails and logs one nobody ended; while it hides the screen it was pressed on it holds the remote focus, and Back cancels it. The sign-in steps are built too (PR #1238): a `SESSION` app wait opened at the press (`signInStep.press`) for a server connect, a user pick, a password or Quick Connect sign-in and a server switch; Back cancels it until `appWaits.commit`, and `loginRouter` drops the late answer. ADR 0053 replaces ADR 0044's "session change blocks input": Back cancels every network wait, and only local commits ignore it. The in-screen actions are built too (blocking-spinners phase C): details Refresh is an `ACTION` app wait (`ItemDetails.startRefresh`), the photo launches open a playback start that `PhotoDetails` takes, and an audio seek that loads a new stream is a `BUFFERING` screen wait on the player (`AudioPlayerView.beginSeekWait`).

Remaining, all still on the plain blocking `startLoadingSpinner()`, where extra presses reach the hidden screen and Back reaches it only by accident:

- The boot fallback font download (`main.bs` `initializeFallbackFont`): Back shows the exit confirmation, since there is no previous screen. After a sign-in, the committed `SESSION` wait covers it for now, so Back is ignored there.
- Sign-out, change user and change server (`main.bs` `handleMenuAction`): local commits, so a committed wait with no cancel.
- Then retire `clearSpinner` on `JRScene.routerNavigate`, which stays only for the cold-start boot spinner.

A render-thread `fetchAsync` wait may be bound to its promise so it ends on success, failure, timeout or abandon (check the pool's settle guarantee first). Once no call site uses bare `startLoadingSpinner()`, add a lint against it. Details open and the TV guide are `content-loads-onto-screen-waits`.

#### Spinner stops that end someone else's wait, found by the ADR 0044 survey (2026-09-25; read from code, the first two checked against it, none run on a device). `[fid: spinner-stops-ending-others-waits]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

Each goes away when its call site moves onto a named wait, so fix it then, not separately. (1) `BaseGridView.onGridLoadFailed()` calls `stopGridSpinner()` before it checks for the first page. It can no longer end a playback launch started from that grid, because a playback start is now an app wait the plain spinner's stop does not reach (ADR 0046; read from code, not run), but it still ends whatever plain spinner is up. (2) Closed by the TV guide's move to a screen wait: `stopGridSpinner()` right after `showTVGuide()` ended the guide's spinner (on a Stick 4K the guide's first load showed none, 2026-10-22), and it cannot reach a screen wait. (3) `JRScene.resolveDeepLink()` never writes `loadingText`, so a cast arriving right after login shows the fallback-font download text. (4) Closed by #1242: the connect now runs under a session app wait that this spinner stop does not end, and the scan no longer takes the focus back from it. (5) Not reproduced (probed 2026-10-22 on a Stick 4K): `isRemoteDisabled` read false on `UserSelect`, `LoginScene` and `SetServerScreen` at a cold start and after Change User, Sign Out and Change Server. Back gives no exit dialog there by design: it scans again on `SetServerScreen` and changes server on `UserSelect`.

#### Per-item `transformBaseItem` without a server version still crosses to the render thread per item outside the grid (from the grid page-cost fix). `[fid: transform-base-item-per-item-crossing]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

Without a version, `transformBaseItem` reads `m.global.server.version` itself — two rendezvous per item. Once `LoadItemsTask2` passed the version it had already read, building a grid page on the 512 MB Stick went from 1.5–3.3 s to 0.22–0.32 s while scrolling (measured 2026-09-25, [`threading.md`](architecture/threading.md#measured-findings)). The same loop shape remains in Home `LoadItemsTask` and `source/api/items.bs`. `SearchTask` was fixed in #1216: search settled in 1040 ms instead of 1239 ms on a Stick 4K and in 1140 ms instead of 1314 ms on a 512 MB Stick (50 per read, query "a", median of 5 cold runs, measured 2026-10-18). **Fix:** read the version once and pass it (or use `transformBaseItemArray` where no per-item fix-up follows), measured before and after. **Then gate it:** a BSC rule flagging `transformBaseItem` inside a loop with no version argument, the sites not yet converted on a pending list like `no-same-node-relaunch`'s `PENDING_MIGRATIONS`. This is the third instance of the class (`LoadScheduleTask` and `LoadChannelsTask` in the TV guide, #991, then the grid), so a gate beats review.

#### Refresh on a details screen makes an in-flight Task print `Rendezvous aborted` — which Task, and does it matter? (from #1047). `[fid: details-refresh-rendezvous-aborted]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

`BRIGHTSCRIPT: ERROR: roSGNode.<lookup>: Rendezvous aborted` 3–13 ms after the press, measured 2026-09-25 on a Stick 4K against demo 12.1.0: 1 of 6 `main` trials (`apiPipeline.brs(187)`), 2 of 6 branch trials (`LogMixin.brs(113)`); no backtrace, the screen right every time. Likely the extras run STOPped by `refreshExtrasData` mid node read — unconfirmed. Identify the Task, then decide whether a STOP landing mid-rendezvous needs handling.

#### Reuse a live channel's open stream on retry and restart, as jellyfin-web does (deferred from the fix that closes a live stream that never played). `[fid: live-channel-reuse-open-stream]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

JellyRock asks `PlaybackInfo` again with no `LiveStreamId` on every live retry (direct play failing over to transcode, `retryPlayback`) and every restart (`PlayerHostView.restartLiveChannel()`), so the server opens or joins the stream again; the close fix releases what each attempt leaves behind but still reopens it. jellyfin-web sends the current `LiveStreamId` back instead (`changeStream` in its playback manager), and the server's `MediaInfoHelper` then looks the open stream up (`GetLiveStream`) rather than opening another — the same on 10.7.7 and 12.1. What it would buy: no reopen per retry (cheap for an `M3U` source, whose probe the server keeps per channel — 0.002 s on 10.11, measured 2026-09-26 — but unmeasured for a tuner-backed source, which may take seconds to re-tune). **Measure before building:** a restart reuses a stream the server may already consider dead (the feed that ended is why the channel is restarting), so check what the server hands back for a reused id after its upstream ended, on 10.11 and 12.1. It does not replace the close: a give-up, or Back before the first frame, still needs its one release, and a never-started stream's stop cannot be that release on every server version ([`playback.md`](architecture/playback.md#reportplayback--server-side-reporting)).

#### Does a live Program ever play without its `LiveStreamId` on the playback reports? (found with the fix that closes a live stream that never played). `[fid: live-program-missing-livestreamid-reports]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

`LoadVideoContentTask` treats `program` as live (it plays the program's channel and sets `transcodeParams`), but `VideoPlayerView.isLiveTvPlayback()` checks the queue item's type for `tvchannel` only, and that check is what adds `MediaSourceId` / `LiveStreamId` to every `start`, progress and stop report and gates the close by id (`endClosesLiveStream()`). If a Program can reach the player with type `program`, its live stream is never linked to the session, so neither its stop nor the close releases it. **The details screen does not:** `ItemDetails.onWatchChannelButtonPressed()` queues a Program as its channel (`TvChannel`), read from code 2026-09-26. Still to check: the guide's watch path (`BaseGridView.onTVGuideWatchChannel()` passes the guide's channel node, so likely a channel too) and a cast or deep link naming a Program id; if one queues a `program` item, widen the check and measure the server's consumer count before and after.

#### The Favorites tab shows nothing at all when the user has no favorites. `[fid: favorites-tab-empty-no-favorites]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

`FavoritesRows.onFavoritesLoaded` / `onFavoritePeopleLoaded` remove every row whose answered list is empty, so an account with no favorites gets a blank tab with no message — no row, no text, no hint how to add one. Since the Favorites failure fix a FAILED load shows a "Couldn't load" tile instead, so empty and failed no longer look alike; this is the remaining UX gap, a separate feature rather than part of that fix. Fix shape: an empty-state message when both loads answered and no row is left (compare the library grid's "No Items" state).

#### Measure what dead-source live opens do to the API pool (from ADR 0045). `[fid: dead-source-live-opens-api-pool]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

A live `PlaybackInfo` now waits up to `timeouts.LIVE_OPEN_MS` (120 s) and keeps its pool slot after Back, because the pool never stops a POST and the answer must be heard to close the stream. Predicted from the code, not measured: a few opens of a source that never answers (the server gives up at ~100 s) or answers with no data (the full 120 s) inside two minutes leave every slot busy, so Home, the guide, details and the next channel's own open all wait for one. **Measure first:** on the 512 MB Stick, a temporary `M3U` tuner pointing at a stalled source, several opens each left with Back, then time a Home load against a baseline. If it stalls, cap how many live opens the pool runs at once so reads keep free slots (the next channel's open would then wait behind a stalled one, so weigh that too).

#### Quick play of a Collections library still lists up to 500 box sets and picks one on the device (`QuickPlayTask.doCollectionFolder`, `qp_collectionBoxsets`); playlists and Live TV now ask the server for one at random (`sortBy=Random&limit=1`, checked 2026-09-30 on 12.1.0: 2 playlists split 5/5 over 10 calls, 10 distinct of 393 channels). Give box sets the same request after checking it on a 10.x server (from #1076). `[fid: quick-play-collections-500-box-sets]` `[captured 2026-09-30]`

#### Quick play of a folder holding several series still asks once per series (`QuickPlayTask.doMultipleSeries`, from `doFolder`), each with the default request limit and one after another, so a slow server or a folder of many series can outlast the playback start's own limit. The TV library stopped doing this in #1076: one request for the folder's watched episodes (`quickplayLibrary.watchedEpisodesQuery`) would replace the loop; check it against a genre and a studio folder first, which filter by id rather than by parent. `[fid: quick-play-multiple-series-asks-each]` `[captured 2026-09-30]`

#### Retry a direct play that fails before its first frame as a transcode `[fid: direct-play-failure-retry-as-transcode]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

(safety net, deferred from the TrueHD/DTS fix). `VideoPlayerView` already has the path, `retryPlayback(false, true)` when the stream has not reported play and `isTranscodeAvailable` is set, but that flag is only set for the `h264`/`hevc` level override in `LoadVideoContentTask` and for live streams (`applyLiveDirectPlayFallback`). So an ordinary direct play the profile over-claims goes straight to the error dialog: on 2026-10-01 a DTS-HD MA + `DTS:X` 7.1 track on a Roku Ultra `4850X` failed with `-3` with `isTranscodeAvailable = false`. Arming it for every direct play would recover any hardware that says yes and then fails; it would not catch a hang, which is how TrueHD failed. It changes error behavior app-wide, so it needs its own device check.

#### Washed-out player controls after scrubbing a Dolby Vision title `[fid: dolby-vision-washed-out-after-scrub]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

(seen 2026-10-01 on a Roku Ultra `4850X`, on a movie transcoded for Dolby Vision): after a fast-forward and two spinners, the control buttons and text draw much lighter than before the seek. The player code sets no OSD opacity or color after init, so the likely cause is how Roku or the TV composites UI over HDR or Dolby Vision video after the stream re-buffers, which the app may not be able to change. **Next step:** scrub an HDR or Dolby Vision title in another app (official Jellyfin, Netflix) and open its controls; if it washes out there too, document it as a platform limitation, and if only JellyRock does it, dig.

#### AV1 and VP9 are not transcode video targets, so converting such a file's audio re-encodes its video too. `[fid: av1-not-transcode-target]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

`getTranscodingProfiles` offers one HLS video profile, `ts`, and lists the video codecs `CanDecodeVideo` accepts in `ts`. AV1 is not among them, and Jellyfin carries AV1 in HLS only in `fMP4` segments, which Roku plays with no audio (decision `hls-transcodes-ts-only`). VP9 answered false in `ts` and in `mp4`, and true only in `mkv` (measured 2026-11-04 on a Streaming Stick 4K and a Roku Ultra `4850X`). So when the server must convert an AV1 or VP9 file's audio, it re-encodes the video too. Measured 2026-10-01: switching a 4K AV1 Dolby Vision movie to its DTS 7.1 track reloads as a full 4K re-encode, 19 s to the first segment on the home server, then about 4.1 s per 6 s segment.

Ruled out 2026-11-04: a progressive `mkv` transcode profile (`Protocol: http`) to copy the video. It is one continuous stream with no segments, so a seek would restart the transcode, and seeking has to keep working. Offered to every play, a second profile would also win plays on audio fit, which is the cause `hls-transcodes-ts-only` fixed. **Next step:** re-check only if Roku's media spec gains muxed CMAF, or Jellyfin serves `fMP4` HLS with audio in a separate track. Either would open AV1; VP9 also needs Roku to decode it in `mp4`.

#### `activeRoutedView`'s observer fires many times per navigation although the field does not always notify, and nobody knows why. `[fid: active-routed-view-observer-refires]` `[captured 2026-10-09]`

`JRScene.onActiveRoutedViewChanged()` ran 23 to 46 times for one navigation (measured 2026-10-07 on a Streaming Stick 4K, while building PR #1137). The field is declared `addField("activeRoutedView", "node", false)` in `source/utils/globals.bs`, so a write of the same node should not notify, and only `JRScreen.onViewOpen()`, `JRScreen.onViewResume()` and `JRScene.resetRouter()` write it. PR #1137 made the handler return early when the node has not changed, so nothing breaks, but the cause is unknown and may mean something else fires too often too.

**Fix shape:** probe on device which writes or events reach the observer (log `m.global.activeRoutedView` and the caller per fire), then record the cause in `docs/architecture/navigation.md` beside the measured count, and fix it if it is a real excess.

#### A server-allowed live channel in a non-manifest container (mp4, mkv, ism) still buffers when the TV cannot reach its URL `[fid: live-non-manifest-unreachable-url-buffers]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

The device check before an upstream direct play (`LoadVideoContentTask.upstreamManifestAnswers()`) runs only for HLS and DASH manifests: `getUpstreamManifestBlocker()` returns `NOT_MANIFEST` for anything else and the server's answer stands. The device profile still allows direct play of `mp4`, `mkv` and `ism` (`supportedContainers` in `GetDirectPlayProfiles`), so a live channel in one of those containers over HTTP that the TV cannot reach is still handed to the Video node, which buffers instead of failing, and the transcode retry does not run in time. MPEG-TS is unaffected: it is left out of the direct-play profiles, so the server always remuxes it.

This is the non-manifest half of the closed followup `live-channel-buffers-forever-unreachable-url`, found in review of the manifest direct-play fix (decision `manifest-upstream-direct-play`). Not done there because it needs a different probe: a GET of an endless progressive stream never completes, so the check would need a ranged or HEAD request, or to read only the first bytes. Closes when an unreachable server-allowed non-manifest live URL falls back to the server's stream, measured on a device.

#### A mixed queue leaves a stopped `AudioPlayerView` under the player `[fid: mixed-queue-stopped-audio-view]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

In a mixed audio and video queue, the non-audio branches of `AudioPlayerView` (`advanceToNextItem`, `previousClicked`, `nextClicked`) stop the audio and call `playQueue`, which plain-navigates to the play route. A covered view is suspended, not closed (the route comment in `JRScene.initRouter`), so `/audio` stays under the player; `PlayerHostView.exitPlayback` (`sgrouter.goBack()`) then resumes a stopped `AudioPlayerView`, which has no `onScreenShown`. It broke in #677: before it, `previousClicked` and `nextClicked` called `sceneManager.callFunc("clearPreviousScene")` before `playQueue`. Found by the batch 9 comment pass.

Recommended fix (`close`): one `AudioPlayerView` helper for the three branches that stops, moves the queue, `goBack()`s to close `/audio`, then calls `playQueue` once that navigation settles, with an RTA spec driving a mixed queue on a device first (it must fail before the fix). Alternatives: `scene` (`JRScene.onPlaybackLaunchRequested` goes back first when the active view is `AudioPlayerView` and the target is a play route); `exit` (an `onScreenShown` that leaves when resumed with nothing playing; hides the symptom, with a flash).

Not checked: what the screen actually shows on a device; whether `PlayerHostView` can play an audio item that comes after the video in the queue; how often users build mixed playlists.

Not built now: it needs a new RTA spec and a device run, outside the comment pass.

#### The app cannot select among audio tracks muxed into one HLS variant: Roku lists none of them `[fid: muxed-hls-audio-tracks-unselectable]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

On an HLS stream that carries several audio tracks in one variant, Roku lists none of them (`availableAudioTracks` empty, `currentAudioTrack` ""), so the app cannot play the viewer's pick. `getRokuAudioTrackPosition()` then falls back to index + 1 and writes a track id that does not exist. Playback Info now says "Playing track: Unknown" for this case (commit `25697e39`), but the audio menu still offers every track and choosing one changes nothing audible.

Evidence (2026-10-12, Stick 4K, 12.1): the muxed fixture `vod2a` gave an empty track list for 20 s, with and without a pick; the same clip with two `#EXT-X-MEDIA` renditions (`vod2r`) listed both, and French played when picked (`currentAudioTrack` "2"). A direct-played MKV with two audio tracks lists them.

Options weighed: reload through the server's stream when Roku lists no tracks and the pick differs from what plays (the pick then plays, but the list is known only after playback starts, so it restarts the play and loads the server); leave it as a known limitation.

Not checked: which track is audible on the muxed stream; how common muxed multi-audio HLS is in real `.strm` or Live TV provider sources (no user report); Live TV TS channels.

Not done now: no real source is known to use this layout. Act when a user report or a real source shows it.

#### An external bitmap subtitle track may take the custom caption path and draw nothing `[fid: external-bitmap-subtitle-custom-path]` `[captured 2026-10-13]` `[prompt: /snag An external bitmap subtitle track may take the custom caption path and draw nothing (VideoPlayerView keys custom subtitles on IsExternal alone)]`

`VideoPlayerView`'s custom-subtitle decision returns `selectedSubtitle.IsExternal` and never checks the codec, so an external `.sub` or `.sup` track could be sent to the text renderer. Related but separate: `custom-subtitles-depend-on-vtt-first`. Read in the code 2026-10-13, not reproduced: whether Jellyfin marks such a track external needs a device and a library with one. A `/snag` candidate.

#### `ExtrasRowList.onDestroy` keeps `m.top.content`, so its cells may keep a retain cycle `[fid: extras-rowlist-keeps-content-on-destroy]` `[captured 2026-10-13]` `[prompt: /snag ExtrasRowList.onDestroy does not drop m.top.content, so its BrowseRowItem cells may keep the retain cycle ADR 0029 breaks elsewhere]`

`ExtrasRowList.onDestroy` clears its task, slot and result state but never sets `m.top.content = invalid`, unlike `BaseGridView` and `SearchScreen`. Its `BrowseRowItem` cells may then keep the cycle ADR 0029 breaks. Tech-debt `observe-without-on-destroy-jrscreen-only` notes the gap but proposes no fix. The `leaks.spec.js` census on a device would settle it. A `/snag` candidate.

#### Remove the unused `transcodeReasons` field from `VideoPlayerView` `[fid: videoplayer-transcodereasons-unused]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

`VideoPlayerView.xml` declares `transcodeReasons`, and `init()` sets it to `[]`; nothing reads it (`LoadVideoContentTask` uses its own `transcodeReasons` on an AA). `docs/architecture/playback.md` already says so. Remove the field and the line, and update that doc. Checked 2026-10-13.

#### The `livetv` placeholder is built from the outlined `liveTV.svg`, against fill Rule 4 `[fid: livetv-placeholder-outlined-source]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

`resources/placeholders/placeholders.json` builds the `livetv` placeholder from `liveTV.svg`, the outlined (fill 0) icon source, while every other placeholder uses a filled source, as fill Rule 4 in `resources/icons/README.md` asks. The README now records it as the one exception (docs-voice Charter review, 2026-10-13); nothing recorded whether it was chosen. Close it by fetching a filled variant (`npm run icons:add -- live_tv --as liveTV_filled --filled`), pointing the `livetv` entry at it and running `npm run icons:build`, then checking the placeholder on a device; or record why it stays outlined and keep the exception in the README.

#### The scene toast is one line capped at 800 pixels, so a long message is cut off, in English already and in every longer translation `[fid: toast-one-line-clips-long-messages]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

The scene toast is one line: `Toast.bs` sizes it to its text but clamps it at `TOAST_MAX_WIDTH` (800 pixels), which leaves about 696 pixels for text once the padding and icon are taken out. A longer message is cut off, and by length that already happens in English: `MessageContentOnUnknownServer` ("This content is on a Jellyfin server you haven't added to this device.", shown by `replayRoute.bs` through `displayToast`) and `MessageSubtitleDownloadFailed` (about 130 characters) are both far past it (estimated from the 654 pixels measured for a 39-character string; not measured themselves). A translation runs longer than English, so every toast in a long language is exposed. Measured 2026-10-13 on a Roku Ultra (Roku OS 15.3.4) with the scene's `testToast` field: the longest quick play string, "Couldn't load the slideshow. Try again.", lays out at 654 pixels; a German-length version of it lays out at the 800 pixels cap. The one-line shape dates from `eab29b03f` ("restyle Toast with icon, auto-width, and action safe anchoring").

Options, raised while building the quick play failure toasts (decision `quick-play-nothing-toast`): `wrap` lets the toast grow to two or three lines past the cap (layout work in `Toast.bs`, a device check over the player and the grids); `shorten` rewrites the long English messages to fit one line (small, but translations can still overflow); or both. Recommended: decide `wrap` vs `shorten` first, then fix it in its own change.

Not checked: the two English messages' widths on screen (estimated from length only); whether the clipped text ends in an ellipsis or is cut mid-word (Roku `Label` truncation was not looked at on screen); which other toasts overflow in English beyond the two named.

#### No toast is spoken by the screen reader: Toast never narrates and never takes focus `[fid: toast-not-spoken-screen-reader]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

Found 2026-10-13 while adding the search failure announcement (`feat/search-failure-state`, #1199). Nothing in `Toast.bs` calls `roAudioGuide`, and a toast never takes focus, so every `displayToast` message reaches a screen-reader user as nothing: quick play's "Couldn't load that. Try again.", the grid's "couldn't load more", Search's "Some results couldn't load." and the rest of the 33 call sites.

**Root cause:** for a group the app draws, the screen reader speaks only the focused element; a dialog's title and message were never spoken until the app spoke them itself (measured 2026-08-01, recorded in `source/utils/dialogNarration.bs`). `Toast` is a group that never holds focus. Roku's text-to-speech table (`rokudev/dev-doc`, `text-to-speech.md`) says a Label "speaks `text` field", but the dialog measurement shows an unfocused label is not spoken. Narration was never there: the toast arrived in `46150743` and was restyled in `eab29b03`.

**Options:**

- `speak` (recommended): `Toast.onShouldShow` queues the message with `narrateStatus`, the helper Search's announcement and the grid's "Loading more" use. One file, about two lines; checked by ear. Covers all 33 call sites and every new one. A toast that follows a focus move is spoken after that move's speech; a caller that also speaks its own message would be heard twice (none does today: Search's announcement leaves its partial toast out for this reason).
- `per-caller`: each important caller speaks its own message beside `displayToast`. 33 sites, and each new toast is another chance to forget; it drifts.

**Not checked:** that a toast is silent when heard on a device; whether speech from inside `Toast` would be cut off by the platform's own focus speech when a toast and a focus move happen together.

#### Home's texture window has five unmeasured tails: edge churn, a buffer-row cell with no texture, scroll-direction bias, loads per unique item, and pop-in on Home `[fid: home-texture-window-tails]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

The `task-thread-budget` project closed on 2026-10-13 with these left open. Two of them are its unmet success criteria. The rest are phase C's leftovers from the windowing audit (#919), which found no defect but measured only the window's geometry:

- Churn at the window's edge: `reloads`, `wipesReload`, `bindsRedundant` while scrolling across the boundary.
- The one cell of 8 with no texture on a buffer row (suspected cause: an item with no artwork, never checked).
- Whether the window should load further ahead in the scroll direction (weakened by the `focusXOffset` peek).
- Loads ÷ unique items traversed reaching 1.0 on Home. `ExtrasRowList` reads 1.05 after #840; never measured on Home.
- Pop-in near zero on Home. Measurable since #843 (`popIns`, read with `loadMsMax`), never shown on Home.

Why not now: each needs a pre-registered device campaign on the cell sweeps (`RTA_BENCH=1`). Closes when each is measured, and fixed where it shows a defect, on `JRRowItem` and `source/utils/textureManager.bs`.

#### Home binds about 16 more cells on some launches than others over an identical workload `[fid: home-binds-variance]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

Over an identical, settled Home workload, `binds` still spans about 16 cells from launch to launch (224 to 240 in one 2026-09-14 run where every census field was identical). A 2026-08-22 campaign of 40 launches pinned the itinerary, the items, the unloads and the row structure, so the harness is not the cause: the variance comes from the app. It was parked inside `task-thread-budget` and carried here when that project closed on 2026-10-13.

Why not now: it needs a device campaign tracing which cells `HomeRows` / `JRRowItem` bind beyond the visible set. Closes when the extra binds are explained, and removed if they turn out redundant.

#### Search rows stop at 50 items: load more as the viewer scrolls a row, so rows can reach 100 or more without building every item up front `[fid: search-row-paging]` `[captured 2026-10-18]`

Search reads each library type on its own (`searchOutcome.LIBRARY_TYPES`, #1216), so no type crowds another out, but each read stops at `SEARCH_READ_LIMIT` = 50, and so does each row. Raising it to 100 was built and measured on 2026-10-18 (median of 5 cold runs, `npm run measure -- --measurement screen-load --nav search --variant query`, per-type reads at 50 against 100): a Stick 4K on a 12.1 server went from 1561 to 2280 ms for "a" and from 1207 to 1726 ms for "the"; a 512 MB Streaming Stick on a 10.11 server went from 1396 to 1969 ms and from 1205 to 1601 ms. Timers in a throwaway build: 864 items for "a" on the Stick 4K at 100, about 2 ms each to build; on the 512 MB Stick the search ended when its two 100-item reads (episodes, people) were parsed and handed over. Each request also has a fixed device cost of about 20 ms, tracked in `api-pool-render-thread-crossings`.

A row shows only a few tiles at once, so building 100 per type up front pays for items the viewer may never scroll to. **Fix:** keep the first read per type small, and have `SearchRow` load the next page when focus nears the end of a row, up to 100 or more, keeping focus and scroll position still while items are appended. Needs RTA specs for the scroll and focus behavior. Not done in #1216 because it is new row machinery and that PR fixes the crowding. Closes when a search row can reach 100 items and a broad search settles no slower than at 50 per read, measured on a Stick 4K and a 512 MB Stick. Related: `lean-tile-node-cost` lowers the per-item cost itself.

#### Search: suggest completions of the word being typed, above the keyboard `[fid: search-word-completions]` `[captured 2026-10-18]`

Raised in manual testing of #1217 (2026-10-18). Like a phone's autocomplete, offer completions of the partial word, built from the library's own titles: typing "shre" offers "shrek". They would sit in the gap between the search bar and the keyboard (the bar ends at y=114, the keyboard's top row starts at y=186) and be reached with Up from the keyboard's top row. This is separate from the suggestion column, which lists whole items.

Needs its own design: where the words come from (the /Search/Hints names already fetched, or a dedicated query), how many fit, and how Up from the top row fits today's overlay focus loop (decision `search-overlay-horizontal-loop`). Not done in #1217: it is a new feature with its own layout and focus rules.

#### Upstream watch: sgRouter history-depth / `canGoBack()` API `[fid: sgrouter-can-go-back]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: @rokucommunity/sgrouter releases for a public history-depth or `canGoBack()` method (none in 0.1.3; pinned)

Last upstream version seen: 0.1.3 (checked 2026-06-23). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `sgrouter-can-go-back` row.

Where JellyRock stands: `sgrouter.goBack()` returns a bare `false` for THREE different reasons (a navigation is in flight, the history is at its root, or the previous entry's `nodeId` resolves to no view), and `JRScene`'s back arbiter must tell them apart to decide between swallowing the key and raising the Exit dialog. It infers: `isRouterNavigating()` reads `routerState.type` directly for the first, and treats what remains as "at root". A public `canGoBack()` / history-depth API would let it ask instead of infer (`m.__router_historyStack` is private, and the vendored copy is regenerated by ropm, so we did not patch it, on purpose). **Narrowed by [ADR 0029](adr/0029-destroy-routed-screens-on-pop.md) (2026-08-15):** the stale-`nodeId` case this row was originally filed against is created only by the `keepAlive` resume branch that reassigns `view.id` (`Router.brs` `addViewToStack`), and no route sets `keepAlive` any more, so that branch is unreachable and the router's `previousNodeIds` fallback is dead code in our configuration. No other path producing an unresolvable history entry was found. The inference therefore looks sound rather than merely lucky, which makes the remaining value of `canGoBack()` clarity rather than correctness. Close this entry if nothing exercises the third case

When it moves: if an official history-depth / `canGoBack()` API lands, bump the pin and gate the `JRScene` Exit confirmation on it to close the stale `nodeId` edge cleanly (replaces the navigation-in-progress mirror). Consider contributing the API upstream as a PR

#### When audiobooks are supported, the audio player must honor a resume position `[fid: audiobook-resume-in-audio-player]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

When audiobooks are supported, the audio player must honor a resume position, and an `AudioBook` details screen must offer Resume again. Neither is true today: `AudioBook` is in `itemTypeOrder.NO_RESUME` until the audio player can start where the book was left.

Why it waits: Jellyfin saves no position for an `Audio` item. `BaseItem.SupportsPositionTicksResume` is false and `Audio` does not override it, so `UserDataManager.UpdatePlayState` forces the saved position to 0 for every song, however long (read at v10.10.0 and v12.2). `AudioBook` overrides it to true, with the server's `MinAudiobookResume` and `MaxAudiobookResume` settings. JellyRock does not support audiobooks yet. An audiobook can still reach its details screen as an edge case: the Home Favorites row drops only `Book` (`LoadItemsTask`), so an audiobook marked as a favorite in another client shows there. Its Resume would have opened the audio player at the start, which is why the button is hidden for now.

What closes it, as part of audiobook support:

- `AudioPlayerView` reads the queue item's `startingPoint` (today nothing there reads it) and starts there, as the video loader sets `content.PlayStart`. Measure seeking on a transcoded audio stream first: the audio transcode is progressive HTTP, not HLS, and the server can also start a transcode at `StartTimeTicks`, so the two must not add up.
- Remove `AudioBook` from `itemTypeOrder.NO_RESUME`. Check on a real audiobook that Resume shows and goes through `launchQueueItemToPlay`, which routes audio to the audio player (decision `play-kind-single-table`).
- Decide whether a tap on a part-played audiobook tile resumes, as `quickplay.video` does for video (`quickplay.audio` sets no start).

#### Rotate through several backdrops for a focused My Media tile (the shuffle part of #1134) `[fid: library-tile-backdrop-rotation]` `[captured 2026-10-21]`

Issue #1134 asks for backdrops that change over time. `feat/library-backdrops` gives each My Media tile one backdrop from an item inside the library: `libraryBackdrop.query` asks for `Limit: 1`, and `HomeRows` caches that one URL per library for Home's lifetime. The Jellyfin web client takes up to 20 (`src/scripts/autoBackdrops.js`, `SortBy=IsFavoriteOrLiked,Random`) and rotates them. Not done now: rotating needs a timer on Home with a lifecycle to get right (pause off Home, stop on teardown), and a UX call on how often to change. Closes when a focused My Media tile cycles through several backdrops, or when we decide not to rotate.

#### The Roku FLAC seek defect behind #662 is unreported, so its workaround has no exit `[fid: roku-flac-seek-defect-unreported]` `[captured 2026-10-21]`

Roku can't finish a FLAC it starts at a non-zero position (a `seek` or a `PlayStart`) when the file's last frame is shorter than its frame size: it stalls in `playing` a moment before the end, or ends at once with "malformed data" (#662, [ADR 0052](../docs/adr/0052-flac-seek-server-stream.md)). JellyRock works around it by loading a FLAC stream the server starts at the seek target (`audioSeek.startAction()`, `AudioPlayerView.startAudioAt()`), with the `AudioPlayer` end guard as backstop. Nobody has told Roku, so no fix is tracked.
