Close it by reporting the defect to Roku (developer forum or partner channel) with the minimal reproduction: the same FLAC with and without its short last frame, seeking to 50% on Roku OS 15.3.4, where only the first freezes. Link the report here. When a Roku OS release fixes it, measure again and, if native FLAC seeks then finish, remove the server-stream path and decide whether the end guard stays.

Why not now: it needs an account and a public post, which are the operator's to make.

#### A server switch confirmed over a player can't be canceled with Back `[fid: server-switch-over-player-not-cancellable]` `[captured 2026-10-22]`

A cast naming another saved server can show the "Switch server?" prompt while a video plays: the `roInputEvent` branch in `main.bs` calls `onRuntimeDeepLink`, which reaches `promptServerSwitch` with no check of the active view (the remote-control socket's Play command takes the same path). On Switch, `performServerSwitch` opens the `SESSION` wait with the player as `launchedFrom`, and `AppWaitHost.begin` never hides a playback screen (the exception channel switches need). So the wait holds no keys: Back goes to the player while the switch goes on, and ADR 0053's "Back cancels a server switch" does not hold there. `main` behaved the same before PR #1238, so this is not a regression; found reading the code in that PR's review, not run on a device.

Before choosing a fix, measure on a device whether `opacity = 0` hides the video plane, and whether the player takes the focus back on its own events (the next episode starting, "still watching") during the probe (`ServerReachableTask`, up to 8 s). Options weighed in the review: hide the player for `SESSION` waits (keys held, Back returns to the playing video), or close the player first (loses the viewer's place when the server is unreachable). Closes when Back cancels a switch confirmed over a player, with an RTA case.

#### A server re-scan ending on a visible `SetServerScreen` takes the focus from where the viewer moved it `[fid: server-rescan-steals-visible-focus]` `[captured 2026-10-22]`

A server re-scan that ends while `SetServerScreen` is on screen moves the focus to the picker (or to the URL box when no server answered), from wherever the viewer moved it meanwhile. Back on the picker, then OK, puts the focus on Submit; when the scan ends the focus jumps back to the picker, so the next OK picks a row instead of submitting. With no servers, Up then Down lands on Submit and the scan's end moves the focus to the URL box, where OK opens the keyboard. `SetServerScreen.onScanForServersComplete` assumes it owns the focus whenever the screen does. #1242 fixed only the hidden-screen case (a connect's wait holding the focus). Read from code on 2026-10-22, not run on a device.

**Not fixed now:** the server select screen is due to be redone, so this belongs in that redesign. **Fix shape:** a scan moves the focus only when it is still where the scan started (the screen itself on first load, or the node Back or Up was pressed on), and the URL box outline follows wherever the focus actually goes.

#### The FLAC seek fallback (no audio transcoding) now ends the song about 1 s after the seek on the demo server `[fid: flac-seek-fallback-ends-early-demo]` `[captured 2026-10-23]` `[pinned]`

`audio-seek.spec.js`, case "without audio transcoding, a FLAC seek says so once and the song still ends", failed 3 of 3 on `main` (`34a621e3`) on 2026-10-23 on a Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4), 6 of 7 counting the phase C branch runs. Every time the song ended 1,084 to 1,643 ms after the seek. The same case passed at 01:33 UTC that day on `a92781b3`, and 5 of 5 when #1234 merged. The app code did not change between the pass and the failures (`git diff a92781b3 34a621e3` touches only `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/progress.md` and one unit spec), and the device OS is the same. The fallback path is `audioSeek.startAction()`: with no transcoding permission and a direct stream, a native seek (`AudioPlayerView.startAudioAt()`).

Root cause not confirmed. Lead: the demo server now reports Jellyfin 12.2.0, and Jellyfin 12.3 was published at 00:30 UTC that day, so the demo may have been redeployed. The run records do not store the server version.

Options: `measure` (recommended): run this one case against the local server matrix (12.0, 12.1, 12.2) with the debug console captured, find what changed, then fix. `loosen`: make the case accept what the fallback's toast promises (notice once, queue moves on) whether or not the rest plays; test only, and it would hide a real change for users.

Not checked: which server version the demo ran at 01:33; whether this case passes against a local 12.1 or 12.0 server; whether the early end is the "malformed data" end or something else (no debug console capture yet).

Why not now: found mid-way through blocking-spinners phase C; it needs its own device session.

Measured again 2026-10-31 on unmodified `main` on a Stick 4K: the case failed 4 of 6 runs, with the song ending 1 to 2 s after the seek, so it still fails on and off rather than every time. Which server version those runs used was not recorded.

#### The OSD gear offers no quality rows for Live TV until a channel reload is shown to release its stream once `[fid: live-tv-gear-quality-rows]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

The playback-settings gear on the OSD has no rows for a Live TV channel, so `setButtonStates()` drops it there (`playbackSettingsMenu.rows()` returns nothing for a `TvChannel`). A quality pick reloads the stream through `VideoPlayerView.reloadAtCurrentPosition()`, and on a channel that reload opens a new live stream. Nobody has checked that this path keeps exactly one release per stream (`releaseVideoLoad()`, `source/utils/liveStreamHandoff.bs`): a second release can end another viewer's shared stream, and a missing one leaves a tuner open. An audio-track change on a transcoded channel already takes the same path, so start there.

Closes when a device run shows a mid-playback reload of a channel releases the old stream exactly once (the server's open live streams before and after), and the gear's quality rows then show for a `TvChannel` with an RTA case. Deferred from the PR that added the gear (branch `feat/osd-settings-menu`), because the check needs a Live TV fixture and its own design pass.

#### `handleTransport`'s `playpause` still writes `control = "play"` to a node that is buffering `[fid: playpause-plays-buffering-node]` `[captured 2026-10-24]`

`VideoPlayerView.handleTransport` routes `play` and `resume` through `voiceTransport.playControl()` since commit `4f9c48cd`, which writes nothing to a buffering node. Its `playpause` branch was left as it was: any state that is not playing or paused still gets `control = "play"`. A device probe (Streaming Stick 4K, Roku OS 15.3.4, 2026-10-23) showed that a `control = "play"` written while a seek buffers leaves the node at `buffering` for good, so a remote or voice play/pause pressed during a seek's buffering likely wedges playback the same way. Not checked on a device.

Not done in that fix: it changes voice and remote behavior outside SyncPlay, and needs its own device probe. Closes with: `playpause` decides its control through `playControl()` (or a sibling helper with a spec), and a device probe sends `playpause` during a seek's buffering and sees the node reach `playing`.

#### The vendored socket client drops a message the server sends in fragments `[fid: websocket-fragmented-message-dropped]` `[captured 2026-10-24]`

A message the server splits into several frames is never posted. The last frame of a fragmented message is a continuation frame (opcode 0); `WebSocketClient._decode_frame` hands it to `_handle_frame` with that opcode after appending the earlier fragments, and `_handle_frame` acts only on close, ping, text and binary, so the reassembled message is dropped. Found on 2026-10-24 while writing `tests/source/unit/remotecontrol/WebSocketClient.spec.bs` (commit `4b725d92`): a fragmented-message case failed on the code before and after that commit, and was left out. Jellyfin sends each session message as one frame, so nothing hits it today.

Not done there: it changes how every inbound message is handed over and needs its own spec. Closes with: `_decode_frame` passes the first fragment's opcode for a final continuation frame, a spec case delivers a three-fragment text message intact, and the existing cases still pass.

#### Audit every size and drawn position outside the overhang against the 3 pixel grid `[fid: app-wide-3px-grid-audit]` `[captured 2026-10-25]`

The operator's rule (2026-10-25): every size and every drawn position is a multiple of 3, the position counted as the sum of the translations above it, because the app lays out at 1080p (`ui_resolutions=fhd`) and a 720p device scales by two thirds. The overhang now meets it, gated by `JROverhangLayout.spec.bs` and measured on device with `tests/rta/lib/ink.js`; the dialogs already round onto it (`snapToGrid` in `source/utils/pixelGrid.bs`). The overhang's glyphs and every `IconButton` icon are on it too (63 pixel canvases in a 63 pixel load box). Icons still off it: the canvases at 16, 24, 35, 36 and 48 pixels, and the 96 pixel `hd`, `speed` and `versions`, whose HD copies are 64. One case to check first: a `TextButton` box's height is the font size × 0.7 plus twice its padding, which need not be a multiple of 3 (`shouldSnapToGrid` covers widths only), and the clock in the video player keeps its 64 pixel label boxes.

Not done now: it touches every screen, so it needs its own captures at 720p. What closes it: a pass over each screen's static layout (constants, XML translations and sizes, computed boxes) and every icon canvas against the grid, fixing or recording each exception in `docs/architecture/design-system.md`, with 720p captures on the low-end Stick before and after.

#### Browse while music plays: bring the overhang cluster back to playback screens `[fid: browse-while-music-plays]` `[captured 2026-10-26]`

The overhang cluster is hidden on playback screens (`JRScene.registerOverhangData`, `appWaits.isPlaybackScreen`) because the app has no now-playing control. Leaving the audio player through search or Settings left the music playing with no screen to stop it from: measured on a Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4, 2026-10-25), a theme reload from Settings opened over the audio player landed on Home with the song still playing.

What closes it: a now-playing control outside the player that returns to it; a rule for starting other media while a player is suspended under another screen (untested: two `AudioPlayerView`s observing the shared `m.global.audioPlayer`); then the cluster on playback screens again. Not now: it needs its own design.

#### `UserSelect` applies 'splash disabled' before the branding answer arrives `[fid: splashscreen-flag-reads-false-unset]` `[captured 2026-10-29]`

`JellyfinServer.isSplashscreenEnabled` (`components/data/jellyfin/JellyfinServer.xml`) is commented "invalid = not loaded yet", but it is `type="boolean"` with no value, so an unset field reads `false`. `UserSelect.loadSplashscreen()` therefore always takes its `isValid()` "already cached" branch and applies "splash disabled" (clearing the backdrop) before the branding fetch delivers the real value. Found while fixing the screenshot capture, whose `settleBackdrop()` reads the server's `/Branding/Configuration` for this reason; the app log shows `Splashscreen disabled - using default background` before every `Branding config cached successfully`.

Not checked: whether the flash of the default background is visible on a device. Check that first; fix by tracking "fetched" separately (an `isSplashscreenLoaded` field) or declaring the field so it starts invalid. Not done now: it needs a device check and an app change outside the screenshot work.

#### Recently Added in a library with no collection type follows the episode image setting `[fid: recently-added-follows-episode-image-setting]` `[captured 2026-10-31]`

`HomeRows` uses `JRRowItem`, so an episode in a Recently Added row for a library with no collection type follows the **Episode Images Next Up** setting. The setting's text names only Next Up and Continue Watching, so what it covers and what it says differ. Surfaced while fixing the Favorites cell in #1290.

TV libraries no longer have this gap: their Recently Added rows use a portrait slot, where an episode shows a parent poster and ignores the setting (decision `portrait-episode-shows-parent-poster`). A library with no collection type keeps the wide slot, so decide whether it should follow TV libraries too.

Not fixed there because telling each cell its row adds work per cell in Home's hot path, and rewording is a separate product choice. Closes when the row stops following the setting, or the setting's text names it.

#### The trick-play carousel's seek flag can never reset once an OK sets it `[fid: trickplay-carousel-flag-never-resets]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

`VideoPlayerView.onPositionChanged` resets `m.carouselHiddenForSeek` inside a branch that runs only while that flag is false, so once an OK on the trick-play bar sets it, it stays set. Its comment expects the reset when the bar closes. The same branch resets `m.trickSeekTarget`, so that value also stays from the last scrub until the bar's text next changes. Read from the code in the branch review (2026-11-01), present since `fc1d48fab`; the effect on screen is unmeasured. The carousel may stop hiding when a later scrub is canceled, since only the key handler then drives it. Not fixed now: it needs a device run to see what the viewer gets, and it is older than the work that found it. Closes with that run and a reset outside the guard, or a decision to drop it.

#### Under Repeat One, the still-watching check counts a Cinema Mode intro's end as a new video `[fid: still-watching-counts-intro-under-repeat-one]` `[captured 2026-11-03]`

`PlayerHostView.onPlayerStateChange()` asks `shouldAskStillWatching()` about the target item's `followsIntro` before it calls `QueueManager.advanceTo()`. Under Repeat One, a Cinema Mode intro's end targets the intro's own slot, which carries no tag, so the move into the episode counts as a new unattended video. The function's comment says that move "neither counts nor asks".

It shows today, one video early. The Repeat One pick is a gear press during the intro, so `stillWatchingOnAdvance` resets the count at the intro's end, but it also moves `itemStartedAt` to the episode's start. The press then predates the episode, so the episode's end counts as unattended: with the short preset (2 videos) the prompt comes after the second replay instead of the third. It needs Cinema Mode, a served intro, Repeat One picked during it and the prompt on. Not a regression: `main` does the same. A repeat mode set without a key press (the SyncPlay group half, reading the group's `RepeatMode`) would add one more count.

Close it by deciding "leaving an intro" from the item that ended rather than from the target's tag, with a unit case for Repeat One at an intro's end. Found reviewing the intro-slot fix that followed #1308.

#### Video repeat can't be set by voice loop or remote `SetRepeatMode` (#1334) `[fid: video-repeat-voice-loop-remote]` `[captured 2026-11-07]`

Tracked publicly as #1334. Voice "loop" while a video plays answers "That is not available": `VideoPlayerView.handleTransport()` returns `error.generic` for `shuffle` and `loop`, under a comment from the voice transport commit `e9f5887f4` (2026-05-06) saying the video player has no loop UI. #1308 (`41f14e62`) made that untrue but wired repeat only into the OSD gear, the same root cause PR #1333 fixed for voice and remote Next and Previous. On the remote side, `getSupportedRemoteCommands()` doesn't advertise `SetRepeatMode`. The `queue-aware-multi-item-casting` followup parks it under multi-item casting, but since #1308 the queue holds the server's own repeat words, so it can be built on its own.

Options: `cycle` (recommended): voice `loop` cycles Off, All, One through a unit-tested `queueRepeat` helper, then `setRepeatMode`, `refreshFromQueue` and a toast naming the mode. It answers `error.generic` on Live TV and in a SyncPlay group, and `voice-control.md` gets updated. `comment`: only correct the stale comment; the voice command still fails. Not done now because it is a small feature with open UX choices, separate from #1333.

Not checked: Roku's exact wording for a "loop" request; whether a toast should confirm the new mode; how a SyncPlay group should take a repeat change.

#### No audit of what the app does, and shows, for a user the server's access policy restricts `[fid: user-access-policy-ux-audit]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

The app loads the user's access policy (`SessionDataTransformer` maps `EnableVideoPlaybackTranscoding`, `EnablePlaybackRemuxing` and more onto `m.global.user.policy`), but no playback code reads most of it. So every fallback that asks the server for a transcode can ask for one the policy forbids: the retry that forces a transcode after a failed direct play, and since PR #1318 the re-encode retry after a `buffer:loop:` overflow (`chooseErrorRetry()`, `capH264Resolution()`, whose second `PlaybackInfo` request since PR #1323 also runs on a first request). A restricted user then sees whatever error the refused request yields, not one that says why. What the server actually answers in that case was not checked.

Found in review of PR #1318. The user ruled out a one-off policy check on the re-encode retry: the gap needs a proper audit of user access and its UX instead. Close it with an audit that lists each policy field the server enforces, which app features and fallbacks each one affects, what the server answers when a request crosses it (measured on a server with the policy off), and what the user should see, then file the fixes it finds.

#### File an enhancement issue for an on-screen log overlay `[fid: log-screen-overlay-issue]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

**File an enhancement issue for an on-screen log overlay in debug builds.** The old roku-log `ScreenTransport` drew the latest lines over the UI; it was deleted in #986 because nothing used it (its code is in git history, in roku-log 0.11.1's `src/components/ScreenTransport.bs`). The maintainer sees value in it beyond the console: RTA screenshots would show the log beside the screen. Draft the issue and show it before filing.

#### A live channel beyond the H.264 ceiling buffers forever, from its upstream link or the server's stream `[fid: live-4k-h264-no-1080p-check]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

A live channel beyond the H.264 ceiling never plays when the device plays its upstream link. Measured 2026-11-05 on a Roku Ultra with a 4K output mode, against a live `M3U` channel serving H.264 High@5.1 at `3840x2160` (the local test servers' HLS fixture). The load found no blocker (`getUpstreamManifestBlocker` NONE, `deviceLoadsUpstream` true) and direct-played the upstream HLS link, so the server never transcoded it. That held on a server that does not encode HEVC and on 12.4 with `NVENC` and HEVC encoding on alike. The player then sat in `buffer` at position 0 for the 45 s watched, and the transcode fallback (`isTranscodeAvailable`) never ran, because nothing failed.

The same channel has a second path to the same hang, found by reading the code and not yet measured: when the load plays the server's stream instead (a blocker such as the user policy's remote-source transcoding, or a link the device cannot load), the server re-encodes the video to H.264 at the source's size on a server that does not encode HEVC, and nothing caps it. A live load has no playlist check (`capH264Resolution` skips it), and HEVC first never fires for a channel that lists no streams, so a hardware HEVC server sends 4K H.264 too.

Two facts limit the fix. The channel item lists no media streams before it opens (seen on 10.9.11 and 12.4), so no check can read its size from the item, and the HEVC first rule from PR #1323 never fires for such a channel. And a file's 1080p check (`LoadVideoContentTask.capH264Resolution`) does not fit live: its second `PlaybackInfo` would open a second live stream.

Not done in PR #1323: the gap is in live playback, which that PR does not change, and the fix is a design choice. For the upstream link, read the upstream playlist's `CODECS` and `RESOLUTION` before playing it (`hlsMasterVariants()` on the upstream master, where one exists) and send the stream through the server when it is H.264 beyond the ceiling. For the server's stream, read its master playlist the same way and, when it declares H.264 beyond the ceiling, close that live stream before asking again at 1080p. A stall fallback that moves to a capped server stream would cover both. Then check a 4K H.264 channel plays on both server setups, through the upstream link and through the server.

#### With a level setting on, a direct play the decoder never starts hangs at position 0 with no fallback `[fid: level-override-direct-play-hangs-no-fallback]` `[captured 2026-11-05]` `[pinned]`

With `playbackTryDirectHevcProfileLevel` or `playbackTryDirectH264ProfileLevel` on, a source whose only transcode reason is `VideoLevelNotSupported` is direct-played (`triesDirectPlayOverLevel()` in `source/utils/streamSelection.bs`), with the transcode as its fallback (`isTranscodeAvailable`). When the decoder never starts that play, the player reports playing at position 0 indefinitely and the fallback never runs.

Measured 2026-11-05 against 12.4, override on, each case a cold start and a deep-link play: on a Roku Ultra `4850X` (OS 16.0.4, 4K output) a 4K HEVC clip tagged level 6.1, whose stream fits level 5.0, sat at `play`, position 0, for 45 s and for 75 s with no fallback. In the same runs the override helped: the Ultra and the Streaming Stick 4K `3820RW` direct-played a 1080p H.264 clip tagged 5.1 (device level 4.2), and the Ultra a 4K HEVC clip tagged 5.2. The Roku Stick `3600X` refused the H.264 5.1 clip with `-3 ... no memory` after 0.6 s, and its fallback transcode played. So decoders look past an inflated tag within limits the docs do not state, and the setting only helps users who find it.

Root cause: the transcode retry runs only from the `error` branch of `VideoPlayerView`'s state handler (`chooseErrorRetry()`), and the one stall watchdog (`bufferCheck()`) starts only in `buffering`. A play the decoder never starts reports neither. The override and its error-only fallback are in the fork's initial commit, and live and upstream-link direct plays share the same fallback (followup `live-4k-h264-no-1080p-check`).

The same gap covers a file transcode. On a first request, `h264CapDecision()` keeps the answer when the master playlist cannot be read, since the video may be copied, so a 4K H.264 answer then plays and hangs the same way. Measured 2026-11-05 on 12.4 with software encoding, the read is rarely the cause: 20 of 20 answered in a median of 11 ms idle, and 30 of 30 in a median of 21 ms (max 51 ms) while the server encoded 4K at about 8 cores, against the 3 s `timeouts.HLS_MASTER_PROBE_MS`; every line from 10.7.7 to 12.4 declares `CODECS` and `RESOLUTION`. Only a network fault during the read leads there. A start watchdog whose retry asks again with a 1080p cap would cover it.

Options:

- `auto` (recommended): remove both settings; when the server's only reason is the level and the stream's own size, frame rate and bitrate fit the device's level, try direct play for everyone, backed by a start watchdog (the position must move within a measured window, or the load retries through the transcode) and the existing error fallback. Touches the settings, a registry migration to clear the saved keys, the strings and user docs, `triesDirectPlayOverLevel()`, a pure watchdog rule with tests, `VideoPlayerView`, `playback.md` and a decision record. Risk: artifacts no check sees, and a failed try costs the watchdog window before the transcode.
- `startwatch`: keep the opt-in settings and add only the watchdog.
- `remove`: remove both settings and always transcode on a level mismatch.

Not done in PR #1323: it is its own change (settings removal, migration, docs, watchdog), and the hang predates the PR.

Not checked:

- whether the picture was correct: a moving position is not a clean picture, and Roku can't capture the video plane;
- real files whose encoders inflated the tag (all the test clips are synthetic);
- other Roku models and OS versions;
- how long a healthy direct play takes to move on the slowest supported Roku, which a watchdog's window depends on;
- whether the Ultra errors on its own after more than 75 s.

Tracked publicly in [#1330](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/issues/1330).

#### Unconfirmed: a never-played `.strm` item may skip the playlist-size fit and the buffer cap on its first play `[fid: strm-first-play-stale-run-time]` `[captured 2026-11-06]`

`LoadItems_AddVideoContent` in `LoadVideoContentTask` passes `playedSource`, read before the probe, to `fitHlsPlaylist` (its `RunTimeTicks`) and `reencodeCheck.bufferCapBps` (its `Bitrate` and `RunTimeTicks`), both from #1324. `reloadProbedStreams` replaces `meta`'s sources after the first `PlaybackInfo` of a source that lists no streams, and `playedSource` is never read again. If a never-played `.strm` source has no `RunTimeTicks` before its probe, both stand down on that first play, so a long `.strm` film could hit the playlist-too-large failure #1324 fixed. Found while reviewing PR #1325, which fixed the same stale read for the Dolby Vision bypass retry.

Check first: a never-played `.strm` item's `RunTimeTicks` before the probe. If it is missing, read both from the source the current answer's request read (`m.playbackInfoRequestSource`, added by PR #1325). If it is present, close this followup. A `.strm` item's `Bitrate` describes the text file (11 bit/s, measured 2026-10-12), so `bufferCapBps` may need its own fix either way.

Not checked: any of this on a device.

#### A transcode of a source with no audio track never starts on Roku: endless buffering `[fid: video-only-transcode-never-starts]` `[captured 2026-11-06]`

A source with no audio track that the server transcodes never starts: Roku's player stays in `buffering` with no error, and JellyRock shows an endless spinner. Found while reviewing PR #1331, which does not cause it.

Measured 2026-11-06 on a Streaming Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4) against Jellyfin 12.4, with four synthetic 1080p HEVC HLG clips (one variable at a time). The two without audio (24 and 60 fps) stuck in `buffer` for 60 s; the two with AAC played to the end. A one-`Video`-node channel outside JellyRock, given the same server URLs, did the same: `state=buffering errorCode=0` for 45 s on the video-only stream, with `ts` and with `fMP4` segments, while the stream with audio played. Roku never requests the first segment (the server wrote no transcode log for the video-only clip in any run, and did for the clip with audio); requested by hand, the server encodes a valid video-only segment in about 4 s. So the fault is Roku's handling of the server's video-only HLS stream (master `CODECS="avc1.42E02A"`), not the app or the server. The Dolby Vision 8.4 test files with an HLG base layer and no audio show it too.

Options:

- `notice` (recommended): before a transcode play in `LoadVideoContentTask`, if the played source lists no audio stream, show a clear message instead of starting a play that hangs. One translation key and a unit test; direct play left alone. Risk: it blocks a video-only transcode some other Roku or OS version can play.
- `watchdog`: a general stall check (a play stuck in `buffer` at position 0 for N seconds stops with an error), designed with followup `level-override-direct-play-hangs-no-fallback`.
- `report`: report it to Roku's developer forum with the one-node channel to reproduce it; can go alongside either.

Not checked:

- direct play of a video-only file;
- other Roku models and Roku OS versions;
- whether Roku plays a video-only HLS stream from another source;
- how common video-only files are in real libraries;
- whether Jellyfin can add a silent audio track.

To rebuild a clip: `ffmpeg -f lavfi -i testsrc2=size=1920x1080:rate=24 -t 20 -vf format=yuv420p10le -c:v hevc_nvenc -profile:v main10 -color_primaries bt2020 -color_trc arib-std-b67 -colorspace bt2020nc out.mkv` (add an AAC input for the control).

#### Whether Clock.textWidth's same-pass read of its layout group can be one minute stale is unsettled `[fid: clock-textwidth-same-pass-read]` `[captured 2026-11-07]`

`Clock.updateTime` measures `Clock.textWidth` as the `localBoundingRect()` of the `clockGroup` layout group on the same pass that sets the labels' text, and the overhang's cluster and the SyncPlay mark in the OSD lay out `overhangRow.CLOCK_GAP` left of it. Whether that read can hold the previous minute's layout is unsettled. A throwaway Rooibos probe on the Stick 4K (2026-11-06) set the clock's labels several times in one function and read the group's width each time: it disagreed with the labels' own widths plus spacing at every step (at 10:00, 111 against 139). A `Clock.spec.bs` case through `resetTime`, including a wider text set between two ticks, matched every time, and at 8:48 the probe's group width (99) agreed with the RTA ink measurement the overhang was laid out against while the label sum (101) did not. If the read is stale in production, the cluster loses about one digit of gap for the minute after a 12-hour rollover from 9 to 10 (and 12 to 1 gains it), so it would touch the clock's first digit.

Closes after a measurement on the real path: a capture or an ODC read of the overhang right after a width-changing tick (the device clock set to 9:59 in 12-hour format), comparing `textWidth` with the drawn digits. If they differ, measure from the labels instead (their widths plus `itemSpacings`) with a spec that fails first; if not, close as not reproduced.

#### The subtitle panel's in-flight delete pulls the focus into a covered details screen `[fid: subtitle-delete-focus-under-cover]` `[captured 2026-11-08]`

`SubtitlePanel`'s delete response focuses the panel whenever `m.top.isOpen` is true (the `panelOpen` check before `applyFocus()` in the delete's result path). If a cast, a deep link or a remote `GoToSearch` covers `ItemDetails` after the viewer confirmed a delete but before its response arrives, the panel is still open on the hidden screen, so `applyFocus()` pulls the focus into it and the visible screen stops answering the remote. No dialog is involved, so ADR 0055's cover cancel does not reach it. Found by reading the code in the PR #1336 review (2026-11-08); not reproduced. Not fixed there because it needs a reproduction and a choice: close the panel on cover in `ItemDetails.onScreenHidden`, or gate the panel's async focus on the host screen being shown (`m.isShown`, as `navigation.md` advises for timers and observers). Close it with a unit case that covers the screen while a delete is in flight.

#### No manager-level unit case reads what `SyncPlayManager` passes the comparison, and the pause landing limit rests on one device `[fid: syncplay-pause-estimate-wiring-untested]` `[captured 2026-11-10]`

`SyncPlayManager` forwards player events and group frames to the two pure models (`playerFact`, `tellGroupModel`, `onCommand`) and carries out what `syncPlayConverge.decide()` asks (`converge`, `carryOut`, `sendRequest`). The models and the comparison are unit-tested; nothing at the manager level reads what the manager passes the comparison (its context from `comparisonCtx()`, a report, an ask). Reports and asks go out through `fetchAsync`, which a spec with a server set cannot run, and the manager's spec has no mounted `PlayerHostView`. The pure specs, the simulator and the RTA specs carry it. One fault in that forwarding was found by reading, not by a test (`87e1e037`: the last stream's path and length kept when a new one started).

Revised 2026-11-16 for the comparison engine: the functions the first text named (`applyPendingPlan`, `dropPausedEstimate`, `syncResumeShift`) went with the old engine, and its "one Play key round in twelve settles ahead" reading was taken on that engine and is not carried over.

Not checked: how long a pause takes to land on the low-end Stick. `PAUSE_LANDED_MS` (250) rests on the Stick 4K alone, and a device whose pause lands later gets no stopped position from it. The manager logs each landing (`pause landed`, `afterMs`), so a run there reads it off the console.

Added 2026-11-17: the same gap hides the manager's count of time offset picks found in step (`sync.picksSettled`, counted in `carryOut`). The comparison answers `settled` only with a clock estimate, and the manager's estimate fills only from `onTimeSyncResults`, which needs a real time sync, so no unit case fails without the increment. The device case in `syncplay-controls.spec.js` accepts a settled pick and does not require one. The seam below would need a way to give the manager an estimate too.

Not done now because the manager-level case needs a design choice: a seam for the requests (a stub for `sendRequest`, or a request sink the spec can read) and a host stub for the writes.

Closes with: a manager-level case that drives a player event and a group command through `playerEvent` and `onCommand` and reads the action carried out, or a recorded reason no seam is worth it; and the pause's landing time read on the low-end Stick.

#### Translated labels are joined to other text in code, so word order is wrong in many languages `[fid: skip-label-joined-to-segment-name]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

Code builds sentences from a translated fragment plus a value (`translate(translationKeys.X) + " " + value`, or the template-string form), so word order follows English in every language. A search for those two forms matched 37 lines with 15 keys in 9 files (2026-11-15). Examples composed from the current locale files: Spanish `LabelStarts` + day reads `Comienza el hoy` (the translator expected a date), German `LabelViewAll` + name reads `Alle anzeigen Filme`, German `ButtonSkip` + segment name reads `Überspringen Rückblick`. Found while seeding `ButtonSkip` in `/translation-import` batch 1; batch 2 found the rest.

The sites: `ProgramDetails.bs` (`LabelStarted`, `LabelStarts`, `LabelEnded` + day + time; `LabelStartedAt`, `LabelStartsAt`, `LabelEndedAt` + time), `HomeRows.bs` (`LabelRecentlyAddedIn` + library name), `LoadItemsTask2.bs` (`LabelViewAll` + name), `VideoPlayerView.bs` (`ButtonSkip` + segment name, `LabelNextEpisode` + countdown), `ItemDetails.bs` (`LabelOn` + studio, `LabelAt` + air time, `LabelAdded` + date), `AudioPlayerView.bs` (`LabelTrack` + `LabelPositionOfTotal`). Number + unit (`LabelMins`) and the abbreviations (`LabelCh`, `LabelS`, `LabelE`) join the same way. The joins came with the fork; nothing has tried to fix them, and no test reaches the joined text (`videoNotifications.spec.bs` covers only the notice timing).

Options:

- `phrases` (recommended): turn the sentence fragments into about 11 placeholder strings ("Starts {0}, {1}", "Recently added in {0}", "Skip {0}") and update the call sites; leave number + unit and the abbreviations. About 11 strings restart untranslated until a seeding batch or Weblate fills them; needs a device pass on Live TV, Home and the OSD.
- `all`: `phrases` plus number + unit and the abbreviations (`"{0} mins"`, `"S{0}E{1}"`): about 15 new keys, more untranslated strings for little gain where the order already holds.
- `guard` (recommended with `phrases`): a lint check that flags these joins, with a baseline of today's count, so no new ones are added. It stops the spread but fixes nothing alone.

To close: build `phrases` with `guard` in its own PR, then map or translate the new strings.

Not checked: none of these strings was watched on a device (the examples are composed from the locale files); whether the seed sources offer placeholder versions that could seed the new strings; whether number + unit order (`"45 mins"`, `"Ch 5"`) holds in every language.

#### A film that restarted for the re-encode cap restarts again in every new player `[fid: reencode-verdict-forgotten-per-player]` `[captured 2026-11-16]`

The re-encode check's verdict lives on the player (`m.retryFlags.disallowVideoCopy` in `VideoPlayerView`), so a new player of a film that restarted a minute ago restarts again: Resume with Group, a halted member's Play on the group's video, or a second play outside a group. Keeping the verdict for the film version for the app run would let a later player ask for the capped re-encode at once.

Measured 2026-11-16 on a Streaming Stick 4K against Jellyfin 12.4.0 (two joins per run, one film): with the earlier read of `dabe5d11` the group sat in Waiting 10.1 to 10.6 s on three restarted joins and 12.5 s on a fourth, against 7.2 and 8.6 s with the check off. The gain from remembering is an estimate from those two sets, not a measurement.

Not built with `dabe5d11` because it adds state: the verdict has to be keyed on the version and the cap, and dropped when the gear's bitrate pick changes, or a stale one re-encodes a stream the server could have copied. Closes when a second player of the same version starts capped with no restart, shown by a unit case and a device run.

#### The photo viewer's Slideshow and Random settings should mean auto-advance and shuffle, so every combination works `[fid: photo-viewer-shuffle-autoplay-model]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

The photo viewer treats Slideshow and Random as two advance modes, and Slideshow wins when both are on: `PhotoDetails.nextSlide` checks `m.slideshow` first, and Random picks a fresh random index on each tick (repeats allowed) while Left and Right still walk the album in order. The library-tab work (ADR 0056) moved the two toggles to **Settings → User Interface → Libraries → Photos** (`photosSlideshow`, `photosRandom`), where a user can turn both on, so the Random Order description now says it has no effect while Slideshow is on. Album Shuffle (`QueueManager.launchPhotoAlbum`) sets both flags, so it probably plays in order today; not checked on a device.

The agreed model: Slideshow means start playing automatically (off opens the album paused on the first photo; Play already pauses and resumes), and Random means Shuffle: shuffle the album's order once, no repeats, and have the slideshow and Left/Right walk that order. Every combination then means something, and the registry keys stay as they are. Close it by changing `PhotoDetails` to that model with a spec, rewording the two descriptions in `settings/settings.json` and `docs/user/features/photos.md`, and checking album Shuffle on a device. Why not now: it changes the viewer's behavior and needs a photo-album fixture (see `slideshow-wait-untested-no-album`).

#### A music library has no Favorites tab: build one as rows per kind `[fid: music-library-favorites-tab]` `[captured 2026-11-12]`

A movie or TV library has a Favorites tab since the library tabs work: its content tab's view with favorites fixed on (decision `library-tab-view-plus-fixed`). A music library has no one content tab (Albums, Album Artists and Artists are peers), so it got no tab and keeps the Favorites filter on each of its tabs.

Build it as rows per kind (albums, album artists, artists, songs), the way Home's Favorites tab is (`FavoritesRows`), not as one grid. Not done with the tabs work because it is a new surface with its own loading, not a view with a filter fixed on. To close: `MusicPresenter.getOptions()` lists a `favorites` tab, the grid view draws it as rows, the Favorites filter leaves the lists it covers, and `docs/user/features/favorites.md` says so.

#### The TV guide filtered to Favorites does not refresh after a channel's favorite changes elsewhere `[fid: guide-favorites-filter-goes-stale]` `[captured 2026-11-13]`

A Live TV library's guide, filtered to Favorites, keeps its old channel list after a channel is added to or removed from favorites elsewhere (for example from the player while watching it) and the viewer comes back. The favorites counter (decision `favorite-toggles-bump-version`) cannot cover it: `BaseGridView.favoritesStale()` reads `m.gridQuery`, which the guide path never builds, and a reload would do nothing anyway, because `BaseGridView.showTVGuide()` returns early when the guide already exists. Found by reading the code on 2026-11-12, not run on a device.

To close: give the guide a refresh of its own channel list (`Schedule` already reloads channels when its filter changes), call it on resume when the guide's filter is Favorites and `favoritesVersion` moved, and add an RTA case that toggles a channel favorite through the server and checks the guide on return.

#### Home's Favorites tab refetches on every return even when no favorite changed `[fid: home-favorites-skip-unchanged-reload]` `[captured 2026-11-13]`

Home's Favorites tab reloads from the server on every return to Home (`Home.refresh()` calls `FavoritesRows.loadFavorites()`), even when no favorite changed. Since decision `favorite-toggles-bump-version`, every favorite toggle bumps `JRScene.favoritesVersion` through `favorites.request()`, so Home could skip the request when the counter has not moved since its last load, as `BaseGridView.favoritesStale()` does for the library grids. The win is one fewer request per return to Home while its Favorites tab is up; `FavoritesRows` already updates rows in place, so the viewer's place is kept either way.

Not done with the library fix because Home works correctly today; this is a cost saving. To close: record the counter when `FavoritesRows` loads, skip `loadFavorites()` in `Home.refresh()` when it has not moved, and check that a favorite toggled from a details screen still shows on return to Home.

#### Move the app's other lists onto the position line `[fid: position-line-across-lists]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

`ListPosition` (`components/ui/listposition/`, rule in `docs/architecture/design-system.md`, "A list that hides rows: the position line") is the one way a clipped list says it has more rows: "3 of 12" between two arrowheads lit by the rows hidden that way. Only `JRListDialog` uses it. Two older counters draw the count alone, each by hand: `SubtitlePanel.updateListCounter` and `SearchSuggestionList.updateCounter`. (Home's rows use Roku's own `showRowCounter`, which stays.) The Settings lists and `OverviewDialog`'s scrolling text have no cue at all.

Why not now: each surface needs its own placement and a look on a device, and the piece that added the line was the list dialog's.

Closes when each of those shows the position line (the overview dialog: the arrowheads without a count), or records why it does not. Also try a half row showing at the cut edge of a list as a second cue: it was set aside because nobody had tried Roku's `itemClippingRect` on a device.

#### The Replay key has no one rule outside playback `[fid: replay-key-one-rule-outside-playback]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

Outside playback the Replay key means "back to the start", but three ways. On Home and the Favorites rows it jumps to the first item of the focused row, sideways (`HomeRows.onKeyEvent`, `FavoritesRows.onKeyEvent`). In a library grid it jumps to item 0. On a library's Genres tab it jumps to the first row, upward. The persistence split of the library screens work made a library's Replay go to the first item from anywhere on the screen, the letter rail included (`BaseGridView.jumpToFirstItem()`), and removed the legacy branch that cleared the letter and the voice text. That settled the library and nothing else, on purpose.

Decide once: which way a row list goes; whether Replay from the letter rail should move focus to the grid, as it does now; what it does on the TV guide, where it does nothing today; and whether screens without it (details, search, settings) get it. Then apply the rule everywhere and write it into `docs/architecture/design-system.md`.

Why not now: it is a design choice for the operator across several screens, and the piece that surfaced it changes one.

#### The TV guide needs a component of its own in place of Roku's `TimeGrid` `[fid: custom-tv-guide-component]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

The TV guide draws on Roku's own `TimeGrid` (`JRTimeGrid` in `components/liveTv/schedule.bs`), which limits what it can show and how it lays out. The library header strip worked around three of its limits: the row count is set by hand (`scheduleGrid.numRows`, seven on screen and three below the edge so logos load ahead), a fast run of Down presses still outruns that buffer, and the details pane and its picture are placed by fixed numbers. A component of the app's own would own its rows, its loading and its layout.

Why not now: it is a rewrite of the guide, with its own design and device work. What the library screen and the guide exchange is small and would stay: the favorites filter, the search term, `isAtTop()` and `focusedSummary`. Closes when a custom guide component replaces `JRTimeGrid`, or the idea is dropped.

#### A portrait poster is three sizes across Home, the library grid and the Genres rows `[fid: one-portrait-poster-size]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

Three screens draw a portrait poster at three sizes: Home's rows at 234 by 351 (`itemAspectRatio.PORTRAIT`), the library grid at 252 by 378 (`libraryGrid.POSTER_WIDTH`), and the Genres tab's rows at 213 by 320 (`genreList` in `BaseGridView.xml`). The library grid was 264 by 396 until the header strip took room above it, so the sizes were never one. Raised by the operator while judging the strip's layout (2026-11-15).

Why not now: one size changes Home and every row list, each with its own row heights and focus chrome (`docs/architecture/list-grid-item-layout.md`), and needs its own captures. Closes when the three share one constant, or the difference is recorded as intended.

#### Hear the library header strip, its Sort menu and its Filter checklist with the screen reader on `[fid: header-strip-screen-reader-check]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

The library header strip has not been heard with the screen reader on. To check by ear, on a movie library: the Sort and Filter chips say their label and value ("Sort: Title", the direction, "Filter: 2"); the Sort menu says which field is in force and reads the Order row's direction in words; the Filter menu reads each category with what is set; a Filter checklist says "Checked" and "Not checked" as a row is ticked. The strip's title and metadata are not focusable, so also check whether a focused poster still names its item.

Why not now: it needs a person listening to a device. It belongs to the screen reader epic (issue 759). Closes when the check is done and anything it finds is fixed or filed.

#### The letter rail's filter cannot be reached inside the safe zone, and whether the rail should jump is undecided `[fid: letter-rail-filter-outside-safe-zone]` `[captured 2026-11-16]`

The library letter rail is a filter: a press reloads the tab with `NameStartsWith` (`BaseGridView.onAlphaSelectedChanged()`, `LoadItemsTask2`), under any sort. It is drawn at x 52, outside the 5% zone, and nothing inside the zone offers the letters, so a TV that clips the margin loses the filter. `docs/architecture/design-system.md` ("The letter rail: the one thing in the margin") records that as an accepted gap, corrected on 2026-11-15 from a sentence that called the rail a jump.

Two changes to weigh together, since each alters what the rail is for:

1. **A "Starts with" category in the Filter menu.** The letters become reachable inside the zone and the rail becomes a true shortcut. Clear filters already clears the letter, and the strip already shows it.
2. **The rail jumps to the letter when the tab is sorted by name,** in place of filtering, as several other media clients' letter rails do. It needs the index of a letter's first item and paging to an arbitrary place in the grid, and a rule for the rail under any other sort.

Why not now: both are design choices for the operator, and the second needs a paging design. See also `library-grid-alpha-menu-trim`, which needs the same per-letter knowledge.

Not checked: whether any TV in use clips x 52.

#### Grid with details is offered only on a movie library's Movies tab `[fid: grid-with-details-beyond-movies]` `[captured 2026-11-16]`

Grid with details (the presentation layout) is offered only on a movie library's Movies tab, so a layout only movies get reads as unfinished. Offer it on Shows, Collections and Favorites. Each needs the fields its page asks the server for (each presenter's `configureLoadTask()`) and a look at what its details panel shows. Deferred by piece 10 of the library redesign (ADR 0061): it is a pull request of its own after the redesign lands on `main`.

#### Library grids have no List layout `[fid: library-list-layout]` `[captured 2026-11-16]`

Add the third kind of library layout, List: a cell with the poster, the title, facts and the overview beside it, where Items per row sets the column count (one wide row per line at the largest, two columns in the middle; whether it goes to three is a bench call). It needs an overview on every page of items, so measure the page cost first (`docs/dev/measuring-performance.md`). ADR 0061 reserves the stored id `list`. Deferred by piece 10 of the library redesign: its own pull request after the redesign lands on `main`.

#### A library's page menu describes the previous tab while a new tab loads `[fid: page-menu-describes-previous-tab-while-loading]` `[captured 2026-11-17]`

`BaseGridView.showsItemGrid()` decides whether a library's page menu offers Items per row and Item Titles from `genreList.opacity`, which changes only when a load lands. So while a tab switch loads, the menu describes the previous tab's surface. Switching to a movie or TV library's Genres tab still offers Items per row until the genre rows draw, and a pick there saves an unused `columns_genres` and reloads. Switching from Genres back, the entry is missing until the first page arrives. Found in the review of PR 1400 (2026-11-16), read from the code.

Low impact: a short window and no lasting harm. Not fixed in that PR because the fix needs the presenter to say which views draw the genre rows; today `onItemDataLoaded` decides from the data, since music genres use the grid. Close it by giving presenters that answer and having `showsItemGrid()` ask it.

#### What the screen reader says when OK on the icon for the screen on show does nothing is unchecked `[fid: overhang-inert-icon-screen-reader]` `[captured 2026-11-17]`

Since the library screens' piece 12 (branch `feat/overhang-consistency`), a cluster icon whose route is the screen on show does nothing: `JRScene.openClusterRoute` returns early for Search on the search screen and Settings on Settings. Nobody has checked what the Roku screen reader (audio guide) says when OK lands on that icon: it may say nothing, which leaves a blind viewer unsure the press was read. Check on a device with the screen reader on; if it is silent, give the press a cue (for example announce that this is the current screen), without adding a visible mark the operator ruled out.

#### Use Web Client's Home Section Arrangement changes nothing until the next sign-in `[fid: web-arrangement-applies-at-sign-in-only]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

Use Web Client's Home Section Arrangement (`uiHomeUseWebArrangement`) is read only by `user.LoadUserPreferences` in `source/utils/session.bs`, at sign-in, where it decides whether the server's `homesection*` display preferences fill `homeSection0..9`. Changing it in Settings does nothing until the next sign-in or launch: Home's `onSettingsChanged` (since the settings-changed-while-covered hook) cannot apply it, because the server's preferences are not kept after sign-in. To close: on a change, fetch the display preferences again (or keep them from sign-in), rebuild the section settings, and have Home rebuild its rows (its section plan is built once per `HomeRows`, in `loadLibraries`). Found by the audit for the settings-changed-while-covered work (2026-11-18). A Home page entry for it (the planned Home page settings) needs this first.

#### A details screen opened with the clock hidden never ticks Ends At once the clock is turned on `[fid: details-ends-at-misses-clock-turned-on]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

`ItemDetails.init` observes the overhang clock's `minutes` only when the clock is shown (`uiDesignHideClock` off), so a details screen built with the clock hidden never ticks its Ends At line after the clock is turned on in Settings: the line itself is rebuilt on return (the item reloads on every show and `populate*` reads the setting), but it keeps the minute it was drawn at. Low impact. Not fixed with the settings-changed-while-covered work (2026-11-18) because the fix is more than "always observe": the clock is the overhang's shared node, and `ItemDetails.onDestroy` calls `m.clock.unobserveField("minutes")`, the shape `ObserverRegistry.spec.bs` shows can remove another component's observer, so observing it on more screens widens that. To close: decide how `ItemDetails` follows the clock without an unobserve on a shared node (a scoped observer once that is measured, or a field the scene relays), then observe whatever the setting and gate the handler on it.

#### Disable Unwatched Count reaches cells bound after the change, not the cells already on screen `[fid: watch-badge-stale-on-visible-cells]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

Since the settings-changed-while-covered work (2026-11-18), `JRPoster` reads Disable Unwatched Count (`uiTvShowsDisableUnwatchedCount`) when it draws the watch badge instead of once in `init`, so every cell bound after a change is right in both directions (a cell resets its badge and sets it again on each bind; `JRPoster.spec.bs` covers it). Cells already on a covered screen when the setting changes keep the badge they drew until they are bound again: a library's grid keeps its cells across a redraw in place (`GridItem.bs`, no bind on `relayoutGrid`), so its visible cells change only on scroll, reload or reopening. Not checked on a device: whether Home's refresh on return (`updateHomeRows`) binds its visible cells again. To close: check Home and a library on a device after the change; if visible cells stay stale, give the screens' `onSettingsChanged` a way to redraw their cells' badges (a rebind of the visible range, or a poster function the cell calls), without a reload that loses the place.

#### The settings icon's menu can show its first open after a cold start before its glyphs have drawn `[fid: page-menu-glyphs-late-first-open]` `[captured 2026-11-19]`

The settings icon's menu (`showOverflowMenu`, `JRListDialog` rows with an `icons` gutter) can open with its glyphs not yet drawn the first time each glyph loads after a cold start. Seen on a Streaming Stick 4K on 2026-11-18 with Home's menu: a capture right after the menu opened showed the labels and an empty gutter, including the existing All settings gear (`pageSettings.ALL_SETTINGS_ICON`); one second later every glyph had drawn, and later opens drew them at once. A second cold-start run caught them already drawn, so the delay varies. The library's menu loads its glyphs the same way, so this is not specific to Home.

Not measured: how long the gap lasts on each device class, and whether a viewer notices it. To close: measure the gap on the 512 MB Stick and the Ultra; if it is visible, load the menu's glyphs before the menu shows (a `Poster` `loadStatus` wait in the dialog, or warming the textures once after sign-in), or record that it is accepted.

#### Star does nothing on a library's letter rail `[fid: star-dead-on-letter-rail]` `[captured 2026-11-23]`

With focus on a library's letter rail, Star does nothing: the page menu does not open. Everywhere else on the screen it does what the settings icon does (`JRScreen.onOptionsKey`), which is the rule decision `star-does-settings-icon` sets for every signed-in screen.

Seen on a Streaming Stick 4K (Roku OS 16.0.4) on 2026-11-23 during the review of the library screens branch: with key tracing in `Alpha.onKeyEvent` and `BaseGridView.onKeyEvent`, a Left onto the rail printed in both and the Star press after it printed in neither, so the key never leaves the rail's `LabelList` (`alphaMenu` in `components/ItemGrid/Alpha.xml`). Not checked: whether Roku's `LabelList` takes the key by design, and whether `main` behaves the same (its Star dialog used the same path).

Why not now: the key cannot be caught above a node that keeps it, so the fix is a rail built on another node (a `MarkupList`, as the other lists are) or a different way to hand the key on, and the rail's future is already open in `letter-rail-filter-outside-safe-zone`. To close: Star on the rail opens the page menu, with an RTA case in `page-settings.spec.js` that presses it there.

#### A library voice search stops searching the whole tree after a reload `[fid: voice-search-loses-recursion-on-reload]` `[captured 2026-11-23]`

A voice search in a library whose presenter does not search its whole tree (a folder, a collection, home videos, photos) searches everything at first, then only the folder's direct children after any reload. `BaseGridView.onVoiceFilter()` sets `isRecursive = true` on the query it builds, and every later load goes through `loadView()`, which builds the query again from the voice text alone (`buildGridQuery(m.nameStartsWith, m.voiceBox.text)`), so the results shrink after a sort or filter change, or on return from details when a reload is due.

Found by reading the code on 2026-11-23, during the review of the library screens branch; not reproduced on a device (an RTA case would need to set the voice box's text). The same shape is on `main`; the branch adds reload triggers (the Filter chip, the favorites check on resume).

Why not now: it needs a test that drives the voice box, and the fix belongs where the query is built (`buildGridQuery` deciding recursion from the search text), which every load uses. To close: a reload with voice text on keeps searching the whole tree, covered by a spec.

#### A movie library's Genres and Studios tabs offer sorts and filters their lists ignore `[fid: movie-genres-studios-tabs-offer-movie-sorts]` `[captured 2026-11-23]`

A movie library's Genres and Studios tabs show the Movies tab's sort and filter lists on the header strip: `MoviePresenter.getOptions()` trims them only for Collections. On Genres they apply to the list of genres, and the sample row under each genre ignores them (`LoadItemsTask2.loadItems()` builds each row's request without them), so a Favorites or Played filter there usually empties the tab. `TVShowPresenter` already gives its Genres view one sort and no filter. An empty Genres tab also shows no "no items" text: the row-list path in `BaseGridView.onItemDataLoaded` sets `loadState` to `empty` and returns before the text is set.

Found by reading the code on 2026-11-23, during the review of the library screens branch; not seen on a device. The lists are older than the branch; the strip's chips put them on screen all the time.

Why not now: which sorts and filters a genre or studio list should offer is a design choice, with captures. To close: the two tabs offer only what their lists honor, an empty Genres tab says so, and `library-tabs.spec.js` covers both.

A TV library's Networks tab has the same shape: `TVShowPresenter.getOptions()` gives it the Shows tab's sorts and its Favorites, Played and Unplayed filters, and `LoadItemsTask2` sends the view to the studios request. Read from the code on 2026-11-23; what the server does with those sorts on a list of networks was not checked. Closing this covers that tab too.

#### The details screen has no settings menu of its own, and a library's could offer more `[fid: page-settings-on-details-and-more-on-libraries]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

The details screen shows the settings icon and declares no page settings, so the icon opens Settings there. Offer the app-wide settings that change it at once, as ADR 0062 allows: Hide Taglines, Community and Critical Ratings, Disable Community Rating for Episodes, Blur Unwatched Episodes and Disable Unwatched Episode Count, each only on the item types it changes. A library can offer more the same way: Item Count on every grid, and Ratings and Hide Taglines on Grid with details. The operator agreed to these on 2026-11-23.

Open: Backdrop Images. It changes details, Grid with details and Home alike, so decide whether it is offered on all three or stays in Settings only.

Why not now: each entry needs its glyph, a redraw in place that keeps the focus (`watch-badge-stale-on-visible-cells` is the same need for badges), and captures. To close: `ItemDetails.pageSettingsEntries()` and the added library entries, built with `pageSettings.settingEntry()`, with RTA cases in `page-settings.spec.js`, and the Backdrop Images question answered.

#### The Live TV Channels tab's letter rail likely never filters: `/LiveTv/Channels` takes no name parameter `[fid: live-tv-channels-letter-never-filters]` `[captured 2026-11-26]` `[pinned]`

On a Live TV library's Channels tab, a letter is sent as `searchterm` with `parentid " "` to `/LiveTv/Channels` (the `ItemType = "LiveTV"` lines in `LoadItemsTask2`'s `NameStartsWith` block, then `executeItemQuery()`'s `liveTV` route). That endpoint declares no `searchTerm`, `nameStartsWith` or `parentId` parameter in the 10.7.0, 10.11.9 or 12.2 specs, and `BuildGetLiveTVChannelsRequest()` passes every parameter through. The code is unchanged from the fork at v2.2.5. Found while removing `LoadChannelsTask`'s dead letter branch (#1427). #1429's "Starts with" Filter category sets the same `nameStartsWith`, so it inherits the gap on this tab: fix it there.

**Options:** `items` (recommended): with a letter or search in force, ask `/Items` with `IncludeItemTypes=TvChannel` and `NameStartsWith`, and the two-range `#` request other libraries use (`executeHashPage`); stay on `/LiveTv/Channels` otherwise. `client`: fetch every channel and filter on the Roku, which breaks paging and costs memory on a 512 MB Stick. `hide`: hide the rail on Live TV, which removes the control.

**Closes when** a letter on the Channels tab shows only channels whose names start with it, checked on a server with Live TV and covered by a query spec.

**Not checked:** whether the server ignores the parameter or acts on it (needs a server with channels). What the tab shows under a letter on a device. Whether `/Items` with `IncludeItemTypes=TvChannel` and `NameStartsWith` returns the same channel fields and numbers that the grid uses.

#### Sorts and filters for both Live TV tabs: the guide has no sort, the Channels tab one `[fid: live-tv-tabs-sort-and-filter]` `[captured 2026-11-26]`

Both Live TV tabs are wanted with more sorts and filters (decided 2026-11-26 while closing #1427). Today the Channels tab offers Title and All or Favorites (`LiveTVPresenter.getOptions()`), and the TV Guide offers no sort: it always sends `LoadChannelsTask`'s `sortField` and `sortAscending` defaults, which #1427 kept as the guide's sort inputs.

What the server allows: `/LiveTv/Channels` takes `sortBy`, `sortOrder`, `isFavorite`, `enableFavoriteSorting` and the content flags `isMovie`, `isSeries`, `isNews`, `isKids` and `isSports` (10.7.0, 10.11.9 and 12.2 specs), and no name filter, so a letter needs `/Items` (see `live-tv-channels-letter-never-filters`). A guide sort is passed through `m.channelQuery` in `schedule.bs`, and needs a decision on `LoadChannelsTask` moving each page's favorites to the top, which a chosen sort would contradict. Filter categories for Live TV belong with #1440's per-library choice; the guide's sort and that favorites rule are this entry.

### source

#### Custom subtitles are correct ONLY because `vtt` is listed FIRST in `getSubtitleProfiles()` (`source/utils/deviceCapabilities.bs`), and nothing pins that. `[fid: custom-subtitles-depend-on-vtt-first]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

Jellyfin hands back the FIRST advertised subtitle format, converting the source to it; the fetcher in `components/video/LoadCaptionTask.bs` accepts only `(http.*?\.vtt)`. Measured 2026-09-22 against `BATCAVE` (Jellyfin 12.1.0), same item and same request varying only the profile ORDER: `vtt,srt,ttml,sub` and `vtt` alone both returned `Stream.vtt`; `srt,vtt` and `srt` alone both returned `Stream.srt`. So reordering that list — a change that reads as cosmetic — silently breaks custom subtitles for EVERY external track: the fetch logs `No valid VTT URL found in:` and nothing renders, because native captions are suppressed on that path. Scope on `BATCAVE`: 413 external subtitle streams across 600 scanned items, **all** `subrip` (411) or `sami` (2), **zero** natively `.vtt` — every one of them depends on this conversion. `srt` / `ttml` / `sub` are dead weight in that list: they can never be chosen while `vtt` leads. **This is NOT a live bug** — an earlier note here suspected one and it is refuted. **Next step:** pin the invariant with a unit test asserting `vtt` is first (and say why in a comment beside both the profile list and the regex); consider dropping the three unreachable formats in the same pass.

#### `inferServerUrl` (in `source/utils/misc.bs`, called from `loginRouter.bs` `onServerSubmitted` — its only call site) runs a synchronous `roUrlTransfer` probe loop up to 15 seconds during server connect. **Corrected 2026-08-15: this blocks the MAIN thread, not the render thread.** `onServerSubmitted` is reached through `handlePreLoginIntent`, which `main.bs`'s event loop dispatches, and `loginRouter.bs` is main-thread code throughout (its own header says so). The original entry named the render thread and a line number that had drifted. Same *synchronous-probe* class as #550 `A2` (fixed for the deep-link server-switch probe via `ServerReachableTask`), but a different thread, so the severity argument differs: it stalls the app's event loop rather than the render thread's frame loop. Out of scope for the current navigation-hardening project (migration cleanup not surfaced by the #550 review). Fix: move the probe to a Task like `ServerReachableTask`. The `preLogin` ledger now measures this span as the `infer` fill, so the 15 s ceiling is observable rather than theoretical. `[fid: infer-server-url-synchronous]` `[captured 2026-08-15]`

#### Confirm (and if real, remove) the duplicate `ServerInfo()` round trip at cold start. `[fid: duplicate-server-info-cold-start]` `[captured 2026-08-15]`

Reading `beginLogin()` says the saved-server path calls `/system/info/public` TWICE: `server.UpdateURL()` → `server.Populate()` → `ServerInfo()`, and then `beginLogin` calls `ServerInfo()` again for the `.Error` check. Unconfirmed on the wire — the bootstrap's sync calls go through `roUrlTransfer` directly and emit no `[http]` log line, and the `preLogin` ledger publishes only its SLOWEST fill, so on a LAN both `serverConnect` and `serverInfo` (~30 ms each) sit far under `userLoad` and never surface. Settle it with a console probe (`npm run measure -- --deploy --measurement item-grid -n 1` dumps the timestamped window), and if confirmed, have `beginLogin` read the result `Populate` already fetched instead of re-fetching. Costs one avoidable round trip on every cold start, and doubles on a WAN server where round trips stop being cheap.

#### Queue-aware multi-item casting `[fid: queue-aware-multi-item-casting]` `[captured 2026-07-13]`

(remote control #666 follow-up): a Jellyfin `Play` carries the full `ItemIds` list + `StartIndex` + `StartPositionTicks` + `PlayCommand`, but #666 casts only the single `itemIds[startIndex]` via the deep-link seam (`remoteDispatch.dispatchPlay`). Consume the whole list to seed the queue, honor `StartPositionTicks`, and implement `PlayNext`/`PlayLast` semantics (`queueMode` is already parsed by `remoteCommand.playCommandToQueueMode` but ignored) — likely `QueueManager.insertNext`/`push` helpers. Fixes casting a music album (currently plays only track 1). Epic #669.

#### Cast command expansion — player-integrated `[fid: cast-commands-player-integrated]` `[captured 2026-07-13]`

(remote control #666 follow-up): advertise + handle more `GeneralCommandType`s that need player/Live-TV integration — `SetAudioStreamIndex`/`SetSubtitleStreamIndex` (remote audio/subtitle switching during playback, via the player's existing track APIs) and `ChannelUp`/`ChannelDown` (+ a `/guide` route for `Guide`) for Live TV. Add each to `getSupportedRemoteCommands` and a dispatch handler. #666 ships nav + messaging only (`GoHome`/`GoToSearch`/`GoToSettings`/`Back`/`DisplayMessage`). Epic #669.

#### Cast command expansion — platform + ECP `[fid: cast-commands-platform-ecp]` `[captured 2026-07-13]`

(remote control #666 follow-up): (a) `SetVolume`/`VolumeUp`/`VolumeDown`/`Mute` — a streaming player has no system-volume API, but a **Roku TV** exposes more OS surface; investigate whether volume is controllable there before advertising it. (b) directional D-pad (`MoveUp/Down/Left/Right`, `Select`, `PageUp/Down`) + `SendKey`/`SendString`/`TakeScreenshot` — not injectable into the SceneGraph focus from inside the app, but doable via **ECP** (as the RTA tests do); would ride the planned #667 server plugin. Deferred to that effort. Also fold `SetShuffleQueue`/`SetRepeatMode` into the queue-aware casting followup above.

#### Cast `DisplayMessage` — resolve the sender name `[fid: cast-display-message-sender-name]` `[captured 2026-07-13]`

(remote control #666 follow-up): the persistent-message dialog uses a static provenance title (`LabelCastMessage` = "Message from another device"). Resolve the command's `ControllingUserId` → username for "Message from `<name>`" (e.g. "Message from Dad"). A regular user often can't `GET /Users/{otherId}`, so match against the public-users list (`GetPublicUsers`) instead, off the main thread (the `RemoteControlTask` receiver enriches the command before marshalling, or a cached id→name map). Short-circuit the self-cast case via `m.global.user`. Fall back to the static title when unresolved (hidden user / fetch fails). Needs `ControllingUserId` captured in `remoteCommand.parseGeneralCommand` + a `LabelCastMessageFrom` = "Message from {0}" key. Epic #669.

#### Cast list shows the Roku MODEL name, not the friendly name `[fid: cast-list-shows-roku-model-name]` `[captured 2026-07-21]`

(cold-cast producer #668 follow-up): every JellyRock session (live receiver + closed-app phantom) surfaces in the web cast list as `getModelDisplayName()` + `(GetModel())` — e.g. "Roku Ultra (4800X)" — because that's the auth header's `Device=` field (`buildAuthHeader()` in `source/api/baseRequest.bs`, `globalDevice.name` = `getModelDisplayName()` in `SaveDeviceToGlobal()`, `source/utils/globals.bs`). Optional UX polish for multi-Roku households: prefer the user's friendly name ("Living Room"). The correct fix is **app-wide** — change the auth `Device=` header — so the open session and the phantom keep ONE continuous name; sending `friendlyName` only in the `/pair` payload would make the phantom's cast-list name flicker vs the open app across the open/closed swap. Out of scope for #668 (the `"JellyRock"` constant fallback at `PhantomSessionService.cs:255` essentially never shows — a session always carries the model name). Epic #669.

#### Verify the `hls` / `ism` / `dash` entries in the DIRECT-PLAY profile — do they ever match a real library item? `[fid: direct-play-hls-ism-dash-entries]` `[captured 2026-08-30]`

The AUDIO half of this followup shipped in #870: the profile was putting `codec` names in a field Jellyfin matches against `MediaSource.Container`, `m4a` / `m4b` were absent entirely, and 196 of 15,364 tracks were transcoding for `ContainerNotSupported` as a result. What remains is the VIDEO half. Half of it is now measured: on both a Stick 4K and an Ultra (2026-08-30) [`GetDirectPlayProfiles`](../source/utils/deviceCapabilities.bs) emits non-empty `hls`, `ism` and `dash` rows, so Roku's `CanDecodeVideo` does accept those container strings — that part is settled. What is still NOT verified is the Jellyfin side: whether the server ever reports `hls` / `ism` / `dash` as a `MediaSource.Container` for a library item, or whether those three rows are dead weight that has never matched anything. Settle it the same way the audio half was settled — aggregate `MediaSources.Container` across the video libraries and see whether the value ever appears.

#### The video-level probe ladders went from decorative to load-bearing — pick the ceilings deliberately. `[fid: video-probe-ladders-ceilings]` `[captured 2026-08-29]`

[`getCodecProfiles`](../source/utils/deviceCapabilities.bs) probes fixed level lists — `h264Levels = ["4.1", "4.2"]`, `hevcLevels` / `av1Levels = ["4.1", "5.0", "5.1"]` — and advertises the highest rung `CanDecodeVideo` accepted as the `LessThanEqual VideoLevel`. Before #868 the AV1 condition never bound (it shipped `120 * level`, so `LessThanEqual 612` passed every AV1 stream in existence), so that ladder's top rung was inert. Now that the scale is right, AV1 content above level 5.1 transcodes on a device that only probes to 5.1 — even if it could decode higher. The same shape is already latent for `h264` (ladder stops at 4.2, so `h264` 5.x transcodes) and `hevc` (stops at 5.1); #868 didn't introduce it, but it made it real for AV1 without anyone choosing the ceiling. Small blast radius — AV1 above 5.1 is rare — but settle whether the ladders should extend upward (probe 6.x) or whether the top rungs genuinely are the hardware ceiling on current Roku models. Nothing measures this today: `CanDecodeVideo` is the only oracle, and we only ask it about rungs we already listed. Raised reviewing #868.

#### Promote the device-profile A/B harness to `scripts/`. `[fid: device-profile-ab-harness-to-scripts]` `[captured 2026-08-29]`

Proven 2026-08-29 verifying #868: `/Items/{id}/PlaybackInfo` takes the `DeviceProfile` in the REQUEST BODY and returns the server's direct play versus transcode decision, so any profile change can be diffed against a real library with two POSTs per item and no sideload. Transcode reasons are in the `TranscodingUrl` query string — there is NO `TranscodeReasons` field on the media source object, which is a dead end worth not rediscovering. It found what nothing else could: zero decision changes across 33 `(codec, level)` buckets on a 5147-item library, plus a reproduction of all three of #868's failure modes on a live server by varying one condition. **Two named consumers already**: the audio direct-play container question above (which needs exactly this to settle whether `m4a` / `alac` match), and every future device-profile edit, none of which has any regression gate today. Shape: a dev-only script alongside `device:check`, taking server URL + credentials from `.env`, with a `--profile-a/--profile-b` diff mode and a captured-profile fixture so it can run without a Roku. Not CI-able (needs a live server with real media), which is exactly why it should be one command instead of rebuilt each time.

#### Convert `main.bs`'s post-`show()` `print`s to `m.log.*` `[fid: main-bs-prints-to-m-log]` `[captured 2026-08-02]`

(log-manager-init follow-up): proven viable on device (Streaming Stick 4K, OS 15.2.4) — `m.global.rLog` is live immediately after `m.screen.show()` (3/3 cold starts, `show()` blocks until `JRScene.init` completes), and the `roku-log` BSC plugin *does* transform call sites in `source/main.bs` (pkg-path injected in the transpiled output, so `bsconfig-prod`'s `strip` applies there too). Scope is **post-`show()` only** — everything up to and including `setGlobals()` must stay `print`, because the log manager cannot exist before `show()` (`log_Log`'s init creates a `Timer`, which fails on the main thread pre-show). That covers the whole event loop, where most of main's prints live. Deferred from the log-manager-init PR to keep that fix isolated; blocked on nothing.

#### Tell the user when the server can't provide the fallback font `[fid: server-cannot-provide-fallback-font]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

(#582 crash-fix follow-up): `FontDownloadTask` sets `errorMessage` to `Server does not support fallback fonts`, and `handleFontDownloadCompletion()` (`source/main.bs`) only `print`s it before booting Home with `uiFontFallback` still reading ON in Settings. That silence was unreachable until now — on those servers the guard *crashed* in `FontDownloadTask.downloadFallbackFont()` instead of taking this branch, so #841 is what turns it into real user-visible behavior. Fix shape: surface a toast (or a settings-level hint) for the *server does not support* reason specifically, kept distinct from a transient download failure (a URL we can't build, a font list we can't parse, a file that never lands), which shouldn't nag. Raised in review of #841.

#### Quick Connect's 10.7-only `Error`-field branch is schema-derived, not probed `[fid: quick-connect-error-field-unprobed]` `[captured 2026-08-24]`

(#847 follow-up): `quickConnectPollOutcome` (`source/utils/quickConnect.bs`) treats a non-empty `QuickConnectResult.Error` on a 200 as a terminal failure, because 10.7.0's spec fingerprint declares the field and 10.8.0's does not. What is established is that the field EXISTS on 10.7 — not when the server populates it. No 10.7 server was reachable to probe. If 10.7 sets it on an ordinary not-yet-approved poll, Quick Connect there ends after three polls with "try again shortly" instead of waiting for the user to approve. That is bounded and reported where the old behavior was an unbounded silent spinner, so it is the safer direction to be wrong in — but it is still wrong, and it is the first thing to check if Quick Connect is reported broken on 10.7. Settle it by probing a real 10.7 server (or reading the 10.7 `QuickConnectController` source) rather than the spec alone.

#### App-wide logging audit, and the diagnostics capture it gates. `[fid: app-wide-logging-audit]` `[captured 2026-09-01]`

Production builds STRIP every `m.log.*` call (`bsconfig-prod.json` `"strip": true`) and effectively nobody runs dev builds, so no failure a user hits is recoverable afterwards — which is why the playback-error dialog carries its own diagnostics ([`error-details-are-the-telemetry-channel`](decisions.md)). A logger-fed ring buffer was the general answer and is blocked on this audit: `error` conflates internal invariant assertions ("`m.scene` is invalid!") with real user-facing failures, per-tile trickplay errors arrive in floods that would evict a fixed-size ring, and the two most valuable playback lines ("Direct play failed; falling back to transcode", the DoVi buffer overflow) are `warn` — so an error-only capture would miss them. Decide between a plugin rewrite (prod rewrites `error`/`warn` into `diagnostics.record` instead of deleting) and curated explicit calls AFTER the audit. Needs redaction too: `url` is passed straight into log args today. Project-shaped — start with `/start-project` from a fresh session.

#### The subtitle language picker shows two pairs of identical rows for Chinese `[fid: subtitle-picker-duplicate-chinese-rows]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

(#750 follow-up). Verified against Jellyfin's own culture table (`Emby.Server.Implementations/Localization/iso6392.txt` at v10.11.0): `zh-cn` and `zh-hans` both carry the `DisplayName` "Chinese (Simplified)", and `zh-tw` and `zh-hant` both carry "Chinese (Traditional)" — so `remoteSubtitles.cultureOptions()` emits four rows reading as two, plus a separate `ze` row reading "Chinese (Bilingual)".
