They are not duplicates to collapse: the codes are distinct and reach the provider distinctly, so a user picking the "wrong" one of a pair may get a different result set with no way to tell which they chose. This is the same class of problem `subtitleDropdownEntries()` already solves for tracks — make the row say what distinguishes it. **Fix shape:** in `cultureOptions()`, detect display-name collisions across the mapped set and disambiguate the colliding rows by appending their code ("Chinese (Simplified) · `zh-cn`"), leaving every non-colliding row untouched — the same "only mark what needs marking" rule the track dropdown uses. Cheap to test: the collision set is fixed per server and the function is already pure and unit-tested.

#### roku-log prints a `LongInteger` or `Double` argument as an empty string `[fid: roku-log-longinteger-double-empty]` `[captured 2026-09-19]`

**The logger prints a `LongInteger` or `Double` argument as an empty string**, so any `m.log` value that is a tick count or the Video node's `position` (a Double) logs blank unless the caller `.toStr()`s it (`Logger.toString` in `source/logging/Logger.bs` handles Integer, Float, String, Boolean, node and AA only). Still blank as of 2026-09-19: `VideoPlayerView`'s "Subtitle change requires stream reload" and "Audio index changed by user" (`position`), and `LoadVideoContentTask`'s "Playback position resolved" (`startingPoint`). **Fix shape:** one change to `Logger.toString`, test-first in `tests/source/unit/logging/LogManager.spec.bs`, rather than `.toStr()` at each call site. The logger is owned code since #986 (ADR 0054), so it is edited in place.

#### Triage the Hint-severity diagnostics every bsconfig reports `[fid: bsconfig-hint-diagnostics-triage]` `[captured 2026-09-21]`

(edits span `source/` and `components/`): bslint `type-reassignment` (195–200 per config) and BSC `overrides-ancestor-function` (49). Both rules predate the alpha.53 bump that surfaced the `bsconfig` warnings — compare counts against alpha.52 first to see whether the bump added instances, then fix each or silence it deliberately.

#### A merged language-filter option takes its label from whichever ISO 639-2 spelling the server listed FIRST `[fid: merged-language-filter-label-first-spelling]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

**A merged language-filter option takes its label from whichever ISO 639-2 spelling the server listed FIRST**, so a library carrying two spellings that resolve to different names gets an order-dependent label. `languageFilters.optionsFrom()` groups by `languageBaseCode()` but resolves the title from the member code, and the first one into the group wins. Latent rather than observed: every alias pair in the 52 codes the local fixture reports (measured 2026-09-22) resolves identically, so no such pair exists there. **The obvious fix is wrong — it was tried on device and reverted.** Resolving the title from the folded BASE code turns "Afar" into a bare `aa`, because `languageEnglishFallbacks` is keyed on the 3-letter forms (`aar` → "Afar") while `languageBaseCode()` folds those to a 2-letter base that no name table carries. A correct fix picks deterministically among the group's own members — sort the codes and take the first that resolves to a real name, falling back to the base — rather than collapsing the resolve call.

#### Every cold launch logs `BRIGHTSCRIPT: ERROR: ParseJSON: Data is empty` from the vendored `rr/Requests.brs`, and nobody knows which request sends the empty body. `[fid: parsejson-data-empty-cold-launch]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

Seen once per launch, 6/6 on `main` and 6/6 on a branch (Stick 4K, BATCAVE, 2026-09-22), so it predates the latest-rows migration it was found beside. Either a real request is answering with an empty body and being parsed anyway, or it is noise that trains readers to skip `BRIGHTSCRIPT: ERROR` lines — which is the worse outcome, because it degrades every future log read. **Next step:** find the caller. Log the URL when `rr` hits the empty-body parse, then decide whether that endpoint should skip parsing (e.g. a 204) or has a real bug.

#### Decide the "DTS" choice in Preferred Surround Codec `[fid: dts-choice-preferred-surround-codec]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

(`playbackPreferredMultichannelCodec`). Since `audio-profile-documented-and-reported` it has no effect: `buildVideoTranscodingAudioCodecsForContainer` never offers `dts`, and `resolveMultichannelSurroundTarget` already turned the preference into `eac3`. Its premise, that the server cannot encode DTS, was verified only on 10.11 (`docs/decisions.md`). **Next step:** re-check a DTS transcode target on 12.x; if it still yields no audio, retire the option with a registry migration of saved `dts` values and fix the setting's description, which still offers DTS "for systems that prefer it".

Found again 2026-10-29 while writing the Surround sound feature page. The setting governs only the codec of a server conversion; DTS passthrough for a DTS track played as it is (`getActualCodecSupport`, up to 5.1) is a separate path, unaffected, and must stay. Options: `drop-dts` (remove the option, reword the description to `eac3` or `ac3`, migrate a saved `dts` to `eac3` with a real-registry integration test per `/new-migration`, remove the unused `LabelDts` string, regenerate `app-settings.md`; nothing audible changes, since a DTS pick already gets `eac3`) or `reword` (keep the option and say it falls back to `eac3`; the menu still offers a choice that does nothing). Recommended: `drop-dts`, in its own fix PR. Not checked: What the Settings screen shows for a saved value that is no longer an option, which decides whether removing the option needs the migration or only benefits from it. No device run (npm run device:check not run).

#### Measure 7.1 DTS-HD without `DTS:X` `[fid: dts-hd-71-without-dts-x]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

before treating the 6-channel DTS cap as settled. `DTS_MAX_DIRECT_PLAY_CHANNELS` caps DTS direct play at 6 channels per Roku's spec, after a DTS-HD MA + `DTS:X` 7.1 track failed on a Roku Ultra `4850X` (2026-10-01). Whether plain 7.1 DTS-HD MA also fails is unmeasured. The maintainer's library has such a file (DTS-HD MA 7.1 default, eac3 5.1, AV1 Dolby Vision), and AV1 Dolby Vision direct-plays on that Ultra (measured 2026-10-01). **Next step:** play it on its DTS track in the prod app, which still has the old profile, and record whether it starts and has sound. If it does, decide whether the cap should be narrowed to `DTS:X`; that would match on Jellyfin's audio profile label, not a channel count.

#### Cold start loads translations twice `[fid: cold-start-loads-translations-twice]` `[captured 2026-10-10]`

`Main()` in `source/main.bs` calls `loadTranslations(resolveTranslationLocale())` before the scene exists, and `reenterLogin()` in `source/loginRouter.bs` calls it again with the same pre-login locale moments later. So a cold start reads and parses `en_US.json`, and the active locale file, twice.

Not investigated: whether the early load in `Main()` is needed for strings shown before `reenterLogin()` runs (migrations, the scene's init). Not fixed now because that needs a device check of the launch path. Closes when one of the two loads is removed with that check done, or the double load is shown to be needed and the reason is written beside it.

Found 2026-10-10 by the `translations.md` fact-check (docs-voice project, session 9).

#### `TRANSCODE_CAUSE_STEREO_CAPPED_CODECS` hand-copies `getStereoOutputCodecs()`, and nothing checks the two agree `[fid: transcode-cause-stereo-codecs-hand-copy]` `[captured 2026-10-12]`

`TRANSCODE_CAUSE_STEREO_CAPPED_CODECS` in `source/utils/transcodeCause.bs` is a hand-written string of the codec list `getStereoOutputCodecs()` returns in `source/utils/deviceCapabilities.bs`. The two hold the same eight codecs on 2026-10-12, but nothing checks that they stay equal, so a codec added to one list silently drifts from the other and the explanation `transcodeCause.bs` gives stops matching what the device profile caps. Found in the docs-voice comment pass (batch 5, recorded at batch 6's PR). Not fixed now: closing it is a code change with a test (derive the constant from `getStereoOutputCodecs()`, or a unit test asserting the two agree), outside a comment-only PR. Closes when one list is derived from the other or a test holds them equal.

#### A tile's `JellyfinBaseItem` costs about 1.5 ms to build on a Task, so large rows are slow and search caps each read at 50 `[fid: lean-tile-node-cost]` `[captured 2026-10-17]`

Every tile in the app is a `JellyfinBaseItem` (167 interface fields) built on a Task by `JellyfinDataTransformer.transformBaseItem()`, and building one is the dominant cost of a large row. Measured 2026-10-17 on a Stick 4K (Jellyfin 12.1, search for "a", 100 items per read, 698 items, temporary timers in a throwaway build): network for the seven pipelined search reads about 240 ms, converting the items about 1650 ms, the handoff to the render thread about 250 ms, `SearchRow`'s row build about 80 ms more than at 50 per read. Reading the server version once instead of per item (`transform-base-item-per-item-crossing`) brought conversion to about 1290 ms, so about 1.85 ms per item remains. A 200-node benchmark on the Task thread: creating an empty `JellyfinBaseItem` about 0.7 ms, writing its 81 fields one by one about 0.8 ms more, one `setFields()` call instead saved about 10%, a plain `ContentNode` about 0.03 ms. So the cost is the node, not the writes' batching.

Because of it, search caps each read at `SEARCH_READ_LIMIT` = 50 (#1216, one read per type: 100 cost about +0.7 s for "a" on the Stick 4K and about +0.6 s on a 512 MB Stick, measured 2026-10-18; `search-row-paging` has the numbers and the other way to longer rows), and Home rows default to 32 (`HOME_ROW_LIMIT_DEFAULT`). The TV guide followup on program-cell memory names the same node from the memory side.

Not now: it changes the item every tile renderer (`BrowseRowItem` and the other row and grid cells) reads, and the path that opens an item, so it needs a design and its own measurements. Direction to evaluate: the Task sends plain associative arrays (about 0.2 to 0.4 ms each in the same benchmark, and cheap to marshal), the render thread builds a light tile node with only the fields cells read, and the full item is loaded when a tile is opened. Closes when a tile costs a small fraction of today's, measured on a Stick 4K and a 512 MB Stick, and search's limit is raised on that evidence.

#### An ECP `/launch` with a `contentId` restarts a running JellyRock instead of delivering an `roInputEvent` `[fid: ecp-launch-restarts-running-app]` `[captured 2026-10-22]`

Seen on a Streaming Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4), 2026-10-22, while verifying the sign-in landing fix: an ECP `POST /launch/dev?contentId=<id>&mediaType=movie` sent while JellyRock sat on the sign-in screen made the console log the app exiting and starting again, instead of the running app receiving an `roInputEvent`. The manifest sets `supports_input_launch=1`, which Roku's `roInput` docs say lets a running app accept deep-link parameters without relaunching. The link still opened after sign-in, through the cold-start `launchArgs` path, so nothing is lost but what the user had typed on the sign-in screen. The companion plugin's wake (ADR 0023) sends this `/launch` whenever it sees no live session, which includes an app sitting on a pre-login screen. Not yet known: whether the test library's own `RTA_LAUNCH` parameter (`ECP.sendLaunchChannel` always adds it), the dev channel, or the app causes the restart. Closes with: reproduce with a plain `curl -d '' "http://<device>:8060/launch/dev?contentId=<id>&mediaType=movie"` against a running app, then `/snag` it if the restart holds.

#### With a bitrate cap set, a stream that reports no video bitrate always transcodes `[fid: capped-unknown-bitrate-transcodes]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

With a bitrate cap set (in Settings or picked in the OSD gear), a stream that reports no video bitrate is transcoded even when it would fit. `GetBitRateLimit()` in `source/utils/deviceCapabilities.bs` sends its `VideoBitrate` condition with `IsRequired: true`, and Jellyfin's `ConditionProcessor.IsConditionSatisfied` treats an unknown value as satisfied only when the condition is not required. This was already true for `h264`, `h265`, `av1`, `vp9` and `mpeg2video`; the fix on branch `feat/osd-settings-menu` that put the caps on every direct-played codec extended it to `mpeg4`, `vp8`, `mpeg1video` and `h263`, which more often come from old files with no bitrate in their metadata. Kept on purpose: an unknown bitrate cannot be shown to be under the cap.

Revisit if reports show needless transcodes of such sources. The choice then is `IsRequired: false` for the bitrate condition (an unknown bitrate direct-plays, and so can exceed the cap), measured against a few real files with no reported bitrate first.

#### The gear has no Auto that measures the connection with a speed test `[fid: gear-auto-bitrate-speed-test]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

The OSD gear's Maximum Bitrate offers No limit, a fixed ladder and the Settings value, but nothing that measures the connection. The `jellyfin-web` client's Auto does: before each remote playback, `bitrateTest.ts` downloads up to three payloads from the server's `/Playback/BitrateTest` (0.5, 1 and 3 MB, 5 s timeout each, stopping early under a threshold), sends 70% of the measured speed as `MaxStreamingBitrate`, caches the answer for an hour, and lifts it to at least 140 Mbps on the local network (read 2026-10-23 at jellyfin-web `0878e33`). It measures once at the start and does not react mid-playback. The gear labels 0 No limit, not Auto, so that the name is free for this (decision `bitrate-zero-means-no-limit`).

Not now: it is a feature, not a fix, and it puts a download of up to a few megabytes in front of every remote playback, with a Task to run it (`source/CLAUDE.md` thread rules) and a design choice about where the measured cap applies (`GetBitRateLimit()` per codec, or `MaxStreamingBitrate`). Closes when an Auto row exists in `playbackSettingsMenu.bitrateOptions()` backed by a measured cap, or when the idea is declined in a decision note.

#### The Bitrate Limit's Enable toggle is redundant now that a Maximum Bitrate of 0 means no limit `[fid: bitrate-enable-toggle-redundant]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

**Settings → Playback → Bitrate Limit** has two settings, the Enable limit toggle (`playbackBitrateMaxLimited`) and the Maximum Bitrate number (`playbackBitrateLimit`). Since 0 stopped meaning Roku's per-codec ceilings (decision `bitrate-zero-means-no-limit`), "on with 0" and "off" behave the same, so the toggle adds a step and no choice: a number alone, 0 for no limit, says everything. `playbackCaps.settingsBitrateMbps()` is the one reader of the pair.

Not now: removing a setting needs a registry migration for users who have the toggle off with a number saved (they must end up with 0), and its own PR. Closes when the toggle is gone through `/new-migration`, or a decision note keeps it on purpose.

#### Cast targets on one Roku cannot be told apart: name each the same open and closed, with dev builds marked first `[fid: cast-target-names-per-build]` `[captured 2026-10-26]`

For the repo `jellyrock/jellyfin-plugin-jellyrock` first, then this one. The mailbox refused the message (that repo is not registered on this machine), so it waits here. The playbook repo has a pinned message (`onboard-jellyfin-plugin-jellyrock`, sent 2026-10-26) to onboard and register the plugin repo and reply when done. Step 1: once that reply arrives, move this entry across with `mailbox.sh send jellyrock/jellyfin-plugin-jellyrock --from cast-target-names-per-build`; before then, paste everything under "Message for the plugin repo" into a session there. Step 2, once the plugin settles the `/pair` field: in this repo, add one name helper used by the auth header (`source/api/baseRequest.bs`, `Device=`) and `buildPairPayload` (`source/remotecontrol/remoteProtocol.bs`), with a unit spec, so a target has one name open and closed, and a dev build's name starts with "Dev · ".

Message for the plugin repo:

Investigate, then propose a plan (no code until it is approved), for a cast-list naming problem. It spans this repo and jellyrock/jellyrock (the app).

What happened: on one Roku with two JellyRock apps installed (the published channel, app id 819325, and a sideloaded dev build, app id "dev"), the `jellyfin-web` "Play On" list showed two targets, "JellyRock" and "Streaming Stick 4K (3820X)", with nothing saying which was which. "JellyRock" was the published channel's cold-launch phantom; choosing it woke that app over the running dev build. Waking the other build is fine and intended. The problem is that the user cannot tell the targets apart.

Why the names differ (read from both repositories, 2026-10-26; app side at `jellyrock/jellyrock@861f4047`):

- Each app on a Roku gets its own roDeviceInfo.GetChannelClientId(), which JellyRock uses as its Jellyfin `DeviceId`, so the two apps pair as two devices. That part is correct.
- Open, a target's name comes from the app's auth header: Device="<`GetModelDisplayName()`> (<`GetModel()`>)" (app: source/api/baseRequest.bs, source/utils/globals.bs). It carries no dev marker, and this plugin never rewrites a live session's name.
- Closed, `PhantomSessionService.RefreshPairingAsync` reuses the existing session's `DeviceName` when one exists, else `DefaultDeviceName(record)`: "JellyRock" or "JellyRock (dev)". So one target's name changes with its state and over time, and only one state marks a dev build.
- The /pair report (app: `source/remotecontrol/remoteProtocol.bs` `buildPairPayload`) sends the IP addresses, `appId` and `isDev`, but no name.

ECP /query/device-info on three Roku devices (2026-10-26) returns user-device-name (the name set in the Roku's settings: "Roku Ultra", "Streaming Stick 4K", "3600X"), user-device-location ("Living room", "Office", "Office"), friendly-device-name (equal to user-device-name on all three), model-name, model-number and default-device-name ("<model> - <serial>"). Two of them share a location.

Proposed shape, to confirm or replace with something better:

1. One target, one name, the same open and closed.
2. The name leads with the build marker for a dev build and is unmarked for the published channel: "Dev · <Roku name>" and "<Roku name>". Dev builds are the edge case; the marker goes first so it cannot be missed or cut off.
3. The app is the single source: it builds the name once and sends it in its auth header and in /pair (a new optional field, additive per the /pair contract). This plugin stores it on `PairingRecord` and uses it for the phantom, falling back to `DefaultDeviceName` only for an older app that does not send it.
Ruled out: the phantom copying the live session's name from the same `WakeIp` (two identical names); this plugin computing a name from ECP device-info (two sources that drift).

Investigate before planning:

- What the `jellyfin-web` Play On list renders for a session (`DeviceName` only, or also the client name or an icon), on web and mobile, and whether it truncates long names. The official Jellyfin Roku app (592369) was installed on the same Roku and is listed too: decide whether the published name needs "JellyRock" in it to tell it from Jellyfin.
- Every naming state of a target (never connected, open, closed with a cached live-session name, closed after the session expired) and what each shows today, with a test per state.
- Which Roku name to use: user-device-name alone, or with user-device-location when two Roku devices share a name; and what `roDeviceInfo.GetFriendlyName()` returns in the app (the app's globals.bs treats it as "<model> - <serial>", while the ECP field friendly-device-name is the user-set name). Check on a real Roku.
- How a beta or second published channel would be marked, since `roAppInfo.IsDev()` is true only for a sideload.
- Whether `IncludeDevelopmentBuilds`' default and wording still fit once dev targets are named.
- The exact /pair field name and version handling on both sides.

The app-side half (one name helper used by the auth header and `buildPairPayload`, plus its unit spec) waits on the /pair field this plugin settles. When the contract is decided, send a message back to jellyrock/jellyrock naming the field and its rules.

#### SyncPlay in a group offers no queue edits from the Roku, and voice fast-forward and rewind are unchecked `[fid: syncplay-queue-edits-shuffle-voice-scrub]` `[captured 2026-10-29]`

Phase E (#1276) chose honor-only for the group's playlist: the server owns it, so the Roku offers no queue editing while following (add, remove or move an item in the group's playlist). The group's repeat and shuffle ship in the OSD gear (decision `syncplay-group-repeat-rides-queue`), so queue edits are the part left to design, in the SyncPlay project's hardening phase.

Voice fast-forward and rewind while following are now a relative group seek (`voiceTransport.groupTrickSeekDelta`, `VideoPlayerView.handleTransport`), with a unit case and an RTA case in `syncplay-controls.spec.js` that passed on the Stick 4K against the local 12.4 server (2026-11-06). Left unchecked: whether a remote fast-forward or rewind key held with the OSD closed can move this member alone before any OK; the scrub bar's own confirm already posts the group seek.

Closes when the queue-edit design ships or is declined in a decision note, and a device check shows the held remote key cannot land a scrub without an OK.

#### A SyncPlay join on a 12.2 server once left the Roku's icon inactive, cause unknown `[fid: syncplay-join-frame-never-seen]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

A SyncPlay join in the RTA specs occasionally never turned the icon active, though the server recorded the join. The cause on servers before 10.11 is found and fixed; one sighting is left unexplained.

**Found and fixed (10.9 and 10.10).** A probe that relaunches the app and joins once per launch lost 6 of 15 first joins on 10.10.7, then 2 of 15 (Streaming Stick 4K, 2026-11-17), and none of 15 on 10.11.11. In each lost round the server's session list showed this device's session with no socket (`SupportsMediaControl` false) while the Roku's socket was open and receiving keep-alive frames. `SessionManager.GetSessionInfo()` at `v10.10.7` and `v10.9.11` looks the session up and stores a new one in two steps; `v10.11.0` uses one atomic step. The receiver now announces the session before each connect and checks it after the attach (`RemoteControlTask.announceSession`, `remoteProtocol.socketSessionState`). With both, 30 of 30 joins arrived on 10.9.11 and on 10.10.7, with no reopen needed.

**Left: the 12.2 sighting.** The same wait failed once in a matrix run on 12.2 (2026-11-01), with no console kept. A 12.x server creates sessions in one step, so the cause above does not explain it. It has not been seen on a 12.x server since: 15 of 15 joins arrived on 12.4.0 with the probe (2026-11-17).

Not checked: a cast or a remote-control command on 10.9 or 10.10 before the fix (the same push path, so it should have been lost the same way); a join pressed in the 2 s between the attach and the check on a launch the announce did not cover.

Close it when a run of the join probe on a 12.2 server shows no lost join, or the 12.2 loss is seen again with a console and explained.

#### Roku's analyzer still flags the custom locale folder and a deprecated API `[fid: analyzer-remaining-warnings]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

Roku's analyzer on #1299's branch (2026-11-01) still warns `Unsupported or incorrectly configured locales found: custom`, because `translate.bs` reads `pkg:/locale/custom/` and Roku treats folders under `locale/` as locales. It also warns that `InitClientCertificates()` is deprecated, in `TimeSyncTask` and the vendored `rr` module. The analyzer exits 0 on all of them. Whether any blocks publishing was not checked.

**Fix shape:** moving the folder touches `translate.bs`, the seed and Weblate scripts and about 40 files naming the path, so it needs its own design. Check first whether the warning ever blocks a submission.

#### The app never fires the dialog beacons Roku requires before Home `[fid: appdialog-beacons-certification]` `[captured 2026-11-01]` `[pinned]`

Roku's analyzer (2026-11-01) warns that the app must fire `AppDialogInitiate` and `AppDialogComplete` if it shows a login, user selection or other dialog before the home page (certification requirement 3.2), and Roku's static analysis docs say the same. `source/` and `components/` fire neither, and the pre-login screens wait for input before `HomeRows` signals `AppLaunchComplete`. It is a warning today (exit 0). Whether it blocks publishing was not checked.

**Fix shape:** fire the pair around each pre-login view (`SetServerScreen`, `UserSelect`, `LoginScene`), then re-run the `Roku static analysis` workflow.

#### Partial lists of number type names may reject real values `[fid: number-type-name-lists]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

Several checks compare a number's `type()` against a partial list of names. On a Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4, measured 2026-10-31) an AA value reports `roInteger`, a value from a native AA or a node field `roInt`, a `ParseJson` value and a local literal `Integer`. These lists leave out at least one: `setFieldTextValue()` in `misc.bs` and `setFieldText()` in `ItemDetails.bs` (no `roInteger`), the query builder in `baseRequest.bs` (no `Integer`), and `ProgramDetails.bs` (only `roInt`). None is shown to misbehave yet: each depends on where its value comes from. `isIntSafe()` and `mediaNumber()` already handle every name.

**Fix shape:** check each site's real value on a device and move the wrong ones to those helpers. A build plugin rule only if the mistake recurs: this shape has now shipped in `GetBitRateLimit` and been caught in #1299.

#### `initializeLogManager` leaves `enabled` false, which `guard` would turn into silence `[fid: log-enabled-default-false]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

**`log.initializeLogManager` defaults `enabled` to `false`, and `JRScene.init()` does not pass it,** so every `Logger.enabled` is `false`. Nothing reads it today, but the `roku-log` plugin's `guard` option wraps each call in `if m.__le = true`, so turning `guard` on would silence every log line without an error. Fix: default it to `true` (or pass it from `JRScene`), with a test in `tests/source/unit/logging/LogManager.spec.bs`. Listed under Known issues in `source/logging/README.md`; remove it there too.

#### Send logs to the user's Jellyfin server (epic) `[fid: log-upload-to-server]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

**Let a user send the app's logs to their own Jellyfin server.** `POST /ClientLog/Document` takes `text/plain` up to 1,000,000 bytes and returns `{ FileName }`; it exists from 10.8.0 (absent at 10.7.7) and answers 403 when the server's `AllowClientLogUpload` is off, which defaults to on (checked in Jellyfin's source at `v10.11.0`, 2026-11-05). Jellyfin's Android TV client uses it for crash reports. Open questions: a production build strips every `m.log` call, so decide what it keeps (for example warn and error into a bounded buffer) and budget it against the thread-crossing cost model; the 10.8.0 guard and its endpoint-availability ledger entry; masking addresses and names on upload (followup `log-redact-secrets`). Next step: draft the epic issue and show it to the maintainer before filing.

**Findings from PR #1329's review (2026-11-06), for the epic draft:**

- Goal agreed with the maintainer: a sent log is safe to paste publicly as it is. Production keeps warnings and errors at least; info is undecided, and the build should force well-formed log calls (a fixed message, values limited to IDs, numbers and enums, no object dumps) at those levels.
- Masking happens when a line is written (`log.Redactor` in `Logger.log()`), so a buffer saved for a crash never holds a raw token.
- Crash detection, from Roku's docs: `main()` receives `lastExitOrTerminationReason` (`EXIT_BRIGHTSCRIPT_CRASH`, `EXIT_BRIGHTSCRIPT_TIMEOUT` and more; lost on reboot). `roAppManager.GetLastExitInfo()` (Roku OS 13.0+) returns the exit code, a timestamp and `console_log`, the last 20 raw console lines, only with `run_as_process=1` in the manifest (not set today). Keep only known-safe `console_log` lines (exit code, error line, backtrace frames); drop the rest, "Local Variables" included.
- Storage: `tmp:` is lost on exit; the registry is 32 KB for the whole app, shared with settings; `cachefs:` survives an app exit but not a reboot, and the OS may evict it, so check the file exists.
- Acceptance: missing or unknown exit info means no crash log, never a blocked or slower launch. A found log is masked, offered or sent, then deleted.
- Not checked: what else `run_as_process=1` changes (enable it, then run the device suites on all three test devices); the per-line cost of a buffer sink (today's transports `callFunc` once per line, the rendezvous-per-item shape).

#### Route every print through the log redactor `[fid: log-print-through-redactor]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

**Route every `print` through the redactor.** The 161 `print` statements in `source/` and `components/` bypass `log.Redactor`; most print user IDs or the server URL (`inferServerUrl` in `source/utils/misc.bs`). `source/utils/session.bs` printed the access token among every user setting until PR #1329 cut that print to keys only. Agreed shape (2026-11-05): a free function such as `log.print(...)` that masks and prints, usable before the log manager exists, and the `print-locations` plugin failing any raw `print` outside `source/logging/`. Comes after `rr-http-trace-leaks-token`.

**Added 2026-11-06:** masking cannot hide free text or a secret passed without its key. The `session.bs` settings dump showed the username and recent searches until PR #1329 cut it to keys, and `m.log` is held to that by the build (`log-personal-text`, `log-secret-value`), which a `log.print()` needs too. A `log.print()` that caches patterns must rebuild them when missing, as `log.Redactor` does: a node's `m` handed to another thread loses its `roRegex` objects.

#### A 4K H.264 source is re-encoded at 1080p even on a hardware HEVC server `[fid: h264-source-cap-limits-hevc]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

An H.264 source caps the H.264 codec profile at `1920x1080` in `buildPlaybackInfoPostData` (`source/api/items.bs`, the block that caps the H.264 codec profile for an H.264 source), and Jellyfin applies a codec profile's width and height to the whole transcode, so a 4K H.264 source is re-encoded at 1080p even on a server that encodes HEVC in hardware and could keep 4K. Seen 2026-11-04 on a Roku Ultra against Jellyfin 12.4 with `NVENC`: a 4K H.264 source re-encoded as HEVC at `1920x1080`. Rare (4K H.264 sources are uncommon). Not done in PR #1323 because it needs its own device check on two server setups. Fix by dropping that cap where `capH264Resolution` already guards H.264 output, then check a 4K H.264 source keeps 4K on a hardware HEVC server and gets 1080p H.264 on a default one.

#### `mediaNumber` loses the low digits of large values `[fid: media-number-long-precision]` `[captured 2026-11-04]`

`mediaNumber` in `source/utils/misc.bs` reads a long integer through a float, so a large value loses its low digits (94848001 reads as 94848000, found 2026-11-04). It is harmless for the values it serves today, but wrong for bitrate and byte values; `reencodeCheck.bitsPerSecond` was added to avoid it. Decide whether `mediaNumber` should keep integers whole, and check its callers.

#### A SyncPlay catch-up's learned lead misses on a file with sparse keyframes: size it from the file's keyframe times `[fid: syncplay-catch-up-lead-from-keyframes]` `[captured 2026-11-12]`

A SyncPlay catch-up seeks ahead of the group by a lead it learns from the stream's last five landed seeks (the slowest plus a margin, between `syncPlayPlayback.CATCH_UP_LEAD_MS` and `CATCH_UP_LEAD_MAX_MS`), and holds for whatever the seek did not use. The player seeks exactly, so seek time depends on how far the target sits past its keyframe, not on the seeks before it: on a Stick 4K it was 421 ms plus 235 ms per second past the keyframe (94 direct-play seeks, 2026-11-11).

On a file with sparse keyframes the learned lead is wrong in both directions. Measured 2026-11-18 on a Streaming Stick 4K (Jellyfin 12.4.0, a 100 Mbps direct play with keyframes about 8 s apart, 20 catch-ups made by a throwaway probe): 6 seeks whose target sat just before a keyframe took 7.8 to 8.3 s against a lead of 3.0 to 3.9 s, so each outlasted its lead and the whole group waited on the member; the 2 whose target sat just after one took 1.1 s; the other 12 took 2.6 to 3.4 s, all under the 6 s lead a slow seek before had taught, and the picture then stayed frozen 2.7 to 3.5 s. Every round ended within 50 ms of the group, and the member never asked the group to come to it.

With the file's keyframe times, `syncPlayConverge.catchUp()` could size each lead as the seek's own time plus a margin, or move the target to the next keyframe: one jump, a shorter freeze and no group wait. It needs its own design. The 12.4.0 server's API has no keyframe data (its OpenAPI description, searched 2026-11-12), so the app would read the file's own index, per container.

Not checked: other server versions, transcodes (HLS segment boundaries), what parsing an index costs on the device, and how common keyframes this far apart are in real libraries. Closes with a measured design, or a recorded no.

#### Rename negatively worded settings to positive names, with migrations `[fid: positive-setting-names]` `[captured 2026-11-14]`

Four settings are named in the negative: `uiDesignHideClock`, `uiDetailsHideTagline`, `uiTvShowsDisableCommunityRating` and `uiTvShowsDisableUnwatchedCount`. The user turns a setting on or off, so a "Disable" or "Hide" name reads as a double negative. Rename them to positive names in the house style, and check the other `settingName` values against the same rule.

Each rename needs a registry migration (`/new-migration`), because settings are saved under `settingName`. A flip to positive wording must also invert the stored boolean, so test each one on a device.

Afterward, run `npm run update-translations -- --rename Old=New` so each `SettingDesc` key matches its setting's new name. Then tighten the key-name rule in `docs/dev/translations.md` and `locale/CLAUDE.md` back to "`SettingDesc` plus the `settingName` without its area prefix". The rename-key PR (`refactor/translation-key-names`) loosened that rule, because the names it would derive from were about to change. Related: `in-app-settings-copy` (the description wording itself).

#### Back-to-back cast commands ride one field with one notification each, the road that held SyncPlay frames `[fid: cast-commands-one-notification-each]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

Cast commands (play, navigate, transport, route, message) still travel from the receiver task to the main thread on one field, `RemoteControlTask.dispatchCommand`, with one notification per command. SyncPlay frames left that road on 2026-11-15 after a frame reached the manager about 20 seconds late, after the frame written behind it (five sightings on a Streaming Stick 4K, see "SyncPlay frames do not take that road" in `docs/architecture/remote-control.md`). Two cast commands sent back to back, such as a play and a seek, could be held the same way.

Not seen with cast commands, and not tried. Why not now: the SyncPlay fix was sized to the frames that showed the fault, and a cast command's seams are main-thread code, so it cannot use the manager's road.

Closes with a device run that sends cast commands back to back in a long loop and either shows none is held, or moves them to a numbered hand-off (`syncPlayInbox` is the model: the main loop would apply each number once and check the field again on a timer).

#### The early-Logger fix sits on a local branch with no PR `[fid: logger-rebind-branch-unshipped]` `[captured 2026-11-17]`

The fix for a `log.Logger` built before the log manager exists is committed on its own branch, `fix/logger-rebind-late-manager` (worktree `.claude/worktrees/logger-rebind`, commit `766ab917`), and is not pushed and has no PR. Without it `AppWaitHost` and the startup `JellyfinUserSettings` write no log line on a device. A Logger with no manager now looks again on each call (`Logger.attach()`).

Checked 2026-11-17 on a Streaming Stick 4K: the new `LogManager.spec.bs` cases failed before the fix and pass after; the same RTA spec with and without the change printed 0 and 1 `AppWaitHost` lines.

Not done now: it was found during SyncPlay work and kept off PR #1355. Its PR goes through `/pr` from a Claude session started in that worktree. `/pr`'s decision pass should record the choice not to buffer lines written before the manager exists.

Close it when the branch's PR is open.

#### A load in a SyncPlay group can fail to reach a first frame, and the buffer check ends it with a playback error `[fid: syncplay-load-never-reaches-first-frame]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

Three sightings, all while following a group: a soak on 2026-11-12; on 2026-11-17 a direct-play restart at a full buffer in `syncplay-controls` ("another member's Seek while this Roku loads a restart"), and a transcode restarting for the re-encode at 33% in `syncplay-menu` (Resume with Group). The viewer sees Error During Playback after the buffer check's window.

Root cause not confirmed. In the direct-play sighting SyncPlay wrote nothing to the node before the stall, and the server resumed the group only by its own give-up (`waited 30273 ms … giving up` in its log). 40 plus 200 rounds of the two restart cases on a Stick 4K at `7329cf94` (2026-11-17) did not reproduce it.

Since `28e49f7c` the buffer check logs the node when it stops a stream (`stalled stream buffer`, `stalled stream node`, `stalled stream reports`). To close: read those lines in every soak console, and decide the fix from the next sighting.

Options weighed and not built:

- Reload once a load stuck at a full buffer (`syncPlayConverge.seekIsStuck()` covers seeks only). It treats a result whose cause is unknown, misses the 33% sighting, and nothing forces a stuck load on a device to prove it (followup `rta-cannot-stall-video-stream`).
- Loop the full `syncplay-controls` spec with a throwaway node trace: about nine hours of device time.

Healthy loads, for a threshold (Stick 4K, direct play, 2026-11-17, 417 loads): buffering 2.4 s at most, at a full buffer 0.41 s at most; a seek sat at a full buffer 0.57 s at most (395 seeks).

Not checked: why the node stalls; whether plain playback outside a group stalls the same way; how long a healthy transcode load buffers.

Why not now: the cause is unknown and the stall cannot be forced.

#### The SyncPlay stuck-seek wait has under 2 s of headroom over a healthy seek on an Ultra `[fid: syncplay-stuck-seek-wait-thin-headroom]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

The comparison writes a seek again once the node has sat at a full buffer for `stuckFullBufferMs` (10 s, `syncPlayConverge.defaultPolicy`), on the reading that a healthy seek never waits that long. On an Ultra a healthy seek comes close: measured 2026-11-18 against Jellyfin 12.4.0 on a 100 Mbps direct play with keyframes about 8 s apart, 60 plain seeks all landed, 47 of them filled the buffer first, and the longest then sat full for 8.2 s (median 2.8 s); whole seeks ran 2.8 to 11.9 s. On a Streaming Stick 4K the longest on the same file was 6.4 s (2026-11-16). A healthy seek that passed 10 s would be written twice, and a group seek that sticks twice reloads the stream.

Nothing was counted stuck in 20 catch-up rounds on the Ultra the same day, so no viewer-facing fault is shown yet. The direction that fits the other limits here is a wait the member learns from the stream's own healthy seeks, with the fixed number as its floor, in place of a larger fixed number.

Not checked: files past 100 Mbps, the low-end Stick on a heavy file it can direct play, and whether time at a full buffer grows with the distance past the keyframe as the whole seek does. Closes with a learned wait and its device run, or a recorded no with a measurement that shows the headroom holds.

#### A SyncPlay member can sit 30 to 55 ms behind the group after a Seek, and nothing corrects it `[fid: syncplay-member-behind-after-seek-uncorrected]` `[captured 2026-11-21]`

After some group Seeks a member plays a few tens of milliseconds behind the group and nothing corrects it, because a playing member is corrected only past `syncPlayPlayback.SKIP_TO_SYNC_MIN_MS` behind. Measured 2026-11-21 on an Ultra (Jellyfin 12.4.0 over HTTP, one film, eight group Seeks with about 29 s of play after each): after four of the eight the member sat 30 to 55 ms behind for the whole stretch, and across all readings the median was 20 ms behind. After a pause and resume the same device sat within about 12 ms. The engine released as v3.5.0 sat 85 to 117 ms behind after every one of the same Seeks, so this is not a regression.

The measurement came from outside the app: a throwaway script joined the group as a silent member to hear the server's commands, and timed the steps of the device's ECP `query/media-player` position against the group's line, on the machine that runs the server. Each reading is good to about 25 ms. The app's own drift figure cannot show this, since it rests on the app's own clock estimate. The script was not kept in the repo.

The likely place is the resume a landing ends with (the `landing` kind of resume lag in `syncPlayPlayer.learnResumeLag`), which is not confirmed. Not checked: other devices after a Seek, a transcode, a server over HTTPS, and whether a viewer notices an offset this size. Start by making the outside measurement a kept tool, then measure before changing anything. Closes with a tighter landing shown by that measurement, or a recorded no.

#### Write a library's default tab back to the server's display preferences `[fid: library-default-tab-write-back]` `[captured 2026-11-13]`

The library screens' default tab reads the web client's pick (`landing-<libraryId>`, or `landing-livetv`, in the `usersettings` display preferences, client `emby`) at sign-in, and keeps a Roku pick in the registry (`display.<libraryId>.defaultTab`, decision `library-default-tab-precedence`). Nothing is written back, so a pick made in the gear menu's Default tab entry stays on this Roku. Writing it back would make one pick follow the user across clients, but it would also change what the web opens on, so it needs a design call first: whether the Roku's pick replaces the web's or stays an override, and what the gear entry's first option then means. A write would POST the whole `DisplayPreferences` record with one key changed, as the web does. Deferred from piece 07 of the library screens work, by design.

#### Nothing deletes a removed library's saved display keys `[fid: library-display-keys-never-pruned]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

`deleteLibraryDisplaySettings()` in `source/utils/displaySettings.bs` has no caller outside its spec, on `main` too, though ADR 0058 says removing a library deletes every key under its prefix. So the sort, layout, items per row and default tab saved for a library stay in the registry after the library is gone. The grid saves them under `parentItem.Id` (`BaseGridView.applyColumns()`), which for an opened genre, studio or network is that folder's id, so each folder a viewer changes adds keys too.

Read from the code on 2026-11-23, during the audit before the library screens branch lands. Not measured: how many keys a real registry holds.

Why not now: when to prune is a design choice (at sign-in against the user's libraries is one answer), and whether a folder should save keys of its own is a second. To close: decide both, call the delete where a library is found gone, and cover it with a spec.

#### Only movie libraries offer the Genres, Parental Ratings, Years and language filters `[fid: library-filters-beyond-movies]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

Only a movie library's Filter menu offers the server's lists: Genres, Parental Ratings, Years and the two language categories. `MoviePresenter.configureLoadTask()` is the one caller of `GridPresenterBase.loadFilters()`. A TV library offers Favorites, Played and Unplayed, and music, photo, Live TV and every other library offer Favorites alone, so a viewer cannot narrow shows or albums by genre or year.

Read from the presenters' `getOptions()` on 2026-11-23, during the audit before the library screens branch lands.

Why not now: each library type needs its own choice of categories, a check of what the server answers for its item types, and captures. To close: each presenter offers the categories that suit its lists, decided for the best experience on a TV and not copied from another client, with specs for the query each builds.

#### Which tabs each library type should have is undecided past the ones it had `[fid: library-tabs-still-to-decide]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

A library's tabs are the ones JellyRock had before the redesign. The presenters' comments list tabs the web client has and JellyRock lacks: Suggestions (movies, TV, music), Upcoming and Episodes (TV), Playlists and Songs (music). A Live TV library has Channels and TV Guide only. Read from `getOptions()` in the presenters on 2026-11-23; the web client's current source was not compared.

Decide per library type which tabs earn a place. The test is the best experience on a TV, never parity with another client for its own sake: a tab that repeats a Home row or holds a list nobody browses by remote stays out. See also `music-library-favorites-tab`.

Why not now: each tab is a surface of its own, with its loading, its sorts and filters, and its captures. To close: a decision per candidate tab (build or decline, with the reason), and a followup or issue for each one to build.

#### Re-run the #1341 probe table on the installed brighterscript before the typecast trial `[fid: bsc-v1-probe-rerun-on-alpha58]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

Epic #1341's table of what BrighterScript v1 checks was measured on 1.0.0-alpha.56. The repo is on alpha.58, which adds checks the table does not list: missing observer callbacks (it found two dangling `observeField` calls in `IconButton.bs`), a warning when assigning to a member of an AA read from a node field, and optional chaining on `callFunc`. alpha.57 also stopped putting functions in an inactive `#if` branch in scope.

To close: run the epic's probe project again on the installed version, update the table in the issue, and note which rows changed. Do it before the Settings `typecast` trial, and start that trial on a release that carries brighterscript PR #1998, since `typecast` leans on the same symbol tables a statement inserted by a plugin can disturb. Not now: it belongs to the epic's first step, not to the dependency bump.

### api

#### Tier-1 API task loops (`ApiTask.runApiLoop` / `ApiQueueTask.runQueueLoop`) are persistent continuous-server loops that die permanently on an uncaught error, silently stalling the pool for the session — the same failure mode #744 fixed for `SideEffectTask` via per-request `try/catch`. Consider adding equivalent per-request isolation to the Tier-1 loops. This is pre-existing (not introduced by #744) and deferred to keep #744 scoped. `[fid: api-task-loops-die-permanently]` `[captured 2026-07-25]`

#### Nine of `ItemDetails`' extras requests build their params in `components/extras/LoadExtrasRowsTask.bs`, where the API usage manifest cannot see them. `[fid: extras-params-hidden-from-api-manifest]` `[captured 2026-09-15]`

Its request-field scan reads `source/api/` only, so a Jellyfin change to those fields would not be flagged by server-upgrade triage. The nine are `buildRowRequest`'s `likeThis`, `artistSimilar`, `seasons`, episodes, `boxSetItems`, `playlistItems`, `photoAlbumItems`, `channelPrograms` and `personVideosRequest`. Not a regression — the same params lived in `components/home/LoadItemsTask.bs` on `main`; #922 kept the four music requests in the API layer (`BuildArtistAlbumsRequest` & co. in `source/api/items.bs`) and left the rest. **Fix shape:** move those nine param sets into builders beside the music ones, then confirm with the regenerated manifest that the request-field list only grows.

#### Pool widths above 6 are untested for server load. `[fid: pool-width-above-6-untested]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

[ADR 0036](adr/0036-api-pool-width-by-device-class.md) stops the non-low-memory width at 6: 8 beat 6 by only 0–7 % (never significant), and above 6 the app sends more concurrent requests than jellyfin-web does over plain HTTP. **Before going wider:** measure server response time under that load on a weak server (e.g. a CPU-limited Jellyfin container), then re-run width 6 vs 8 at n ≥ 30 per arm.

#### Dispatching the visible rows' requests first might let low-memory devices run a wider pool. `[fid: dispatch-visible-rows-first-wider-pool]` `[captured 2026-09-16]`

ADR 0036 gives them width 4 because 6 and 8 delayed Home's first paint ~12 % at +150 ms there; the mechanism is unmeasured (a guess: more concurrent responses competing with the visible rows on a slow CPU). A priority lane in `ApiQueueTask` for above-the-fold rows is the untested idea.

#### A live channel whose server probe outlasts the API wait fails its first start and leaks the server's live stream (found with the live HLS direct-play fix; pre-existing on every server version). `[fid: live-probe-outlasts-api-wait-leaks-stream]` `[captured 2026-09-21]`

Opening a live stream makes the server probe it inside `PlaybackInfo` (a 3 s delay, then `ffprobe`; `AddMediaInfoWithProbe`, the same on 10.11). `fetchRes` gives up at `timeouts.API_WAIT_MS`, so a slow probe shows "error retrieving playback", and because JellyRock never got the `LiveStreamId` it cannot close the stream the server goes on to open. Measured 2026-09-21 on 12.1 with Apple's HLS example as an `M3U` channel: a 20.65 s probe, the stream still held 11 min later, and later starts joining it without a probe. A held stream takes a slot on a tuner with a "Simultaneous stream limit". **Agreed fix shape (user call, 2026-09-21):** let a request carry its own timeout and have `fetchRes` honor it, keeping the HTTP layer the first to time out, and give live `PlaybackInfo` a longer one. **The first half shipped with #1043 (library grid slow servers):** a request AA's `timeoutMs` sets its HTTP limit and every wait on it follows (`apiTimeout.waitMs` — `fetchRes`, `fetchAsync`), so what is left is setting a longer `timeoutMs` on the live `PlaybackInfo` request and choosing its value. It is a POST, so the pool never skips or stops it once its caller leaves — which is what this needs, since only a delivered answer carries the `LiveStreamId` to close. Not an automatic retry: it would join the leaked stream and leave it open. Related: #988.

#### The API pool's own render-thread crossings slow EVERY Task while the render thread is busy. `[fid: api-pool-render-thread-crossings]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

Measured 2026-09-21 on a Stick 4K while the TV guide scrolled at 400 ms per row: a ~70 ms channel fetch through `fetchRes` took 2.5–8.8 s, and a Task's first few `m.top` / `m.global` reads before it took up to 1.4 s — each crossing waits on the animating grid ([`threading.md`](architecture/threading.md#measured-findings)). The guide's own per-item crossings are fixed; this is the shared remainder. `fetchRes` appends a result node to the render-owned coordinator and observes it per request; find which of those crossings dominate before designing anything.

The cost shows on an idle screen too, as a fixed price per request. Measured 2026-10-18 (median of 5 cold runs, `npm run measure -- --measurement screen-load --nav search --variant query`): search reading 15 requests instead of 7, for the same 129 items ("the" on a 10.11 server), settled 221 ms later on the 512 MB Stick (pool width 4); on the Stick 4K (width 6, a 12.1 server) "the" went 978 to 1207 ms. The server accounts for about 50 to 65 ms of it (13 against 5 concurrent `/Items` searches from the dev machine, at width 4 and 6), so roughly 20 ms per request is spent on the device. Not yet split by crossing; this entry's two candidates (the coordinator's result node and observer, `api-slot-http-wait-event-driven`) are where to look. Search pays it on every query since #1216 (one read per type), and Home pays it per row.

#### The API pool slot's HTTP wait crosses to the render thread on every pass — replace it with an event-driven wait (#1043 follow-up; user call 2026-09-25). `[fid: api-slot-http-wait-event-driven]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

`rr_Requests_run` (roku-requests) polls `GetMessage()` without sleeping and reads `m.top.quit` on each pass, and the slot's node is render-owned, so every pass is a rendezvous. Measured 2026-09-25 on the 512 MB Stick, 60 requests, throwaway counters: 98.7% of the loop's time was inside that read — ~270 µs per pass against a fast local server, and up to 469 ms for a single read while Home was building. It is one named candidate for the crossings in the followup above. #1043 stops a long request whose caller has gone by ADDING a `quit` field to the slot for the length of the stop (`apiSlotQuit` / `apiSlotClearQuit`), not by declaring one: a declared field costs more to read on every pass (measured 2026-09-25, 512 MB Stick, local 10.11 server, 4 alternating rounds of 5 Home launches, 300 requests per arm: 6.9 ms against 3.7 ms median per read, and more total request wait in all 4 rounds, +3% to +28%). The replacement waits on one port for the transfer's `roUrlEvent` or a `quit` change, keeping the stop and dropping the per-pass crossing for every request in the app; with no per-pass read left, `quit` can be declared on `ApiTask` again, which an observer needs. It also takes the stop over from `roku-requests`, whose quit check reads `m.cancel_and_return` instead of its local variable, so today a stop ends through the timeout path and is one cancel only because the pool passes no `retryCount` (the note in `ApiTask.xml`). **Measure before and after** (render-thread time across a Home load and grid scrolling): it changes the path every request takes, which is why it is its own PR.

#### Video `PlaybackInfo` may be rejected outright by Jellyfin 10.7 `[fid: playbackinfo-rejected-jellyfin-10-7]` `[captured 2026-10-01]`

(found 2026-10-01, not yet seen in the app). POSTing the device profile a Roku Ultra builds to the 10.7.7 test server returns HTTP 400, `$.DeviceProfile.CodecProfiles[34].Conditions[1].Property ... could not be converted to MediaBrowser.Model.Dlna.ProfileConditionValue`, which is the `VideoRangeType` condition on the `h264` codec profile. 10.7 has no `VideoRangeType` (`docs/dev/jellyfin-server-versioning.md`), and nothing found strips it for `apiVersion` 1. Identical with and without the TrueHD/DTS fix. **Next step:** play any video against the 10.7.7 test server in the app; if it fails, drop `VideoRangeType` conditions for 10.7, the way `dropConditionsUnevaluableOnAudioFiles` does for audio files.

#### A promise rejection from a transport failure has no `reason`, so 26 `.catch` handlers log INVALID `[fid: promise-rejection-missing-reason]` `[captured 2026-10-09]`

Found 2026-10-09 while rewriting `docs/dev/promises.md` (docs-voice, Phase C) .

**Root cause:** `fetchAsync` rejects with two shapes. Only the adapter-built error (`buildApiPromiseError()`, used for a timeout and for an unavailable pool) carries `reason`. When the pool's own response comes back with `statusCode` 0 or below, or with no `statusCode`, `settleApiPromiseIn()` in `source/api/apiPromise.bs` rejects with that response itself, which has no `reason`. All 26 `.catch` handlers log `err.reason`: `ItemDetails` 8, `SubtitlePanel` 7, `UserSelect` 5, `BaseGridView` 2, `VideoPlayerView` 2, `Home` 1, `schedule` 1. The logger prints a missing value as `INVALID`, so a network failure's cause is lost. Nothing branches on `reason`; every use is a log argument. The form dates from #624 (2026-06-06) and was copied by every later conversion to `fetchAsync`.

**Options:**

- `reason` (recommended): before `settleApiPromiseIn()` rejects with the pool's response, set `res.reason = apiResponse.jsonFailure(res)` ("network error -28", "not sent", "timed out", "abandoned"), so every rejection carries a `reason`. Add a case to `tests/source/unit/api/apiPromise.spec.bs` that asserts the transport-failure rejection's `reason`. Then fix the "What lands where" text in `docs/dev/promises.md`, which says `reason` can be missing. The 26 handlers stay as they are. This fixes the contract in one place and needs low upkeep. Risk: it changes `source/api/` (a shared mechanism), so run the spec on a Roku with `test:tdd`.
- `callers`: add `err.statusCode` to all 26 log calls. This doesn't fix the contract, and new callers would copy the old form.
- `helper`: add `apiPromiseFailureText(err)` and change all 26 sites to use it. This makes a second way to read a rejection.

Filed `later` because it is a runtime change in the API layer, which should not ride in the docs-only voice PR.

**Not checked:**

- I haven't seen the `INVALID` line on a device.
- My earlier count of 27 included a comment in `apiPromise.bs`; the real count is 26.

#### The API pool's caller watch drops every observer of a Task's `state`; measure whether the scoped pair isolates it on device `[fid: api-pool-watch-scoped-observer]` `[captured 2026-10-10]` `[pinned]`

`ApiQueueTask`'s caller watch (`watchCaller()` / `forgetCaller()`) subscribes with `observeField("state", m.port)` and unsubscribes with `unobserveField("state")`, which drops every observer of the Task's `state`. It is safe only because nothing else observes a Task's `state` (the task ledger reads it instead). Rechecked 2026-10-09 by grepping every `observeField("state"`: the only other observed Task is `TrickplayCarousel`'s `tileLoader`, which does not use the pool.

ADR 0043 ruled out the scoped pair (`observeFieldScopedEx` / `unobserveFieldScoped`) as needing Roku OS 12. That reason is gone since #1126 raised the floor to Roku OS 15.1. The true reason now is that scoped isolation is unproven on device: [`components/CLAUDE.md`](../components/CLAUDE.md) records `unobserveFieldScoped` removing a parent's observer (#898).

**Work:** an on-device record test of this exact case (Task thread, message port, `observeFieldScopedEx` on another Task's `state`, plus a second plain observer from a render component; `unobserveFieldScoped`; does the other observer still fire?). If isolation holds, swap the two calls and drop the invariant; if not, keep the code and record why. Not now because it needs a new test component and device runs, and it ships as its own PR.

First filed 2026-10-07 in `89c8185c`, which never reached `main` (PR #1126 merged without it). The docs-voice project states the true reason in [`api.md`](architecture/api.md#a-long-request-already-on-a-slot) and the `forgetCaller()` comment.

#### Some remote `.strm` items fail on every play because the server's `PlaybackInfo` outlasts the 10 s request limit `[fid: strm-first-play-probe-outlasts-10s]` `[captured 2026-10-12]` `[pinned]`

Some remote `.strm` items fail on every play with "There was an error retrieving playback information from the server": their `PlaybackInfo` takes longer than the app's default request limit (`timeouts.HTTP_MS`, 10 s). `LoadItems_AddVideoContent` in `LoadVideoContentTask` gives only a live item its own limit (`openLimitMs`).

Evidence (2026-10-12): on a device (`.176` and `.177`, Jellyfin 12.1), `.strm` items pointing at a public HLS stream failed with `[http] Timeout= 10000`, then `Event Timed Out` and `PlaybackInfo failed cause timed out isLive false`, on a first play and on a later one. With curl, every `PlaybackInfo` for those items took 10.4 to 11.5 s on 12.1 and 10.7 to 12.0 s on 10.11, before and after the server had saved their streams (44.6 s once while a library scan ran). The same stream answered a plain GET in 0.6 s from inside both server containers, so the time is the server's own work. A `.strm` item served from the LAN answered `PlaybackInfo` in under 0.3 s and played.

Options weighed (`unprobed-limit` was recommended, then disproved: it assumed only the first play was slow):

- A per-source limit for remote `.strm` sources (an `Http` source whose `Path` is not the server's), sized from measurements. The `PLAYBACK_START` app wait already shows "Still loading…". Back is the open design: the pool never stops a POST, so the slot stays held up to the limit. That is the third part the `screens-outgrowing-10s-request-limit` rule asks for.
- `all-playback-info`: a longer limit for every non-live `PlaybackInfo`. Rejected: a dead server would take that long to report on every Play.
- `retry-once`: ask again after a timeout. Rejected: every request for the source is slow, so a retry fails the same way.

Not checked: what the server spends the 10 s on (log lines show a metadata refresh for the item finishing as the answer arrives; not confirmed as the cause); whether it depends on the stream (a public HLS stream with 14 streams, against a local one with 2); how long the server takes at most; versions 10.7 to 10.10.

Not done now because the Back and pool-slot design belongs with the `failure-not-empty` project's slow-server work, and the cause should be found first.

#### `AudioStream()` and `setupVideoContentWithAuth()` can send the text `null` in stream URL params `[fid: stream-url-params-send-null]` `[captured 2026-10-13]`

`items.AudioStream()` passes `mediaSources[0].container` and `.id` straight into `params`, and `LoadVideoContentTask.setupVideoContentWithAuth()` passes `PlaySessionId` and `Container`, which can be `invalid`. `buildParams` turns `invalid` into `k=null` (tech-debt `buildparams-no-array-support` records that behavior but says no caller sends such values; these two can). Found by the docs-voice comment pass, checked against the code 2026-10-13. Close it by guarding the fields (omit a missing one) and adding a spec case for each caller.

#### Upstream watch: server keeping not-yet-started series out of Next Up again `[fid: jellyfin-next-up-first-episode-filter]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: Jellyfin restoring a server-side way to keep the first episode of a series with no played regular episode out of `GET /Shows/NextUp`: a new query parameter, or `DisableFirstEpisode` acting again. jellyfin/jellyfin#13687 removed the filter in 10.11; its report, jellyfin/jellyfin#13743, was closed by the stale bot

Last upstream version seen: Jellyfin `v12.2`: Next Up takes no filter parameter (checked 2026-10-06). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `jellyfin-next-up-first-episode-filter` row.

Where JellyRock stands: Home's Next Up drops those episodes client-side wherever the request does not ask the server to (`nextUpNeedsStartedFilter()`: 10.8-10.10 with Max Days on, and every 10.11+ server), at one extra round of lookups on Home's first paint (measured 2026-10-06 on a Stick 4K against 12.1: +377 ms). Decision `next-up-started-series-filter`

When it moves: verify the new filter with `npm run jellyfin:matrix`, register it as a version-gated parameter (decision `version-gated-query-parameters`), send it from `buildHomeNextUpParams()`, and recognize it in `nextUpNeedsStartedFilter()` so those servers skip the client lookups

#### Upstream watch: companion plugin's Jellyfin server ABI pin (#667) `[fid: jellyrock-plugin-server-abi]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: new Jellyfin **server** lines (esp. the **12.0 RC**) against the out-of-tree companion plugin `jellyfin-plugin-jellyrock`, whose `Jellyfin.Controller` / `Jellyfin.Model` package refs + `targetAbi` track the server line, not a stable SDK

Last upstream version seen: 10.11.11 (checked 2026-07-13). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `jellyrock-plugin-server-abi` row.

Where JellyRock stands: pinned to 10.11.11. The plugin's session-controller + long-poll endpoint use `ISessionManager` / `SessionInfo` / `ISessionController` surfaces that can shift between server minors (for example `EnsureController`, `SessionInfo.SupportsRemoteControl`); the plugin's own CI matrix is the primary breakage signal, this entry is the reminder on the app side

When it moves: re-pin the plugin's `Jellyfin.Controller`/`Jellyfin.Model` + `build.yaml` `targetAbi` to the new line, rebuild in the SDK container, and re-verify the cast and closed app liveness gate on a test server (12.0 RC restructures the API: check `ISessionController` / `SessionInfo` shapes)

#### Nobody has checked whether Roku's FLAC seek defect also hits FLAC in an Ogg container, which the workaround skips `[fid: ogg-flac-seek-unchecked]` `[captured 2026-10-21]`

The FLAC seek workaround (#662, ADR 0052) marks a stream `isFlac` only when its container is `flac` (`AudioStream` in `source/api/items.bs`). The device profile also direct-plays an `ogg` container probed for `flac` (`deviceCapabilities.bs`), so FLAC audio in an Ogg file seeks natively, with no server stream, and nobody has checked whether Roku's short-last-frame seek defect hits it too. Found in the PR #1234 review, 2026-10-21.

Close it on a device: seek to 50% in an Ogg file holding FLAC whose last frame is shorter than its frame size. If it stalls before the end like a `.flac` does, extend the check to the audio codec and cover it in `audio-seek.spec.js`; if it ends cleanly, record that here and drop the entry. Why not now: it needs such a fixture, which the demo server does not have.

#### `fetchAsync` overwrites a pending request that reuses its `requestId`, so the first promise never settles and the second can settle early `[fid: fetchasync-reused-requestid-orphans-promise]` `[captured 2026-10-28]` `[pinned]`

**Root cause:** `fetchAsync()` in `source/api/apiPromise.bs` keys its pending registry (`m.__apiPromisePending`) by the caller's `requestId` alone and overwrites an existing entry without checking. The result node and the timeout timer are tagged with that id, and the handlers find the entry through `event.getNode()`. So a second request under an id still pending (1) leaves the first promise with no entry, so it never settles and a `promises.all` over it hangs; (2) leaves the first `isDone` observer in place, which on firing settles the second entry from the second result node's `result`, usually still `invalid`, so the second promise rejects as `"timeout"` before its own answer arrives; (3) leaves the first timer running, which on firing abandons the second request in the pool through `timeoutApiPromiseIn()`.

**History:** in place since `f712a9d3` (#624, the promises adoption); never fixed and undone. It already bit once: `fe48a073` fixed the SyncPlay menu's double OK, where a second group-list fetch under the same id left the first wait open and the screen blank, by giving that caller unique ids and a guard. Callers work around it one by one (`VideoPlayerView.fetchNextEpisode()` says "a reused `requestId` is a pool-contract bug").

**Depends on it:** about 35 `fetchAsync` call sites. Several use an id that can repeat while pending: `"favoriteToggle-" + item.id` and `"watchedToggle-" + item.id` (an `isLoading` guard protects them), `"filters-" + parentid`, `"libraryBackdrop-" + libraryId`, `"nextEpisode-" + m.top.id`, the five `"subtitle…-" + m.top.itemId` ids, `"quickConnectEnabled"`, and `SyncPlayManager.fetchQueue()`'s `"syncplay-queue-<lastUpdateMs>-<page>"` into `promises.all`. The RTA fault injection (`apiFaults.take` in `ApiQueueTask`) matches on the `requestId` the pool sees, so a fix keeps that string. `tests/source/unit/api/apiPromise.spec.bs` has no case for two requests under one id.

**Caller audit (2026-10-27, read from the code):** one caller is exposed with no guard: `schedule.loadProgramDetails()` uses `"liveTvProgram-" + programId` and marks a program loaded only on success, so focusing program A, then B, then A again while the first A is pending reuses the id; the first A's observer then settles the second as a `"timeout"` and the details pane does not fill on that focus. `SyncPlayManager.fetchQueue()` is exposed only if a queue update repeats a `lastUpdateMs`. The rest are guarded by hand: `isLoading` (favorite, watched), `GridPresenterBase`'s `filtersInFlightFor` (its comment names this flaw), `libraryBackdropPendingId`, `checkedForNextEpisode`, `claimMutation()` in `SubtitlePanel`, and once-per-mount calls; the rest use a sequence number.

**Options:**

- `unique` (recommended): `fetchAsync` makes a private registry key from a per-`m` counter (`requestId + "#" + n`) and tags the result node and timer with it; the pool, logs, fault injection and error AAs keep the caller's `requestId`. Each request settles on its own. `apiPromise.bs` plus one spec case. Risk: anything that reads a result node's or timer's `id` as the caller's id sees the new key; grep that nothing outside `apiPromise.bs` does.
- `supersede`: on a collision, reject the earlier promise with `reason: "superseded"` and tear it down first. Every caller's `.catch` fires on an ordinary double press, which can toast errors.
- `refuse`: reject the new call at once as `"duplicate"`. A caller that retries or re-fetches under the same id gets a rejection instead of data.
- `warn`: a debug-build print on a collision. Fixes nothing.

**Close it:** build `unique` test first (a spec that submits two requests under one id and asserts both settle with their own result), run it on a Roku with `npm run test:tdd`, then drop the caller-side workarounds' comments that call this a contract bug only if they become wrong. Filed rather than fixed during SyncPlay phase D because it is a shared-mechanism change outside that work.

**Not checked:** whether any of those callers fires twice under the same id while the first is pending (for example, `fetchQueue` seeing the same `lastUpdateMs` twice through a re-follow); the early settle of the second promise by the leftover observer is from reading the code, not a device run.

**Tracked in** [#1272](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/issues/1272); close it with the fix.

#### Out-of-spec Dolby Vision `mp4` files may still direct-play into Dolby Vision mode on 10.9 and 10.10 `[fid: dovi-out-of-spec-mp4-before-1011]` `[captured 2026-11-06]`

Jellyfin 10.9 and 10.10 report a Dolby Vision profile 8 stream with compatibility ID 6 as `DOVIWithHDR10`, which the HEVC range list already takes, so `allowHdr10DoviInvalid` (`source/api/items.bs`, MKV only since PR #1325) never sees it and an `mp4` file of it direct-plays. On 10.11.11 the same file tagged `hvc1` played with the Roku in Dolby Vision mode, carrying the out-of-spec configuration (measured 2026-11-05 on a Roku Ultra `4850X`, decision `dovi-invalid-hdr10-direct-play`), so it most likely does the same on 10.9 and 10.10, as it did before PR #1325. A `dvh1`-tagged one is repackaged on every server from 10.9 by `refuseDvh1DolbyVision`; that conversion keeps the Dolby Vision data in the video, and on 10.9 and 10.10 no server code strips it.

Check first: play the compatibility ID 6 `hvc1` and `dvh1` clips under `dovi-invalid/` in the test servers' Testing library from 10.9.11 and 10.10.7 on a Roku with a Dolby Vision TV, and read `Video.streamingSegment.hdrModeStr`. If either plays in Dolby Vision mode and the picture is wrong, or fails, have those requests drop the Dolby Vision range types for a stream `isOutOfSpecHdr10Dovi` marks, as `refuseDvh1DolbyVision` does.

Not checked: any of this on 10.9 or 10.10 on a device; whether a Roku shows the out-of-spec Dolby Vision with the right colors.

### tests

#### Re-derive what actually reddened PR #800 — the device-contention explanation is REFUTED. `[fid: pr-800-red-cause-rederive]` `[captured 2026-08-10]`

An ECP sweep on 2026-08-10 found three Roku devices: CI drives `.200` (org secret `ROKU_DEVICE_IP`, unmodified since 2026-03-18, read by both device workflows and RTA), local dev drives `.177`. A `hardRelaunch()` on `.177` cannot reach `.200`, so the original reading — a local `npm run test:rta` disturbing CI's device — cannot be what happened. The failing test was `SessionManagement.spec.bs` → "connects to Jellyfin stable demo server", and the only resource both parties genuinely shared was `demo.jellyfin.org`. Two candidates remain, undistinguished: contention on the shared demo account, or plain flake (the diagnosis rested on a temporal correlation plus one passing re-run). **Experiment:** run `npm run test:rta` locally against `.177` while dispatching a CI device run on `.200`, and see whether CI reddens — server contention if it does, flake if it doesn't. If it is the server, the thing to lock is the demo account, not the device. Do not re-add a CI-yield check to `device-lock.js` before this is settled.

#### Expand RTA functional-test screen coverage beyond the current 6 (`userSelect`/`home`/`libraryGrid`/`movieDetails`/`osd`/`trickplay`) to more screens, and make screenshot capture the default for RTA runs (currently `RTA_CAPTURE=1` opt-in) so every covered screen yields a store image. From #642. *(Largely addressed by #621: coverage grew 6→23 website-gallery screens; the "every screen → store image" clause is superseded by `rta-screenshot-store-website-split`.)* `[fid: rta-screen-coverage-expand-beyond-six]` `[captured 2026-06-10]`

#### Remaining RTA gallery screens after #621 (`personDetails`/`seasonDetails`/`episodeDetails`/`audioDetails` are now done via the `openChildDetailByRowType` content-based row helper). Content-blocked on the richer custom server (zero demo content): `BoxSet`, `Photo`/`PhotoAlbum`, `MusicVideo`, Live TV, OSD per-button dialogs, the non-cast extras rows (trailers/special features/similar), and the `Networks` TV view (the demo's single series has no network — captured as its empty "No Items" state for now). `[fid: rta-gallery-screens-remaining]` `[captured 2026-06-11]`

#### The `ws://` `DeviceId` session binding has no end-to-end gate `[fid: ws-deviceid-session-binding-ungated]` `[captured 2026-07-30]`

(#743 follow-up): RTA drives the `https://` demo server (`tests/rta/config.js`), but the `ws://` receiver only runs against an `http://` server (`remoteProtocol.isHttpServer`), so RTA takes the long-poll branch and never exercises the socket at all. The real binding was verified manually against a local 10.11.11 server using a token deliberately minted under a mismatched `DeviceId` (PR #747); `scripts/lint/socket-auth-binding-check.js` now gates only the SOURCE SHAPE those runs validated. Closing the gap needs RTA to target a local `http://` Jellyfin, which conflicts with the demo server's license-clear screenshot role — so the open question is whether a second RTA target is worth standing up. **2026-08-06: promote this to its own tracked project (`/start-project`) rather than growing it as a bullet — it now has FOUR independent drivers and carries a real decision (second RTA target vs. re-pointing the existing one) that reads like an ADR.** Driver 2, from PR #770: the public demo server cannot exercise the `View All` row affordance at all — it renders only above 5 items in a genre, and measured against `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` on 2026-08-06 the maxima are 4 (movies, 14 genres), 5 (music, 3 genres) and 1 (`tvshows`, 3 genres), so `populateGenreRow`'s View All + `collectionType`-inference block is unreachable by any automated test on that fixture. Driver 3: the fixture keeps shrinking out from under us (it reset away the playlists library, and the vacuous-assertion rule now in `tests/rta/CLAUDE.md` exists because of it), so content-dependent coverage is capped by a server we do not control. **Driver 4, from the #288 phase-3 dialog PR (2026-08-22): the cast-message dialog has ZERO functional coverage for the same structural reason — showing it needs a `GeneralCommand{DisplayMessage}` from a real sender, and the only route to one is the `ws://` receiver this entry is already about.** That PR's new `JRDialog` subheading slot could only be seen on a device by hand-patching a throwaway build to give a REACHABLE dialog a subheading; it ships gated by unit specs and an eyeballed capture, with nothing driving the real flow. This driver is **narrower** than a second RTA target, though, and worth checking first: Jellyfin's `POST /Sessions/{id}/Message` reaches the session over `https://` too, so a session-message helper in `tests/rta/lib/jellyfin.js` may close it without any fixture work — and it would be the first end-to-end exercise of `remoteDispatch` either way. **Driver 5, from the #288 phase-4 playback-info PR (2026-08-25): the report's richest paths cannot be rendered on this fixture at all.** The rebuilt playback-info report shows Dolby Vision profile labels, spatial-audio format, bit-depth conversion and a scrolling body — and `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` serves a single 1080p `h264` / stereo AAC file, so every one of those rows is either absent or trivially short in the only capture anyone can take. The transcoding half had to be forced by seeding a 1 Mbps bitrate cap, which exercises the code path but not the CONTENT: nobody has seen a Dolby Vision / TrueHD report render, and the two defects this PR shipped-then-fixed were both found by looking at a screenshot. This driver is about fixture *richness* rather than protocol reach, which is a different axis from drivers 1 and 4 and argues the same way. A local seeded fixture is achievable license-clear — the demo server itself runs on public-domain film (Dracula, Night of the Living Dead, The Great Train Robbery) plus Blender's freely-licensed open-movie shorts; the problem is control, not sourcing. Not scaffolding it now — this bullet is the capture.

#### RTA playback specs cannot run on the Ultra `.178`: a runtime deep-link cast does not navigate to the player, so nothing ever plays. `[fid: rta-playback-specs-cannot-run-ultra]` `[captured 2026-08-29]` `[pinned]`

Narrowed on 2026-08-26 — the symptom LOOKS like "the OSD never opens" (`nav timed out waiting for osd visible`), but a probe that waits blind instead of gating on ECP shows the app still on Home: focus is `#overhang`, `#osd` DOES NOT EXIST, and `/query/media-player` reads `close`. No player is mounted, so there is no OSD to open. **This may be a real user-facing bug rather than a harness one** — it is the runtime deep-link path (`main.bs`'s `roInputEvent` branch → `stashDeepLink` → `onRuntimeDeepLink`), which is how a cast from another Jellyfin client starts playback. **INTERMITTENT**: earlier runs the same day DID reach `PlayerHostView`, and it reproduces on BOTH the demo server and a healthy LAN server, so it is neither fixture content nor server load. **Not the branch** — a control run of the commit before the #859 review round failed identically, and that same commit passed 8/8 on `.177` the same morning. **Start here, do not re-derive:** four explanations were tried and all four disproved by measurement — a hidden dialog swallowing keys (focus was on `VideoPlayerView`), a buffering stall (`player=play`), `stateAllowsOSD()` refusing (`videoNode=playing`, which it accepts), and a flaky demo server (reproduces on a local server too). Failure records now carry `player=` and `videoNode=`; read them first.
