Untested candidates: whether the cast is ignored by the server-match branch in `onRuntimeDeepLink` (a mismatch stashes or toasts rather than playing), whether the ECP input arrives at all, and whether `hardRelaunch` races the app's readiness to accept one. Worth an issue if it reproduces by hand.

**A second shape, 2026-11-10, with a player mounted.** Two runs of `syncplay-controls`, `syncplay-correction`, `syncplay-reload` and `syncplay-menu` on the Ultra (Roku OS 16.0.4) against a local 12.4 server, one with the SyncPlay shadow hooks and one at the commit before them, failed the same 15 cases. Each is a key press during playback that does not reach the player: Play, Right, Up and Replay, sent by `press()` in `tests/rta/lib/steps.js`. Examples: `nav timed out waiting for the trick-play bar up after three Right presses (last=false)` and `waiting for the group paused on this member's Play key (last="Playing")`. Unlike the shape above, the player is there: the failure dump reads `view=PlayerHostView`, focus on `VideoPlayerView`, `state: playing`, `player: play`, `isRemoteDisabled: false`. The 36 cases that pass move the player by group commands, and the menu cases that walk the overhang with keys pass, so ECP keys work outside playback. Root cause not confirmed. Ruled out: the shadow hooks (the control fails the same way) and the merge of `main` before the runs (it changed no app code). The same specs passed 49 with 1 skipped on a Stick 4K (Roku OS 15.3.4) the same morning; no record of them ever being run on the Ultra was found.

Options: `remote` (the operator presses Up and Play on the Ultra's own remote during any video in the dev build and reports what happens, which splits "the app ignores keys on this device" from "ECP keys behave differently there"); `probe` (run one playback spec outside SyncPlay on the Ultra and the same four specs on the Stick 4K, then add throwaway key logging if it is still unclear). Recommended: `remote`. Pinned 2026-11-10 at the operator's `ok`: if the real remote is affected this is an app bug on that device.

Not checked: whether the physical remote works during playback on the Ultra; whether playback specs outside SyncPlay fail the same way there; whether it is the OS version or the model; whether the same specs still pass on the Stick 4K today.

**The low-end Stick too, 2026-11-13, outside any group.** In plain playback of a direct-play movie, ECP Right, Right, Fwd, Left and OK each did nothing: no scrub bar, no OSD, the node stayed `playing`, and the Back after each did not leave the player (a throwaway probe; its spec is in the SyncPlay project's spikes). So the second shape is not the Ultra's alone, and not specific to SyncPlay. The same keys reached the player on the Stick 4K the same day. Still not checked: the physical remote on either device.

**The low-end Stick, 2026-11-17: the physical remote works, and the OS takes the focus at playback start.** The operator confirmed that on the low-end Stick the remote's Up opens the OSD during playback, and that nothing is drawn over the picture. A throwaway probe (local 12.4 server, outside any group) sent single presses through `press()` with the video playing, the position advancing and focus on the player: 0 of 120 (20 each of Up, OK, Right, Left, Play and Back) and 0 of 24 in earlier runs reached it. Temporary prints in `VideoPlayerView.onKeyEvent` and `JRScene.onKeyEvent` printed nothing during playback, while the same print fired for presses on Home. In each of those playbacks whose console was kept, Roku sent the app an `roDeviceInfoEvent` with `appFocused: false` as the video started (Roku's docs: "the System Overlay takes focus"), and `appFocused: true` only at exit; in the one playback without that event, every press arrived. Sending each key as a separate press and release changed nothing. So on that device it is not the app: something in the OS takes the focus at playback start and keeps network-sent keys from the app. What takes it, and whether the Ultra's failures share the cause, is not known. Member-driven SyncPlay cases run there (80 of 80 steps, 2026-11-17).

#### Exercise `scripts/capture-screenshots.js` and `tests/rta/demos/run.mjs` on hardware against the new `lib/registry.js` snapshot/restore. `[fid: capture-screenshots-demos-registry-hardware]` `[captured 2026-08-10]`

Both were migrated off the removed `snapshotSession`/`restoreSession` pair and their imports verified statically, but neither was RUN this session — a screenshot matrix is ~15 min × 5 locales and the demo runner needs an operator at its stop gate. They keep the in-process `armRestoreOnInterrupt` path, which is correct for main-process scripts (nothing else installs an exiting SIGINT handler there) but is now the only interrupt path not proven end-to-end; `scripts/rta-run.js`'s parent-owned path was verified on `.178` 2026-08-10.

#### `scripts/capture-screenshots.js` has the same missing-library exposure the RTA spec just fixed. `[fid: capture-screenshots-missing-library]` `[captured 2026-08-03]`

It loops the shared `tests/rta/screens.js` registry and calls `libraryIdFor(libraries, screen.view.collectionType)`, which returns null when the server has no such library — then `screen.nav(ctx)` throws and `captureScreen`'s 3-attempt retry burns three full seed+relaunch cycles per locale before giving up. Every `vw(...)` screen is `capture.eligible`, so `playlistsLibrary`/`playlistDetails` hit this today. Deliberately NOT folded into the RTA fix: the right behavior differs (a store/gallery run losing a screen should probably warn loudly rather than skip quietly), and a ~15-min matrix run against real store assets is not something the RTA change verified. Fix shape: reuse the same null-library check, skip the screen, and log a visible warning so the gallery set's shrinkage is never silent.

#### The demo server's hourly reset is a candidate cause for WHOLE-SUITE flake, not just the two screens it was filed against. `[fid: demo-server-hourly-reset-suite-flake]` `[captured 2026-08-11]`

The reset itself is already recorded (2026-08-02, above: `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` resets hourly, which is why `playlistsLibrary`/`playlistDetails` are content-flaky) — what was never drawn from it is the consequence for a full run. A `npm run test:rta` pass is ~13 min (13.6 and 13.7 min measured on `.177`, 2026-08-10), so any run starting after roughly 46 minutes past the hour has the reset land **mid-run**, and that surfaces as an unrelated-looking nav/lookup timeout rather than as an obvious fixture error. **Precision, because the original wording implied a seeding step that does not exist:** the suite seeds only the *registry* (`lib/seed.js` — which server/user to sign into); it makes no server-side writes beyond auth (`postJson` is called once, from `authenticate`). What the reset actually changes underneath a run is the demo server's *own* content (playlists have come and gone) plus state the run creates *through the app* — the watched toggle in `dialogs.spec.js`, resume points from `deeplink.spec.js`. The mechanism holds; "its seeded state" was the wrong name for it. That makes it a named, testable candidate for [#789](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/issues/789)'s intermittent `screens.spec.js` failures, and a third candidate for #785's *"four back-to-back full suites degraded into unrelated failures"* alongside cumulative polling load. **It does not explain #800** — that window (15:45–15:52 UTC) never crossed the top of the hour, and there is now a unit test asserting exactly that, so this entry cannot quietly hand that open investigation a cause it does not have. **The cheap first probe now exists** ([#803](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/pull/803)): `scripts/rta-run.js` records each run's wall-clock window in `out/rta/run-meta.json`, flags a run that crosses the top of the hour, and stamps `afterHourBoundary` on every captured failure — which also gave that file the reader its "written by four entry points, read by nothing" debt asked for. So this is now a **measurable** question rather than a speculative one, and answering it falls out of the Phase-3 flake baseline rather than needing its own experiment. Working around a fixture that resets under you is recurring effort that belongs on the self-hosted-fixture project's ledger, next to its existing three drivers — the demo server is still the right call today at ~98% and it is the only license-clear source for store screenshots.

#### The `restoreRegistry` re-mint fix landed for the password-LESS case only — the password-protected branch still needs its own outcome. `[fid: restore-registry-remint-password-protected]` `[captured 2026-08-11]`

The convergence half shipped 2026-08-11 (above; rationale in [`restore-compares-credentials-by-presence`](decisions.md)); what follows is the part that did not, kept here with the measurement that produced it. **First, the trigger condition as originally recorded was too broad**, and the narrower one is what changes the remaining fix. That original wording said anything forcing a re-login — *"an expired token, a seed that switches user"* — trips it *"every time"*. Measured on `.177` 2026-08-11 during #803 hardware verification: `npm run demo -- server-switch` forces a full re-login (casts to the unstable demo server, re-authenticates there, plays) and its restore **converged**. The real trigger is narrower: **the app must re-authenticate for the user+server the snapshot's ACTIVE session belongs to**, because that is what invalidates the snapshot's token server-side. A login to a *different* server writes a different user section and leaves the active token valid. Confirmed by a targeted probe rather than inferred: planting a bad token in the active user's section (`JellyRock.active_user`) and cold booting re-minted it (`ce312603…` → `f717435b…`), after which `restoreRegistry` failed all 3 attempts — and a `compareRegistries` diff of the stranded snapshot against live showed **exactly one** differing key, `<activeUser>.authToken`, with all 9 sections otherwise identical. **The fix therefore needs THREE outcomes, not two.** [`loginRouter.bs`](../source/loginRouter.bs) `resolveUser()` calls `getToken(username, "")` *unconditionally* when the saved token fails validation. For a password-LESS user (`demo`, and the maintainer's own local user) that succeeds and only `authToken` changes — converge. For a password-PROTECTED user it fails and the app `unset`s `authToken`/`username`/`primaryImageTag`/`active_user` and logs out — deleting the very identity keys an identity-assertion fix would check, so the restore must fail fast with *that* message instead of retrying 3 times. **`.177` has only password-less users, so no run has ever exercised that branch** (and no Rooibos test references `resolveUser` or `validateSavedToken`) — which is why it stayed invisible.

**Seen again on the shared dev device, 2026-11-12, with a password-protected user.** An RTA run took its snapshot right after another session's run released the device, so the active user was the test user on the local test server, not the operator's. After the run, `restoreRegistry` failed all 3 attempts on the same 10 keys of that user (`authToken`, `username`, seven `homeSection*` keys, `JellyRock.active_user`), and so did two `npm run rta:restore` runs by hand; every other section restored. That is the unset this entry predicts for the password-protected branch, though why the token failed validation was not checked. The kept snapshot then blocks every later RTA run on the device until `npm run rta:restore -- --accept`, which was run (recorded in `.device-runs/accepted-<host>.json`).

#### The restore's presence exemption matches `authToken`/`primaryImageTag` in ANY section, but the mechanism only explains the ACTIVE one. `[fid: restore-presence-exemption-any-section]` `[captured 2026-08-11]`

`compareRegistries` in [`tests/rta/lib/registry.js`](../tests/rta/lib/registry.js) keys `SESSION_CREDENTIAL_KEYS` on key NAME alone, so a value change on an *inactive* user's token is accepted silently — while the measurement in the entry above narrowed the trigger to *"the app must re-authenticate for the user+server the snapshot's ACTIVE session belongs to"*, since `resolveUser()` only re-mints for the active session. A token changing in a non-active section is therefore unexplained by the exemption's own rationale and should still be red. **Tightening is cheap and the data is already in the snapshot:** `saved.JellyRock.active_user` names the active user's section, so the exemption can be conditioned on `section === saved.JellyRock?.active_user`, with a fallback for a snapshot taken while signed out (no `active_user` key). **Deliberately NOT folded into [#805](https://github.com/jellyrock/jellyrock/pull/805)** — the current form still fails in both directions that matter (a destroyed session and a leaked credential), and `username`/`serverId` stay byte-asserted in the same section as a backstop. So this tightens *assert identity* from "except where a key name matches" to "except where the app provably re-minted it"; it is not closing a hole. Raised in review of #805.

#### `SessionManagement.spec.bs` throws away the connection error it already has, so the #800 failure carried no evidence. `[fid: session-management-spec-swallows-error]` `[captured 2026-08-12]`

The test asserts on `server.UpdateURL(...)`, which returns a bare boolean — so a failure reads only *"Should successfully connect to stable demo server"*. The detail exists one layer down and is discarded: `server.Discover()` returns `{Error, ErrorCode, ErrorMessage}` and `server.Populate()` drops it. That is why re-deriving #800 on 2026-08-12 needed CI-log archaeology — the run's **16100 ms** duration against the 35 s `CONNECTION_CHECK_MS` (so: not a timeout, a non-200 or transport error) was the only usable clue, and it is nowhere in the failure message. **Fix shape:** on failure ONLY, re-set `serverUrl` and call `server.Discover()` for the diagnostic, putting `ErrorCode`/`ErrorMessage` into the assert message — the same *capture at the throw site, never in the loop* design Phase 2 settled on for RTA, so the pass path stays free. The second call may well SUCCEED, which is itself the useful signal (*transient*). This is the Rooibos twin of the RTA diagnostics work.

#### The "`focusOverhangIcon` fails on the THIRD consecutive relaunch / caps overhang screens at n=2" shape is DISPROVED — close this on merge of the guarded-re-press fix. `[fid: focus-overhang-icon-third-relaunch-disproved]` `[captured 2026-08-19]`

Filed off two `.177` 2026-08-14 `measure -- --nav settings` series that both aborted at launch 3, so the cap looked like a property of the third launch. It is not: 30/30 cold `settings` samples ran clean on 2026-08-16, which retires the n=2 cap and the "holds for two launches and not the third" framing with it. The mechanism the entry describes — "after `waitHome()` the `Up` press never moves focus out of Home, and 15 s of guarded `Right` presses do nothing" — turned out to be a plain description of the harness defect fixed on `fix/rta-overhang-escape-and-login-gate`: `Right` walks the ROW while focus is still in Home, so it could never have helped. Two observations from the original filing are still worth keeping and are **not** superseded: focus rests on the `HomeRows` **container** (`keyPath` ends `.#homeRows`, no deeper descendant) with `isRemoteDisabled: false`, so the app is responsive and simply is not being escaped; and `waitFocusInside('#homeRows')` is NOT a usable guard, because the failing `keyPath` already contains `homeRows` and it would pass immediately in exactly the failing state. The one live question left — WHY the first `Up` is lost — is tracked in the entry below, which owns the instrument for it.

#### The item-index hypothesis for `focusOverhangIcon` is DEAD; the guarded re-press is proven necessary but NOT proven effective. `[fid: focus-overhang-item-index-dead]` `[captured 2026-08-19]`

The `[0,2]` that motivated it (`.176` 2026-08-16, `.177` 2026-08-18) is the OLD walk's own artifact — its `Right` presses dragged the item index and `wrapRowFocus` wrapped it round a short row — so the standing "maybe release also needs item index 0" candidate was reading its own footprint. It is dead from source independently: `Home.onKeyEvent` gates on `rowItemFocused[0]` and never reads `[1]`. What actually explained the record: `focusOverhangIcon` pressed `Up` once and then spent its timeout on `Right`, which walks the ROW while focus is still in Home, so a single lost `Up` was unrecoverable by construction; `overhangWalkKey` ([`tests/rta/lib/steps.js`](../tests/rta/lib/steps.js)) now picks the key from observed focus and warns on a retry. **What is still open, and what that warning is for:** WHY the first `Up` is lost remains unknown, and the recovery path has **never executed on hardware** — its only evidence is unit tests. The class did not recur in 4 post-fix runs (0 warnings each), but against a base rate of ~1 run in 3 before the fix that is a ~0.30 chance of seeing zero even if nothing had changed, so it is not evidence of effectiveness. **The next `[nav] overhang … re-pressed N time(s)` line in a run log is worth more than any further green run — capture it.** Two hypotheses already killed: item index (above), and "Home was still filling" (an `rtaSkeletonHoldMs` hold reproduced the record's unsettled-rows state and the first `Up` escaped fine, 26 s, no retry; the hook was reverted because it reaches Home by LOGIN while the failing path reaches it by relaunch).

#### The Favorites `cell-load` sweep is unblocked app-side; what is left is writing it. `[fid: favorites-cell-load-sweep-unwritten]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

`Home.onTabChanged` used to re-create both `HomeRows` and `FavoritesRows` with `CreateObject` and assign no id, so RTA could only address the active list by CHILD INDEX — verified on `.177` 2026-08-20: the focused node's `keyPath` ended `.0` and `#favoritesRows.content.getChildCount()` read `undefined`. **Both ids are now assigned at creation (2026-08-26)**, so Favorites is addressable by `#id` like the other four sweeps and is no longer the one sweep resting on a positional path. The harness-side fragility that sat under this is fully closed too (2026-09-08): the suite no longer names either row list by id — content reads resolve the active list through `homeListId()` and focus gates ask the focused node its `subtype` — so a Favorites sweep needs no special handling. **Remaining work:** add `navCellSweepFavorites` beside the others in [`tests/rta/lib/nav.js`](../tests/rta/lib/nav.js), and a spec arm for it. Test work only; there is no app change left under it.

#### Re-baseline the `.177` RTA full-suite timing band — every recorded figure now predates a test-population change, and the population has moved TWICE in two days. `[fid: rta-177-timing-band-rebaseline]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

The original 1289.9–1330.2 s band was measured at **60 passed / 2 skipped**. It then ran **58/4** when the demo server reported Quick Connect disabled, so `quick-connect.spec.js`'s two `testCtx.skip('server reports Quick Connect disabled')` tests stopped running (the other two skips are the `BENCH_ENABLED` bench specs, as before) — three consecutive runs at 1263.66 / 1256.13 / 1266.45 s were written up across two sessions as an unexplained sub-baseline drift and were simply a smaller suite. **As of 2026-09-08 it has moved again, in the other direction:** the demo server re-enabled Quick Connect (those two tests run again) AND `dialogs.spec.js` gained a NEW skip — Jellyfin **`v12`** landed on the demo server and dropped Dracula's color version, so the hero reports one `MediaSource`, `OSD.setButtonStates()` correctly removes `#showVideoSourceMenu`, and the `osd video-source` test now skips its absent precondition (0 of 11 demo movies have >1 `MediaSource`, so no other item can stand in for it). **So neither the 1289.9–1330.2 s band nor the 58/4 figure describes a current run — treat both as unverified.** **Still not established:** that the two Quick Connect tests account for the whole ~25–30 s. That is now answerable, since QC is enabled again — but it needs a run on a QUIET device (a parallel local session caused a mid-run sign-out on 2026-09-08) with a HEALTHY fixture (the same day's third run self-classified `blocked` on a 503 from the freshly-upgraded server). Until then, do not read a sub-1290 s run as an anomaly, and do not treat any recorded band as a floor the suite is beating.

#### Any Vitest run that loads `tests/rta/lib/` FABRICATES failure records into a live RTA run, and `test:scripts` also deletes `out/rta/` outright. `[fid: vitest-rta-lib-fabricates-failure-records]` `[captured 2026-08-20]`

**Reproduced live 2026-08-20**, unintentionally, which is the strongest form of this report: `npx vitest run tests/rta/lib/steps.test.js` was run while `npm run test:rta` was mid-suite, and the RTA run finished **48/48 green** while printing `[rta] 2 failure(s) captured with device state in this run` — a `batch-read-failed` and a `wait-focused-timeout` carrying `focus=ResumeButton`, both timestamped inside the run window by ~3.5 minutes, both invented by mocks in a unit test that never touched the device. So the failure mode is not only evidence LOSS, it is evidence FABRICATION attributed to a real run: `kind` is the field [`flake-baseline.js`](../scripts/flake-baseline.js) aggregates by, so mock records inflate real flake buckets, and a reader of that run record cannot tell the two apart. Chain: `npm run build` runs `rimraf` over `out/`; `rta-run.js`'s `beginRun` recreates it and calls `resetFailures()`; the concurrent Vitest process reaches `diagnostics.js` → `recordFailure`, which resolves `out/rta/failures.jsonl` relative to the working directory and appends; `close()` folds whatever it finds. Note `resetFailures()` at run start does NOT bound this — it fires before the contamination, not after. The DELETE half needs the whole `tests/scripts/unit/` directory: `mkdir -p out/rta && touch out/rta/marker && npx vitest run tests/scripts/unit/` and the marker is gone, while `run-record.test.js` alone is safe and so is either half run separately — `run-record.test.js` relies on its own `process.chdir(tmpDir)` while `tests/rta/lib/jellyfin.test.js` steers the same module via a process-wide `RTA_RUN_DIR`, and Vitest's default pool shares one process across concurrent files. **Cost:** a corrupted run record that reads as authoritative, plus silent evidence loss, and `test:scripts` runs in pre-push and CI. **Fix shape:** set `RTA_RUN_DIR` to a temporary directory for the whole of `tests/rta/**` (a `beforeAll` fixture, or `vitest.config.js` `setupFiles`) so no test can ever resolve an `out/` path inside the repo. That stops the class; naming individual files does not — the offenders are at least three (`run-record.test.js`, `jellyfin.test.js`, and `steps.test.js` reaching it indirectly through `diagnostics.js`), and the next one will arrive the same way.

#### No spec in the repo instantiates a SceneGraph cell component, so cell BEHAVIOR is unreachable by unit test — only the pure helpers around it are covered. `[fid: no-spec-instantiates-cell-component]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

Every existing spec tests namespace or pure functions (`cellLoad`, `textureManager`, `rowItemImage`, `rowItemText`); none creates a `JRRowItem` / `GridItem` node, so anything reading or writing `m.poster` / `m.itemPoster` has no unit-level home. **Concrete instance, not a hypothetical:** #840's `deferTextureLoad()` fix is a genuine behavior change on the bind path — it clears the poster URI so `isTextureUnloaded` is true by construction, which stops `unloadTexture()` early-returning forever on a recycled cell and stops the previous item's poster surfacing under a new item. Its only verification is `validate` + `test:rta` 48/48 + a measured four-field null on device (`loadsStarted` p=1.0000, `wipesReload` p=1.0000, `binds`/`unloads` p=0.17). That is real evidence, but the null is the weakest kind — it says nothing changed measurably, not that the right thing happens. The ORDER inside that sub is load-bearing and untested: the flag must be set BEFORE the URI is cleared, or `onPosterLoadStatusChanged` sees Roku's synthetic `failed` status without the guard that swallows it. **Fix shape:** establish one Rooibos pattern that builds a cell node with a stubbed content root and asserts against its fields, then backfill the two or three cases that matter (defer clears the URI; the flag precedes the clear; a failed image does not reload while an unloaded one does). Filed as a harness gap rather than against #840 — the pattern is missing repo-wide, not in that PR.

#### `ItemDetails` sits at exactly 8 of 8 buttons, and the RTA specs read that row by INDEX and by id 31 times — the day a ninth button lands they read a stash. `[fid: item-details-eight-buttons-index-reads]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

main's #890 added `<Group id="buttonOverflow" visible="false" />` beside the row and moves the tail into it once over the cap, leaving a `moreButton` in the last slot. Both spec shapes break silently: `#watchedButton.id` still RESOLVES (the node is in the scene, just inside a hidden group) and an index assertion is satisfied by a different button — the `#homeRows` defect again on a new surface. Not fixable today, and deliberately not built ahead: the overflow is unreachable on the fixture (`source/utils/buttonOverflow.bs` puts `ItemDetails` at 8 of 8, the OSD at 7 of 10), so a resolver would be speculative machinery with nothing to exercise it. What IS in place is the detector — `hiddenAncestor` walks up, the stash is `visible: false`, and `tests/rta/lib/resolution.test.js` pins that a stashed button reports OFF-SCREEN while one still on the row does not. When the ninth button lands, convert the index/id reads onto a resolver on the `tests/rta/lib/home-list.js` pattern.

🚨 **SECOND INSTANCE, and this one shipped a WRONG PUBLISHED NUMBER — 2026-08-22.** The pop-in instrument's `appearCounted` gate re-armed only on `bind()`, and a `RowList`/`MarkupGrid` does not rebind a cell that scrolls off and back onto the same item, so every re-entry was skipped — the exact race the texture buffer exists to win. It was reported as *"the buffer wins 6 of 7 re-entry races"* before the ledger's own arithmetic (`binds` 28 against 28 `items`, i.e. nothing rebound all sweep, against `reloads` 7) showed the rate had no events behind it. Mutation-verified unit tests did not catch it and structurally could not: **they feed the ledger synthetic call sequences, and the defect was a MISSING CALL SITE.** A unit test cannot see a call that was never made. Corrected in `e681f792` (`cellLoad.departed()`); `appearances` 28 → 46, `popInsCold` 0 → 6, and the actionable conclusion inverted with it.

⚠️ **A short feasibility spike was run 2026-08-22 and is the reason this entry is worth funding — it also shows the job is BIGGER than the fix shape above implies.** Rooibos 6.0.0-alpha.52 **does** support node tests (`@sgnode("ComponentName")`, compile-time validated against real components; `AnnotationType.NodeTest = "sgnode"`), so the capability exists and is simply unused here. A throwaway `@sgnode("JRRowItem")` suite nonetheless failed to compile on **three stacked blockers**, none of which is "a node cannot be instantiated":

1. **Scope.** `Cannot find name 'BaseTestSuite' for namespace 'tests'` — with the *identical* file mapping under which `cellLoad.spec.bs` builds fine. Adding `@sgnode` alone breaks it, so node tests need build-configuration plumbing that neither `bsconfig-tdd.json` nor `bsconfig-tests-unit.json` provides today. This is the first thing to solve and it blocks everything else.
2. **Typing.** `m.poster`, `m.cachedPosterUri`, `m.contentRoot`, `noteAppearance()` all fail `cannot-find-name` / `cannot-find-function`. At runtime `m` IS the component's `m`; BrighterScript's type checker will not allow a typed suite class to reach it. Same wall as `m.cellLoadState` in `cellLoad.spec.bs`, which needed a namespaced free function to get around.
3. **The escape hatch costs production shape.** The usual workaround is `m.top.callFunc()`, and [`callfunc-interface.cjs`](../scripts/bsc-plugins/callfunc-interface.cjs) requires every `callFunc` target to be declared in the component's XML interface — so testing an internal sub means **widening `JRRowItem`'s public interface to enable a test**. That is a design call, not a mechanical step, and it should be decided deliberately rather than discovered mid-build.

**Revised cost: half a day or more, with real risk of not landing** — not the 1–2 hours a reading of the fix shape suggests. It is establishing the repo's first node-test pattern, which is a tooling project with its own review surface. Deliberately NOT folded into the pop-in instrument PR for that reason. ⚠️ **What is worth knowing before starting:** the `m.global` dependency is avoidable — `renderStandardItem` reads `m.global.user` on its first line, but `renderLibraryTile` passes `invalid` for its `userSettings` argument and [`rowItemImage.bs`](../source/utils/rowItemImage.bs) reads no globals at all, so a **library-tile item** (`CollectionFolder`/`UserView`/`Channel`) drives bind → `renderItem` → `cachedPosterUri` → `noteAppearance` with nothing to stub. And whether `renderTracking` is writable from a spec never needs answering: calling `onRenderTrackingChanged()` directly still proves the call site exists, which is the property that matters.

#### The `test:rta` "48/48" baseline is a property of the DEMO SERVER, not a fixed number — and it is quoted as fixed in several places. `[fid: rta-48-baseline-demo-server-property]` `[captured 2026-08-22]`

A clean run reads **46 pass / 2 skip / 0 fail** whenever the demo server has no `playlists` library, because exactly two registry entries (`playlistsLibrary`, `playlistDetails`) require one and [`screens.spec.js`](../tests/rta/specs/screens.spec.js) skips a screen whose `collectionType` is absent. **Verified 2026-08-22 by querying the fixture rather than inferring**: `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` currently serves exactly `movies` / `tvshows` / `music`. This has now cycled at least three times — session 8 chased the same 2 skips to the same cause, session 11 reconciled it back to 0 skips when the library reappeared, and it is absent again. Each cycle costs a session the same investigation, because "48/48" reads like a regression baseline. **Fix shape:** state the dependency in [`docs/dev/rta-tests.md`](dev/rta-tests.md) — the pass criterion is *0 failures*, and the skip count tracks the demo server's library inventory — so the next reader sees a fixture property instead of a regression. The durable fix is the self-hosted fixture already tracked above, which this is one more driver for.

#### Two integration tests report neither pass nor fail, so the suite cannot add up its own results. `[fid: two-integration-tests-no-result]` `[captured 2026-08-21]`

`npm run test:integration` reads **Total: 57 / Passed: 55** with `Crashed: 0`, `Failed: 0`, `Ignored: 0` and `RESULT: Success` — 2 tests are counted in the total and land in no outcome bucket. Reproduced standalone on a Stick 4K (OS 15.3.4) 2026-08-21, and it composes into `test:all` exactly (2757 unit + 57 = 2814 total; 2757 + 55 = 2812 passed), so **CI has been reporting the same gap on every run and passing**. Pre-existing and unrelated to the branch that surfaced it — #841 adds one unit spec and touches no integration test. **Why it matters:** a test reporting no outcome is indistinguishable from a test that silently did not run, and this is the suite covering registry + migration behavior, where a silently-skipped test is most expensive. Note also that only **57 of 142** declared `@it` cases under `tests/source/integration/` execute at all; that may be deliberate config scoping or the same hole one level up, and it is unexplained either way. **Fix shape:** identify the 2 by diffing declared `@it` decorators against the runner's per-test report lines, then either make their outcome explicit or make the harness fail when the total doesn't reconcile — a suite that can't account for its own tests can't be trusted to report a regression. Found while verifying #841 for merge.

#### A mutation-testing pass must verify the mutation ACTUALLY APPLIED, or it reports false survivors. `[fid: mutation-pass-must-verify-applied]` `[captured 2026-08-23]`

In PR #846 two `sed`/`python` replacements silently matched nothing, so the file was unchanged and the suite passed — which is indistinguishable from a well-tested line, and reads as "this guard is redundant" when in fact it was never probed. Hit **twice in one pass**; re-run properly, one mutation was caught and one exposed a real gap. **Fix shape:** the harness diffs the mutated file against a pristine copy and refuses to run the suite on a no-op (prints `!! NO-OP` instead). Fold the rule into [`tests/CLAUDE.md`](../tests/CLAUDE.md) beside the existing mutation guidance, since the harness is hand-rolled per session and the lesson otherwise dies with the transcript. Related but distinct from the vacuous-test class this repo has hit three sessions running: there the test was real and asserted nothing useful; here the *check on the test* was the thing that verified nothing.

#### A sub-floor or zero measurement is not a result, and two candidates proved it in one session. `[fid: sub-floor-measurement-not-a-result]` `[captured 2026-08-23]`

Building the Task-thread ceiling (ADR 0031), an append into `GetGlobalAA()` measured BELOW the apparatus floor and `m.global.taskLedger.push(x)` measured a plausible 58 µs — and the second one **did nothing**, leaving the field at its original length after 200 pushes, because reading a node's array field returns a COPY. Both are indistinguishable from a correct cheap operation unless the postcondition is asserted; had the second shipped, the ledger would have been permanently empty and the ceiling silently disabled with every test still green. **Fix shape:** fold the rule into [`tests/CLAUDE.md`](../tests/CLAUDE.md) beside the existing mutation guidance — a benchmark cell must return something that proves the work happened (a resulting length, a count), and a reading at or below the floor is a prompt to add that proof, not a result to report. Sibling of the mutation-no-op entry above: there the check on the test verified nothing; here the check on the *measurement* did.

#### RTA runs in CI only on `release-*.*.*` branches and `workflow_dispatch`, so every RTA gate catches regressions at release prep rather than at PR time. `[fid: rta-ci-only-on-release-branches]` `[captured 2026-08-23]`

That is a deliberate trade (the self-hosted device runner is heavily contended — session 28 lost the lock to a parallel run three times in one afternoon), but it is worth an explicit decision now that a gate with real teeth depends on it: `tests/rta/specs/task-thread-peak.spec.js` is the PRIMARY defense against a future Task-thread fan-out (ADR 0031), and a runtime ceiling cannot report anything in a store build. **Question to settle:** is a thread-budget regression worth an earlier trigger than release-prep — and if so, does it run per-PR, on a merge queue, or nightly?

#### The RTA specs cannot run against an `.env` server that has been re-pointed, and the failure does not say so. `[fid: rta-specs-repointed-env-server-silent]` `[captured 2026-08-24]`

`RTA_CONFIG.heroMovie` is `"Dracula"` and the other content-dependent expectations (`trickplayMovie`, `searchQuery`) likewise describe the PUBLIC DEMO's library — `tests/rta/config.js`'s own header says as much — but `RTA_SERVER_URL` changes only the SERVER. So a contributor who points `.env` at their own Jellyfin gets `tests/rta/specs/dialogs.spec.js` dying in `beforeAll` with *"could not resolve the hero movie on the demo"*, which reads like a device fault or a flake and is neither. Cost two full runs to diagnose on 2026-08-24 before `RTA_SERVER_URL=https://demo.jellyfin.org/stable RTA_SERVER_USER=demo RTA_SERVER_PASS=` was used instead. **Fix shape:** the setup already knows both facts it needs — the configured server is not `PUBLIC_DEMO_SERVER`, and the expected title is absent — so it can say *that* ("`heroMovie` 'Dracula' is not on <server>; content-dependent expectations describe the public demo") or skip the content-dependent specs outright rather than failing them. Related to the self-hosted-fixture entry above, but much narrower: this is a diagnostics/ergonomics gap, not a fixture-sourcing one.

#### The other three cell sweeps still publish conflated totals. `[fid: other-cell-sweeps-conflated-totals]` `[captured 2026-08-24]`

`cellSweepHome` now reads the cell-load counters at the settle gate (`readCellCounts` in [`tests/rta/lib/steps.js`](../tests/rta/lib/steps.js)), so its report line separates what the screen did loading ITSELF from what the sweep provoked. That split is why the separation matters: measured on `.177` against the 13-library server, n=40, the sweep’s contribution is **exactly constant on the two fields that carry the argument** — `binds` +16 and `appearances` +75 on every launch, in both modes — while the published total spans `binds` 222-251, so **all** the run-to-run spread is page load. (The other four counters move by ±1-2 and the mechanism is unresolved: the page-load tail crossing the gate was measured and rejected, corr = 0.275.) `cellSweepGrid`, `cellSweepExtras` and `cellSweepSearch` still emit one end-of-session total each, so any figure of theirs that moves between launches cannot be attributed the same way Home’s was, and a reader has no way to tell. **Fix shape:** `readCellCounts` is already batched and unit-tested, so the work is picking the gate to read at — unlike Home each of those three calls `reportSweep` without a settle object, so there is no established "structure has stopped moving" instant to subtract from. Deferred from the `task-thread-budget` session that built it because the Home question did not need them.

#### `restoreRegistry` can never come back clean on `homeSection*` keys, so every run touching them banks an accepted difference. `[fid: restore-registry-homesection-never-clean]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

The shared dev device carries 4 from 2026-08-22 (`homeSection2/3/5/6`, on a NON-active user section), and `npm run device:status` warns about them on every invocation — which is the cost: a permanent warning no future run will ever clear, sitting on the channel that is supposed to mean "this device is not as it was found". **They are not damage.** `homeSection0-6` are server-authoritative Jellyfin `DisplayPreferences`, fetched per sign-in and deliberately never registry-cached (established in `task-thread-budget` session 33), so the app rewrites them from the server the moment it starts — the restore's write is overwritten before `restoreRegistry` can verify it. It is asserting over keys nothing on the device is allowed to hold. **Fix shape:** either exclude server-authoritative keys from the comparison by name, or give them a third outcome distinct from both "restored" and "accepted difference", so the accepted-difference warning keeps meaning something. Clear the existing 4 on that device (`rm .device-runs/accepted-<host>.json`) once the mechanism is handled, not before. Surfaced 2026-08-26 during `task-thread-budget` phase C device work.

#### A killed `measure` holds the device lock until its lease expires, and the refusal does not say how to clear it. `[fid: killed-measure-holds-device-lock]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

Hit twice on 2026-08-26: an `npm run measure` running in the background was killed, its GitHub-ref lock (`device-lock/<key>`) was never released, and the next run refused with `The Roku at <host> is in use by measure (local, pid 680690)` — for a `pid` that was no longer running. The lease-expiry steal path (`stealIfExpired`) does eventually recover, so this is friction rather than a wedge. **Fix shape:** when the holder is `local` and its process is not alive, either say so in the refusal and name `npm run device:release`, or steal the ref outright — a local holder's liveness is strictly better evidence than the lease clock. **Cheaper as of 2026-09-05:** that predicate now exists as `isProcessAlive` in `scripts/lib/process-liveness.cjs` (shipped in `af38ff9c` for the `device:status` live-run fix), so this is a call plus a message, not new machinery. The refusal message quoted above has also changed since — it now prints `ROKU_IP=<host> npm run device:status` — so the remaining gap is naming `device:release` for a dead local holder. Surfaced 2026-08-26.

#### The RTA suite's whole fixture is a third-party demo server, and that dependency is recorded only in a source comment. `[fid: rta-fixture-demo-server-undocumented]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

[`tests/rta/config.js`](../tests/rta/config.js) points at `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` and its own header says the server "will not be here forever; when JellyRock stands up its own demo server" — which is the plan (owner, 2026-09-07), but it lives nowhere a journal reader would find it. The costs are already paid and are all in the RTA project's record: a run recorded `blocked` when the demo host served a cert for a wildcard on a private domain; the hourly content reset lands mid-suite for anything starting after about 46 minutes past the hour, which is why the ledger carries `crossedHourBoundary` and why `probeFixture` now takes the server's pulse on both sides of every run; the Quick Connect pair flips between skipped and run depending on whether that hour's server has it enabled; and content-gated assertions like `moviesLibraryGenres` can weaken silently as the fixture churns. Measured 2026-09-07: the demo user has exactly **1** item marked as a favorite — enough for the Favorites TAB to mount (it does so regardless of content), but thin for the `navCellSweepFavorites` sweep tracked above. Not blocking anything; captured so the dependency is visible in a journal rather than only in a comment nobody greps.

#### The swallowed-Back hypothesis for `navHomeReturn`'s exit press is GUARDED but UNPROVEN. `[fid: swallowed-back-nav-home-return-unproven]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

Two 45 s `waitHome` timeouts on `.177` 2026-09-08 left the app motionless — focus still inside `#itemGrid`, view still `BaseGridView`, `readErrors`/`actionErrors` both 0, so the device was answering and the press simply never landed. The bare `press(ecp.Key.Back)` at [`tests/rta/lib/nav.js`](../tests/rta/lib/nav.js) was the last unguarded press of three in that file (the OK press nine lines above it and the Back in `navCellSweepExtras` both already carried `resendIfSwallowed`), and it is now guarded the same way — ORIGIN-gated, which is what makes it safe where the DESTINATION-gated `backToHome` was not, that one having been tried on this same function 2026-09-06 and signed the device out. **It has not fired since: three full suites green with `recoveries: 0`, so the guard has never been exercised and the mechanism remains a documented precedent (~1 in 6, measured for the OK press in this very function) rather than a confirmed cause.** No action is owed unless it recurs — `resendIfSwallowed` now calls `recordRecovery`, so the next occurrence answers this on its own: a recovery record means the guard caught the swallow, while a `waitHome` timeout with `recoveries: 0` kills the hypothesis outright. **The unexplained half is the TRIGGER, not the mechanism:** 48 prior ledger runs recorded zero `waitHome` timeouts before 2026-09-08 ~13:00 UTC, so something shifted the timing that day. Leading suspect is the demo server moving to Jellyfin 12 overnight (its library count went 4 to 3), which is a fixture change no code commit will show.

#### `scanHomeLibraryTiles` is the one read loop in `nav.js` left on sequential round trips. `[fid: scan-home-library-tiles-sequential]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

It issues one `getVal` per row plus one per tile column in nested loops — roughly 50 sequential ODC round trips per call, ~6 calls per suite run — while `pressProbe` and `selectionProbe`, in the same file, batch five fields into a single `getVals`. Deferred rather than fixed during the 2026-09-08 audit of `rta-harness-reliability`, before that branch closed, because the measured cost is small (~1.6 s of a ~1320 s suite, 0.12%) and it sits on the SUCCESS path of every library nav, so changing its read pattern is medium risk and needs a full hardware suite to re-validate — a bad trade for 0.12%. **Fix shape:** convert the nested loops to batched `getVals` reads, matching `pressProbe`, and re-run `npm run test:rta`.

#### `ordering-asserts-presence` re-walks the enclosing test body once per ordering assertion. `[fid: ordering-asserts-presence-rewalks-body]` `[captured 2026-09-09]`

`provenPresentIn(enclosingBody(node))` runs inside the `CallExpression:exit` handler, so a test block with N ordering assertions does N full walks of the same body — O(N x body) instead of O(body) — on every lint run. Deferred in the same audit for a measured reason: the whole JS lint is 2.3 s, so this is invisible today. **Fix shape:** cache the per-body result in a `WeakMap` keyed on the body node. Revisit only if lint time becomes a complaint.

#### A device test run cannot tell you whether a NEW spec actually executed. `[fid: device-run-cannot-tell-new-spec-ran]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

Two things combine: `scripts/run-roku-tests.js` opens its console socket at port 8085 only after the app has already begun printing, so `roku-test-output.log` loses its head and starts mid-line; and `bsconfig-tdd.json` sets Rooibos `showOnlyFailures: true`, which keeps passing suites out of the report entirely. A passing suite therefore appears NOWHERE in the log. Cost a wrong conclusion on 2026-09-04: a new spec was compiled into `build/source/tests/…` and counted in the run total, but grepping the log for its suite name found nothing, so it read as never having run — and the only way to prove it had was to invert an assertion and watch the run go red at that file. **Cheap fixes to weigh:** print the discovered suite names at run start, or have the runner assert the report's total against the number of built `*.spec.brs` files and fail loudly when they disagree.

#### `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` now runs 12.1.0: default RTA + `SessionManagement.spec` no longer exercise 10.11 (only local servers do) `[fid: demo-server-12-1-no-10-11-coverage]` `[captured 2026-09-15]`

#### RTA's `screens.spec` intermittently fails `nav timed out waiting for <type> grid (last=undefined)`, and it is NOT branch-specific. `[fid: screens-spec-grid-timeout-flaky]` `[captured 2026-09-19]`

Measured 2026-09-17 on `.177` against the public demo: untouched `main` (252a851b) failed `musicLibraryGenres` this way, while a feature branch failed 2 then 3 DIFFERENT screens across consecutive runs, and a full run on the same branch was clean apart from a genuinely absent playlists library. The app is on a loaded screen when the wait expires, so the navigation never happened rather than the grid failing to load; `task-thread-peak` reads 8-9 live (gate 30) and the demo server answers in under a second, so neither thread exhaustion nor server health explains it. Suspects not yet separated: the demo's hourly reset churning library ids mid-suite, and the spec's rapid screen-to-screen navigation. Worth pinning down before anyone reads a red `screens.spec` as a regression — it costs a full A/B against `main` every time, as it did here.

#### `tests/rta/lib/jellyfin.js` `postJson` crashes the Node process on an empty 204 body `[fid: post-json-crashes-on-empty-204]` `[captured 2026-09-19]`

(`/Sessions/Playing*`, `/UserItems/{id}/UserData`): it `JSON.parse`s inside the response stream's `end` callback, so the throw escapes every `.catch` and skips the caller's `finally` — on 2026-09-19 it left a device's registry seeded (recovered with `npm run rta:restore`). **Fix:** read the body as text and parse only when non-empty. Until then, scratch probes use Node's own `fetch` for those endpoints.

#### The test bsconfigs load 3 BSC plugins where the dev build loads 19, so a rule violation in `source/` / `components/` is invisible to `test:tdd` and `test:unit`. `[fid: test-bsconfigs-load-3-of-19-plugins]` `[captured 2026-09-20]`

All five test configs (`bsconfig-tests.json`, `-tests-unit`, `-tests-integration`, `-tests-complete`, `-tdd-sample`) carry only `rooibos-roku`, `bslint` and `translation-keys`; `bsconfig.json` carries all the custom ones (`no-raw-run`, `no-task-fanout`, `no-same-node-relaunch`, `unobserve-before-release`, …). The omission looks deliberate rather than a gap in any one file — it is identical across all five, and `bsconfig.json`'s `files` never includes `tests/**`, so several plugins would fire on spec code where their rule does not apply. Nothing is ungated (pre-push and CI both run the full build), but the feedback arrives a cycle later: observed 2026-09-20 when new `source/utils/` code was only checked against those plugins by the RTA run's full build, after `test:tdd` and `test:unit` had both gone green. **Fix shape:** add the pure-diagnostic plugins to the test configs with a `diagnosticFilters` entry scoping them off `tests/**`, and write the rationale down either way — no doc currently states why the lists differ.

#### `dialogs` "a user-capped bitrate transcodes" passes in ISOLATION and fails inside the full suite — it is order/state-dependent, not flaky-random. `[fid: dialogs-bitrate-test-order-dependent]` `[captured 2026-09-20]`

Measured 2026-09-20 with `The Dark Knight` on the local servers: **9/9 passes in isolation** (three runs each on 10.11.11, 12.0.0, 12.1.0) against **2 of 3 failures in-suite** (failed on 10.11 and 12.1, passed on 12.0). The isolation result is what rules out a server-version cause, and it is worth keeping: the in-suite pattern looked exactly like a 12.1 regression until the isolated runs were done, and a spec that only fails in company is a gate nobody will trust. The failing assertion is that the transcode reasons contain a label matching `Bitrate`/`BitRat`, so the likely mechanism is residual state from an earlier `dialogs` test (the report/OSD is left open, or the capped user setting has not settled) rather than the cap itself. **Fix shape:** find what the preceding tests leave behind before touching the assertion — the assertion is probably right. Reproduce either way with `node scripts/rta-run.js tests/rta/specs/dialogs.spec.js` (fails) vs adding `-t "user-capped bitrate"` (passes).

#### The TV guide has no automated regression test. `[fid: tv-guide-no-regression-test]` `[captured 2026-09-22]`

The bug #991 fixed — the guide stopped at 25 channels, since pagination was added — was caught by a hand-run device probe, not a gate. Promote the probe's `cold` trial (every channel page delivered; each held block's programs equal one `/LiveTv/Programs` query for its range) to an RTA spec with a `requires` gate on a Live TV library, so it skips on the public demo server, which has no Live TV, and runs against a local fixture (a synthetic `M3U` + `XMLTV` lineup on a throwaway Jellyfin). Document the fixture in `docs/dev/rta-tests.md` too, or the spec will only ever skip.

#### The per-library Collections view has no permanent RTA coverage, and the blocker is the fixture, not the spec. `[fid: collections-view-no-rta-coverage]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

Its device verification — 12.0.0 and 12.1.0 loading each library's own collections, 10.11.11 withholding the entry and falling back to `MoviesGrid` — ran through a throwaway spec against a temporary `ZZ Probe TV Collection` seeded and torn down inside the session that built it, so nothing on `main` asserts the view again. A permanent spec needs a permanent collection in the local fixture, which was deliberately NOT created: seeding collections is what made the `Collections[boxsets]` and `Playlists[playlists]` libraries non-empty in the first place, so they began appearing in `/UserViews` on all three servers and moved the RTA baseline. **Decide the fixture question first** — a permanent movie collection plus a permanent TV one, and a re-taken baseline — then write the spec. A spec written before that either has nothing to assert against or silently re-perturbs the baseline.

#### The playback report's slow-fetch races have no permanent RTA gate. `[fid: playback-report-slow-fetch-races-ungated]` `[captured 2026-09-23]`

Two bugs have now come from the window while `GetPlaybackInfoTask` is in flight: the report reopening after you close it (decision `playback-report-fetch-per-run`) and a late answer replacing a dialog opened meanwhile (decision `playback-report-newest-dialog-wins`). Both were proven only by throwaway probe builds. The hook this needs already exists in shape: an `ENABLE_RTA`-only hold on `m.global`, the `rtaSkeletonHoldMs` pattern, read by `GetPlaybackInfoTask`, plus a spec that opens a picker or dialog during the hold and asserts the report does not appear. Deferred 2026-09-23 by user call, as fragile relative to its value; revisit if a third bug lands in this window.

#### `focus.spec.js` fails ~1 run in 10 at its second Back, on `main` too — the swallowed-Back guard can never fire. `[fid: focus-spec-flaky-second-back]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

The spec presses Back from the library grid, then awaits `waitHome()`, and only AFTER that runs the wait carrying `resendIfSwallowed(ecp.Key.Back, '#itemGrid')`. A Back swallowed mid-navigation therefore times out `waitHome()` before the resend exists. Measured 2026-09-25 on the Ultra `.178`: `main` 1/10 failed, the #1033 branch 1/5, identical symptom (`view=BaseGridView`, focus in `#itemGrid`, `readErrors=0`). Fix shape: put the guarded resend on the Home wait itself. Test-only.

#### Every OSD RTA spec fails on the 512 MB Stick (`3600X`): the video plays, but the OSD never comes up. `[fid: osd-specs-fail-on-512mb-stick]` `[captured 2026-09-25]`

Found 2026-09-25 in a full `test:rta` run on the low-end Stick (Roku OS 15.3.4, demo server): `screens` "osd" and "trickplay", and the five `dialogs` specs that open the OSD first, all time out in `waitOsdUp` with `#osd.visible` false while `player=play` / `videoNode=playing`. **Pre-existing, not device noise:** `screens -t 'screen "osd" loads'` fails the same way on `main` (`9c43ebad`) on the same device, and passes on a Stick 4K.

**The key never reaches the app** (measured 2026-11-01, Roku OS 15.3.4). With a temporary `print` at the top of `onKeyEvent` in `VideoPlayerView`, `OSD` and `JRScene`, a Play press during playback printed nothing on this Stick, in `syncplay-controls` and in `playback-check-in`, and printed every key on a Stick 4K. ECP answers 200, and a key on Home does change the screen, so only playback loses them. The same cause takes out every key-driven case in `syncplay-controls` and `syncplay-correction` on this Stick, and both cases of `playback-check-in`.

Not checked: whether a physical remote works there, and whether the cause is the OS, the model or the way ECP reaches the node. Closes when a press during playback reaches `onKeyEvent` on this Stick, or the cause is shown to be the device and the suite says so instead of timing out.

#### Gate that a render-thread callback is not split by a Task's node access (from ADR 0045). `[fid: gate-callback-split-by-task-node-access]` `[captured 2026-09-26]`

The live-stream handoff never leaves a stream open with nobody to close it only if `VideoPlayerView.releaseVideoLoad()`'s read of the load's `liveStream` and its write of `isAbandoned` cannot have the load's write of `open` and read of `isAbandoned` land between them. That is assumed from how the render thread serves a Task's node access, not measured. **Probe:** a `TaskRelaunchProbeHost` function that reads one field, spins a few ms, then writes a second, run while a probe Task loops write-first-then-read-second on the same pair; assert the Task never reads the old second value after its write landed between the host's read and write. Add it with no tag next to `TaskStopProgress.spec.bs`, with a control, and record the result in `threading.md`.

#### Verify the channel-switch playback start on a device. `[fid: channel-switch-playback-start-device-check]` `[captured 2026-09-27]`

`VideoPlayerView.switchToQueueItem()` opens a labeled `PLAYBACK_START` wait that the next player takes (ADR 0046); only the label wiring was checked on a device (2026-09-26). The `M3U` fixture's channels are converted by the 12.1 server and never reach `playing`, so the channel list never loads and "next" returns `error.generic`. Use the HLS Live TV fixture (`hls-fixture.sh`) to switch channels and check the label, the stage text on a slow channel, and that the old player's teardown does not end the new start.

#### `user.Login()`'s registry-load loop has no test for its skip rules `[fid: login-registry-load-routing-untested]` `[captured 2026-10-04]`

`user.Login()` ([`source/utils/session.bs`](../source/utils/session.bs)) loads the user's registry section in an inline loop that routes each key: it skips global keys (`isGlobalSetting`), `display.*` (handled by `TransformDisplaySettings`), `homeSection*` (server-authoritative) and `authToken` / `primaryImageTag` (set by the login flow itself), sets user-node fields, and passes settings-node fields to `user.settings.Save()`. No test exercises that routing. The registry specs used to test a parallel loader, `SessionDataTransformer.transformUserSettings`, that production never ran; the #1072 cleanup removed it and pointed them at `BaseTestSuite.loadSettingsFromRegistry`, which covers `user.settings.Save()`'s type conversion but not the routing. A key rename or a new prefix can therefore break loading silently. Closing it needs the loop pulled out of `user.Login()` into a named function a spec can call (a login-path change, so test on a device), then specs for each skip rule.

#### `SubtitlePanel.shouldOfferPerfectMatchFilter()` has no test `[fid: perfect-match-filter-rule-untested]` `[captured 2026-10-04]`

`SubtitlePanel.shouldOfferPerfectMatchFilter()` ([`components/subtitles/SubtitlePanel.bs`](../components/subtitles/SubtitlePanel.bs)) decides whether the "perfect matches only" toggle appears: kept while the filter is on, hidden when the result set fits on screen, and offered only when some but not all results are hash matches. It has no test. Its only coverage was the removed `remoteSubtitles.hasAnyHashMatch` spec, which tested a helper the panel no longer called (removed in the #1072 cleanup). The function is not declared in the component's interface, so a spec cannot call it as is. Closing it means moving the rule into `remoteSubtitles` as a pure function of the results, the filter state and `subtitleLayout.VISIBLE_ROWS` that the panel calls, then a spec for each case.

#### The stepped RTA walk's drop wait rests on one device's measured key delay `[fid: stepped-walk-drop-wait-one-device]` `[captured 2026-10-08]`

`STEPPED_DROP_WAIT_MS` (`tests/rta/lib/steps.js`, 1000 ms) is how long `scrollFocus`'s stepped mode waits for an index to move before it re-sends a key as dropped. It rests on measurements from one device: on `.177` (Stick 4K, Roku OS 15.3.4, 2026-10-08) a Down or Up took 350-444 ms to show in Home's `rowItemFocused`, and a Right or Left 372-405 ms in the Movies grid's `itemFocused`. If another device answers slower than the wait, a slow key reads as dropped and the re-press is the overshoot the stepped mode exists to stop. Neither the 512 MB `.176` nor the Ultra `.178` has been measured.

Closes when the same press-to-index delay has been measured on `.176` and `.178` (a scratch ODC script that presses one key and polls the index until it changes, for both Home rows and a library grid). Then either confirm the wait still has headroom, or set it from the slowest device and record that measurement next to the constant. Came from the row-walk and grid-walk fixes on `fix/rta-row-walk-step`.

#### The release RTA job has never passed in CI: a 25-minute limit on a suite that now takes over half an hour, and the rest of its setup needs redoing with it `[fid: release-rta-never-passes-in-ci]` `[captured 2026-10-08]` `[prompt: /start-project]` `[pinned]`

The `rta` job in `.github/workflows/rta-functional-tests.yml` has `timeout-minutes: 25`, set in #772 (2026-08-06) when a full pass took 10-15 min. The suite has since grown from 10 tests in 5 spec files (2026-08-14) to 62 in 21 (2026-10-04), and a full pass now takes 35-44 min locally (RTA ledger, early October: `.178` Ultra 35, `.177` Stick 4K 35-42, `.176` 43-44). Every release run where the job started (15 since 2026-08-07) failed or was canceled, most at the limit; run 37243742270 (release-2.35.0) reports `The job has exceeded the maximum execution time of 25m0s` and uploaded no run record. Releases still showed green because the path gate skips the job on release pushes that touch no app or test paths. The "10-15 min" figure is repeated in `tests/rta/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/dev/rta-tests.md`, `docs/architecture/build-and-tooling.md` (twice), ADR 0031 and the workflow's header comment.

Do this as one project, not piecemeal, because the parts depend on each other:

- **Suite audit:** decide whether all 62 tests earn their device time, or whether some were written during implementation and never retired. The audit sets the run time, which sets the limit.
- **Server:** the workflow sets no `RTA_SERVER_URL`, so `tests/rta/config.js` falls back to the public demo server, unless the self-hosted runner's per-user env file overrides it. The operator believes that file has not changed since the runner was set up, so it probably does not, but this needs verifying on the runner. Decide which server the release run should target. Cases that need data the demo server wipes hourly (the Favorites cases in `home-failure.spec.js` need a favorite Movie) either seed it themselves (the app uses `POST /UserFavoriteItems/{id}`; RTA already writes to the demo server when it signs in and authorizes Quick Connect) or wait for the new demo server.
- **Limit:** raise `timeout-minutes` with headroom over the audited run time, and replace the "10-15 min" figure with a scale plus the command that shows it live.
- **Loud failure:** a skipped or canceled `rta` job should show on the release as "RTA did not run". Read the release flow first.
- **Optional trim:** move measurement-style specs (leaks, task-thread peak) to an on-demand job.

Options weighed: `limit` (raise to 60 min, fix the docs), `trim` (limit plus move measurement specs out), `loud` (limit plus a visible did-not-run signal). Recommended `limit` as the first step; the operator chose to defer and do all of it together.

Not checked: whether the 2026-08-07 and 2026-08-17 failures would pass today; whether anything in the release flow reads this job's result; the CI device's actual run time (assumed to match `.177`, the same model); whether a single spec got slower, since the ledger has no per-spec times; the self-hosted runner's env file.

Close it by deleting both loaders, or by adding the mocks when a spec needs them.

#### Five test bsconfigs exclude `!source/Main.bs`, which matches nothing since the file became `main.bs` `[fid: test-bsconfigs-dead-main-exclusion]` `[captured 2026-10-11]`

Five test bsconfigs (`bsconfig-tests-unit.json`, `bsconfig-tests.json`, `bsconfig-tests-complete.json`, `bsconfig-tests-integration.json`, `bsconfig-tdd-sample.json`) exclude `"!source/Main.bs"`, which has matched nothing since the 2026-04-06 rename (`git log --diff-filter=R -- source/main.bs`) renamed the file to `source/main.bs`; the match is case-sensitive on Linux. Found in phase G of docs-voice (s19).

Root cause: The 2025-10-24 test-structure refactor (`git log -S'!source/Main.bs'`) added the line to keep the app's `Main` out, so the Rooibos plugin would generate its own `source/rooibosMain.brs`. Since the rename, `npm run build:tests-unit` produces `build/source/main.brs` with `Rooibos_init("RooibosScene")` injected first, and every test run since has used that. The `log-manager-init-check.js` header ("WHY A LINT GATE RATHER THAN A TEST") and its diagnostic message state the old reason ("Rooibos excludes source/Main.bs"); the conclusion still holds, because `keepAppOpen` defaults to true and `Rooibos_init` loops until the screen closes.

Options: `drop` (recommended): delete the line from the five configs and correct the lint header and message; the build output doesn't change. `restore`: change it to `!source/main.bs` to return to the generated main; brings back warning 2213, changes the test environment, and needs `test:unit` and `test:integration` on a device. `leave`: fix only the lint text.

Not checked: on a device, whether the app's `Main` code after `Rooibos_init` runs when the test screen closes, and whether that has any effect; whether any spec has come to depend on the app's `Main` being in the build.

Closes when the line is gone from every tracked config, the lint text is corrected, and `npm run test:unit` passes. Ships as its own PR from `main`.

In `tests/scripts/unit/lint/rta-sleep-budgeted.test.js`, the `dialogs.spec.js` case uses `sleep(1)` against a `BUDGETS` entry of 2 in `scripts/lint/eslint-rules/rta-sleep-budgeted.js`. The next budget change breaks it again, as one already did. Derive it as the entry minus one. Checked 2026-10-13.

A unit test should not run git against the checkout: inject the git runner for that step. Checked in the code 2026-10-13; not run.

#### The `SubtitlePanel` Perfect match toggle after a failed search has no automated guard `[fid: subtitle-toggle-after-failure-unguarded]` `[captured 2026-10-14]`

`SubtitlePanel`'s Perfect match toggle after a failed search has no automated guard. Found in review of PR #1203: `togglePerfectMatch()` re-rendered without the failed flag, so turning the filter off after a failed search showed "No subtitles found". The fix is structural (`m.searchFailed` beside `m.allResults`, written only by `showSearchOutcome()`, and `renderResults()` takes no flag), but nothing fails if a later render path bypasses it.

Why not now: the toggle appears only with more than `subtitleLayout.VISIBLE_ROWS` results, some but not all hash matches. `failRequests` can inject only an empty list, a provider without credentials answers `[]`, and a Rooibos `@sgnode` suite is blocked (see `no-spec-instantiates-cell-component`).

Close it with a `results` kind in `source/api/apiFaults.bs` that answers a matching request with a list the rule carries (the shape `responseFor` builds, with `apiFaults.spec.bs` cases), documented in `tests/rta/lib/failRequests.js`. Then add a case to `tests/rta/specs/subtitle-search-failure.spec.js`: canned mixed results, Perfect match on, a failed search, the filter off, and the status still reads the failed text. Canned results also lower the panel's fixture from "a provider with credentials" to "the plugin installed", which is what the deeper focus and scroll spec in tech-debt `subtitle-panel-rta-fixture-gated` waits on. Related: `perfect-match-filter-rule-untested` (the toggle's visibility rule as a pure function).

The same RTA case should also press the toggle while a search is in flight (a `slow` rule on the search), now that `togglePerfectMatch()` skips the redraw then: that path's guard today is only the pure predicate `remoteSubtitles.shouldRerenderOnPerfectMatchToggle()`, not the wiring.

#### Voice search on `SearchScreen` is unverified under the router, and a unit suite cannot verify it `[fid: search-screen-voice-path-unverified]` `[captured 2026-10-14]`

Roku grants `voiceEnabled` to one node at a time. Navigating Home -> `/search` covers Home rather than destroying it (ADR 0029, ADR 0050), so Home's hidden `VoiceTextEditBox`, which `Home.initVoiceBox` sets `voiceEnabled = true` once and deliberately never sets back to false, is still alive and may still hold the route when `SearchScreen.initVoiceBox` asks for it. Observed on `.178`: a standalone `SearchScreen` built in a suite where another voice node exists reads back `voiceEnabled = false`, which is why `SearchScreenVoiceEntry` no longer asserts it (its sibling `HomeVoiceSearch` never did either, for the same reason). A unit suite cannot reproduce the covered-Home arrangement, so that assertion could not have answered the question anyway.

Since ADR 0050 (#1137) the order is also new: `SearchScreen.init` claims the route when the node is created, and Home's `onScreenHidden` (which sets its box `active = false`) runs only later, from `hideCovered()` in the step `SearchScreen` opens. And a covered view now stays in the outlet, hidden, rather than being detached, which is why `SearchScreen` now toggles its own box's `active` in `OnScreenShown` / `OnScreenHidden` as Home does (so voice on a details screen above it cannot run a hidden search). Whether the firmware delivers voice to a hidden, active box at all is not measured.

**Closes it:** an RTA case that drives Home -> `/search`, confirms the search screen's voice box actually holds the route, and confirms voice entry fills in the query; and one that speaks on a details screen opened from search and confirms no search runs behind it. Until then the voice path on the redesigned search screen is untested on a device. Not done now because it needs device-side voice injection designed for RTA. First captured 2026-09-04 on `redesignSearch` (`f2bd8076`), and re-captured in the current journal format during the main sync.

#### Upstream watch: demo server has no movie with two video sources `[fid: jellyfin-demo-single-mediasource]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: `demo.jellyfin.org/stable` regaining any movie that reports `numVideoSources >= 2`, or JellyRock standing up its own demo server, which is the plan (owner, 2026-09-07) and would let the fixture be seeded rather than waited on

Last upstream version seen: Jellyfin `v12` on demo.jellyfin.org/stable: 0 of 11 movies with >1 `MediaSource` (checked 2026-09-09). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `jellyfin-demo-single-mediasource` row.

Where JellyRock stands: `tests/rta/specs/dialogs.spec.js`'s `osd video-source button opens the list dialog; back cancels it` skips at runtime, because `OSD.setButtonStates()` correctly removes `#showVideoSourceMenu` when `numVideoSources < 2`. Jellyfin **`v12`** landed on the demo server and dropped Dracula's color version, so the hero reports one `MediaSource` and **0 of its 11 movies** carry more than one (checked 2026-09-08). The skip is therefore not conditional: the test **cannot run against the only fixture the suite has**, and has not run since `v12` landed. That is coverage which reads as present in the suite listing and is inert, which is the same "green for a reason nobody chose" class the rest of this harness work is about; it is tracked here rather than as a followup because the trigger is an EXTERNAL fixture change nobody here controls. The four `#showVideoInfoPopup` callers beside it still throw on a missing button, because they have no content precondition, so a missing button there is a real defect

When it moves: re-point the test at whichever item reports two sources and DELETE the `testCtx.skip`: a skip that outlives its cause is worse than a red, because nothing reports it. If the fixture moves to a `JellyRock`-owned demo server instead, seed one item with two video sources and delete the skip the same way. Either move also invalidates the run-count figures in the RTA timing followup in `docs/progress.md`, which counts this skip

#### Upstream watch: `roku-test-automation` ODC socket setup never settles, and orphans its own rejection `[fid: rta-odc-connect-hang]` `[captured 2026-10-20]`

Watching upstream: `roku-test-automation` releases after 2.2.2 (including the `next` 3.0.0-alpha line) that fix EITHER half of `setupClientSocket`: settling its promise when the post-connect handshake fails, and attaching a handler to the promise its own `.finally()` returns

Last upstream version seen: 2.2.2 (checked 2026-09-06). Moved here from the retired `docs/signals-backlog.md`, where this was the `rta-odc-connect-hang` row.

Where JellyRock stands: pinned to 2.2.2. In `client/dist/OnDeviceComponent.js`, `setupClientSocket` resolves its cached `clientSocketPromise` from the socket's `connect` handler only after a `setSettings` handshake succeeds; the rejection arm of that `.then()` calls `debugLog` and **never settles the promise**. So a handshake that fails leaves every later ODC call awaiting a dead promise, and no timeout can fire. The per-request `promiseTimeout` wraps the request, not the socket setup, so no `defaultTimeout` / `timeoutMultiplier` value would help. Still present on upstream HEAD (verified 2026-09-04; the 3.0.0-alpha refactor to `RokuDeploySocket` kept the same handler). Observed once on `.178`: SIGINT mid-suite left `restoreRegistry` hung 8+ minutes on an ESTABLISHED socket to port 9000 with zero bytes queued and no timeout, after which the device refused new ODC connections and its debug console reported "already in use". The code defect is certain; that it is what hung that process is consistent with the evidence but not proven. Recovery is `kill` + a re-deploy, and `npm run test:rta` does it automatically, since `snapshotRegistry()` restores from the stranded snapshot first. **The hang is now bounded on our side** (2026-09-06): reproduced deterministically with no device (a socket that accepts and never answers leaves `readRegistry()` still pending at 45 s), and `REGISTRY_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` in [`tests/rta/lib/registry.js`](../tests/rta/lib/registry.js) now caps it at 60 s with a message naming this row. **A SECOND defect in the same function, found 2026-09-06 and the reason this row was widened rather than closed:** line 1080 attaches a `.finally()` to clear the cached promise and never handles the promise `.finally()` RETURNS, so ANY connect rejection goes unhandled, and an unhandled rejection is a hard `exit 1`. Measured against the real client: a caller that awaits, catches and carries on still dies. Proven by control: patching `.catch(() => {})` onto that one derived promise removes the crash and changes nothing else. It **cannot be suppressed from outside the library** (`setupClientSocket` returns the ORIGINAL promise, not the derived one), which is why the in-repo answer is `ensureOdcReachable` in [`tests/rta/lib/driver.js`](../tests/rta/lib/driver.js), which avoids making the call, rather than a `.catch()` anywhere

When it moves: read `setupClientSocket`'s `connect` handler in the new release. If the rejection arm now rejects (or the setup is wrapped in a timeout), take the bump and drop the recovery note from [`docs/dev/rta-tests.md`](dev/rta-tests.md). ALSO check line 1080's `.finally()`: the two halves are independent and either may land alone, so a release that fixes one leaves this entry open for the other. If the orphan is fixed, `ensureOdcReachable`'s reason for existing narrows but does not vanish (it still turns a 10 s refusal into a 1 ms one and names the cause). The watchdog this entry used to defer to a second occurrence was built on 2026-09-06 instead, on the strength of a deterministic reproduction (see where JellyRock stands, above)
