#### The RTA gear test's reload sometimes ends on an error dialog, and the failure dump does not say which `[fid: rta-gear-reload-error-dialog]` `[captured 2026-10-23]`

The `dialogs` gear test (`osd gear caps the bitrate for this playback…`) failed 1 run in 4 on `.177` (2026-10-23, branch `feat/osd-settings-menu`, against the stable demo server): after the 1 Mbps pick the reload ended on a `JRDialog` instead of playback (`nav timed out waiting for player playable (pre-OSD) (last="stopped")`, focus on `#jrDialog.#buttonRow.0`, `player=close`). The same build passed the test in the other three runs. The run started at 14:12 and failed about two minutes in, so it did not cross the top of the hour and `demo-server-hourly-reset-suite-flake` does not explain it. It has the shape of the suite's other intermittent demo-server failures (a shared server that transcodes in software, 8 fps by its own `TranscodingInfo`), but the cause was not captured: the failure dump does not say what the dialog was.

Seen a second time the same day, in a draft case making two gear picks 0.5 s apart: `nav timed out waiting for player settled after two gear picks (last="stopped")`, focus again on `#jrDialog.#buttonRow.0`. A temporary capture of the dialog's text was in place for a later run, which passed.

Closes when the dump in `tests/rta/lib/diagnostics.js` includes an open dialog's `title` and `message`, and a failing run shows whether the dialog is the playback error (the server's transcode failing) or something the app raised. Not now: it needs a run that fails, and two runs with a temporary capture in place both passed.

#### `navOsd()` leaves the player playing, so the OSD screenshot can lose its OSD `[fid: navosd-leaves-player-playing]` `[captured 2026-10-29]` `[pinned]`

`navOsd()` (`tests/rta/lib/nav.js`) presses Play to pause, then writes `#<heroId>.seek`, and assumes the player stays paused. A device probe (Stick 4K, 2026-10-29, three runs of `navOsd()` sampled every 500 ms) read `state=playing osd=true pos=1724` when the nav returned and `osd=false pos=1727` about 2.5 s later, every run. `OSD.inactiveCheck()` hides the OSD 5 s after the last key press unless `playbackState` is `paused`, so the OSD shot survives only if taken within about 2.5 s of the nav; the screenshot capture's backdrop step and reads can push it past that (`de/osd` lost its OSD in the 2026-10-29 capture and was retaken). Affects the `osd` screen in the screenshot capture and in `screens.spec.js`; nothing checks the player is paused. #1276 reworked the player's pause, play and seek (`pausePlayer()`, `resumePlayer()`, `seekPlayer()` in `VideoPlayerView.bs`) the same day, which may be what moved it.

Options: `pause` (recommended): find which step resumes playback, then have `navOsd()` wait for the player to read `paused` after the seek, pressing Play again if the seek resumed it; if the Play press itself no longer pauses, that is an app bug to raise separately. `guard`: `capture()` refuses an OSD shot unless `#osd.visible` is true, so the capture retries; it does not fix the cause.

Not checked: which of the two (the Play press or the seek) leaves it playing; whether #1276 changed it; whether a real viewer's Play press during playback still pauses.

#### A time-suffix spec fails in the first hour of the day daylight saving ends `[fid: spec-disambiguated-time-dst]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

`misc-disambiguatedTime.spec.bs` assumes now + 12 hours is always the opposite half of the day. On the day daylight saving ends that fails for the first hour: at 00:57 EDT on 2026-11-01, +12 hours is 11:57 AM EST, so `getDisambiguatingTimePeriod()` correctly returns `""` and two cases fail ("suffixes a time in the opposite half of the day", "appends a space-separated suffix when it isn't"). Both cases passed in a `test:unit` run at 01:09 EDT that day, once that hour had ended. The spring change was not checked.

**Fix shape:** build the anchor and target times from fixed values, or skip the opposite-half cases when the offset between now and the target changes.

#### RTA: the playback report test can time out with an unnamed dialog over a stopped player `[fid: rta-playback-report-dialog-over-stopped-player]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

The test "the playback report scrolls, hands focus to OK, and only closes from the button" (`tests/rta/specs/dialogs.spec.js`) can time out in `pausedOsd()` at `waitOsdUp`, with a dialog over a stopped player. On 2026-11-01 at 05:46 UTC, in a full run on #1299's branch, it reported `nav timed out waiting for player playable (pre-OSD) (last="stopped")` with focus on `#jrDialog.#buttonRow.0`, before the hourly reset (`afterHourBoundary: false`). It then passed 3 of 3 alone on that branch. The same wait failed in this test on 2026-10-23 at `d963b1ee`, after a reset, and in `osd gear caps the bitrate for this playback without saving it` the same day. The sibling followup `dialogs-bitrate-test-order-dependent` records a test with the same 1 Mbps cap that passes alone and fails in the suite, so order may matter here too.

Cause not confirmed. A playback error dialog is only a guess, because the records so far don't name the dialog. The failure record now does (`dialog=<type>("<title>")`), so this can't be taken further until the next failure, and it isn't worth holding the shared Roku for repeated runs before then.

**To close:** at the next failure, read the `dialog=` value. If it is "Error during playback", the dialog's `message` holds the cause and error code but is left out of the record on purpose (see "What it never reads" in `tests/rta/lib/diagnostics.js`), so capture the console for that run. Then fix the cause, or show it belongs to `dialogs-bitrate-test-order-dependent` and fold this entry into it.

**Also seen in the same run, 05:48 UTC:** "Play on a song's details opens the audio player" timed out waiting for focus in `#extrasGrid` (`wait-focused-timeout`, view `ItemDetails`, focus on an `IconButton`), then passed 3 of 3 alone. Cause not found, and this exact timeout has no earlier record. File it separately if it comes back.

**Not checked:** which dialog appeared; whether the failure repeats in `dialogs.spec.js` run alone; whether the sibling followup has the same cause; whether the memory monitor plays any part, though the same wait failed on a commit without it.

#### RTA cannot reach the ready branch of the SyncPlay duplicate-restart fix `[fid: syncplay-absorb-ready-branch-no-rta]` `[captured 2026-11-04]`

`syncPlayQueue.restartAbsorbed` has two branches (decision `syncplay-duplicate-restart-absorbed`). The RTA case in `tests/rta/specs/syncplay-controls.spec.js` covers the loading branch only. The ready branch (a duplicate restart that finds the Roku already ready at the start, so it sends `Ready` again) has unit tests alone: the REST second member ignores the wait, so the group's `Unpause` follows the Roku's `Ready` within about 100 ms and a poll cannot land in that window. Not done now because it needs the REST member to send its own `Ready` (`IgnoreWait` off) so the wait stays open. Closes when an RTA case drives the ready branch.

#### Document the BrightScript simulator for one-spec and boot checks `[fid: brs-simulator-howto]` `[captured 2026-11-05]`

**Document the BrightScript simulator (`brs-node`, `brs-cli`) as a no-device check.** Tried 2026-11-05 with `brs-node` 2.6.2: a dev build boots to pre-login and prints log lines in the device format, then the engine faults (`e.registerCallback is not a function`) when the first Task (`ServerDiscoveryTask`) starts. A single-spec TDD build runs cleanly (the logger spec, 6 of 6). The whole unit suite ran 90 suites (697 pass, 67 fail) before the process ran out of memory with an 8 GB heap; failures cluster in focus and visual suites, Task-relaunch platform suites and codec answers, and none was checked against a device. So it suits one spec at a time and boot checks, never as a replacement for `test:all` on a device. Add a short how-to to `docs/dev/unit-tests-tdd.md` (zip `build/`, `npx brs-cli <zip> -c 0`). The maintainer does not want upstream bug reports filed.

#### A `SyncPlayManager` spec cannot send a queue frame after a catch-up: the Rooibos run crashes on a stripped state `[fid: rooibos-frame-after-catchup-crash]` `[captured 2026-11-08]`

A Rooibos spec in `SyncPlayManager.spec.bs` that sends a queue frame after a halted member's catch-up crashes the run (seen 2026-11-08 on the Stick 4K, while building the catch-up's `QueueNext` addition). The manager's membership state reaches `syncPlayQueue.isStaleQueueUpdate()` with its `LongInteger` and `invalid` fields gone (`lastupdatems`, `joinedatms`, `playbackqueueatms`, `startpositionticks`, `isplaying`), and the comparison raises a type mismatch. The console also logs "promises_Promise is inaccessible from render thread" right after the catch-up, from the `start` promise the spec creates on its own thread. The app does not crash on the same sequence: the `syncplay-menu` RTA case for the addition runs a catch-up followed by a `QueueNext` frame on the device.

So the confirmation check became a pure function (`syncPlayQueue.queuedNextCount()`), and no manager spec sends a frame after a catch-up. Not checked: which thread copies the state, and whether creating `start` on the render thread (a helper node) avoids it. Close it when a manager spec can send a frame after a catch-up, or when docs/dev/unit-tests.md says why it cannot.

#### Capture a SyncPlay group's stream restart after a real playback error `[fid: syncplay-error-restart-uncaptured]` `[captured 2026-11-09]`

The stream restart after a playback error (`PlaybackErrorRetry.RESTART_STREAM`, from #1339) reports itself to a SyncPlay group through the reload path of decision `syncplay-reload-reports-its-start` (`VideoPlayerView.reloadInPlace()`, then `SyncPlayManager.playerEvent()` with the `reloaded` event), which `tests/rta/specs/syncplay-reload.spec.js` covers for a gear-pick reload. The error-triggered restart itself was never captured inside a group on that build: twice on 2026-11-08 (Streaming Stick 4K, Roku OS 15.3.4, Jellyfin 12.4, the server's ffmpeg killed and its segments deleted until the player reacted) Roku reported a stall instead of the `-5` error, and Roku decides which. Not done now because no way to make a stream error on demand exists yet (tech debt `playback-error-dialog-untestable-on-device`). Close it by capturing one error restart while following a group, checking it sends one Buffering and one Ready at the group's position with no corrective Seek (`syncPlayManager.sync.groupSeeks` unchanged), and adding that step to the spec once the error can be produced.

Captured once on 2026-11-16 (Streaming Stick 4K, Jellyfin 12.4.0, commit `fe2d3f16`): with the server's ffmpeg killed and its segments deleted for about 20 s while the Roku followed a playing group on a transcode, Roku raised the `-5` error (code 950) 54 s after the stall began. The server's 30 s group wait had already ended, so the restart ran while the group played on: one Buffering and one Ready, both at the group's position, with no Seek from the server. The member's catch-up seek then ran past its limit and the member asked the group to seek to it (`groupSeeks` 0 to 1, its own request), and it ended 40 ms from the group. Still open: a restart that lands inside the group's wait has not been seen, and the step is not in a spec, because producing the error needs control of the server's ffmpeg.

#### The two-pick reload fix has no device step outside SyncPlay `[fid: two-pick-reload-rta-outside-syncplay]` `[captured 2026-11-09]`

Decision `reload-in-place-one-path` fixed a second gear, track or version pick made while a reload loads restarting the video from 0, for every viewer, not only in a group. Its only RTA step (the two-pick step in `tests/rta/specs/syncplay-reload.spec.js`) runs inside a SyncPlay group, so it skips on any server without a push channel to the Roku, including the public demo server a plain `npm run test:rta` uses; unit tests reach only `reloadStartTicks()`. Not done in #1344 because whether the demo server can drive it is unchecked: the closed followup `reload-during-reload-stale-position` recorded the hero sitting at 300 s after a seek, and the OSD auto-hiding there. Close it with an RTA step that plays an item partway in with no group, makes two gear picks with the second landing while the first reload's node reads 0 and has not played, asserts the second reload's `content.PlayStart` is near the position before the picks, and fails once with `reloadStartTicks()` broken on purpose.

#### The RTA suite cannot stall a real video stream, so stall recovery has no device test `[fid: rta-cannot-stall-video-stream]` `[captured 2026-11-11]`

The RTA suite's request-failure rules (`rtaFailRequests`) reach only requests the app sends through its own API pool and `SideEffectTask`. The Video node fetches the stream itself, so no case can make playback stall for real.

Found 2026-11-11: the `syncplay-correction` case "a member's own stall recovery keeps its place" stages its stall by pausing the node over ODC and injecting a `buffering` player event. The SyncPlay comparison engine (`DECIDE_BY_COMPARISON`) reads that as a pause and undoes it, so the case is skipped under that engine (`skipUnderComparison`). Stall behavior (the Buffering and Ready reports of a SyncPlay member, the frozen-position watchdog, playback check-ins while buffering) therefore has no device test.

Why not now: it needs a new piece of test infrastructure and a choice of approach. Closes when an RTA case can make the stream itself stall (for example a local throttling proxy in front of the test server, or a server fixture that serves a segment slowly) and the stall-recovery case runs under the comparison engine.

#### The SyncPlay simulator never delivers a late result from an earlier try of a request `[fid: syncplay-sim-no-late-request-result]` `[captured 2026-11-11]`

The SyncPlay simulator never delivers a late result from an earlier try, so the stale-result flaw fixed in `bc9bcb4d` (a request's result matched to the try it belongs to) has only unit cases behind it, in `syncPlayGroup.spec.bs`.

`tests/source/shared/syncPlaySim.bs` delivers each request's result once, within about a tenth of a second of the send, and a lost request draws no result at all. So `syncPlayConvergeSim.spec.bs` cannot reach a result that arrives after the request limit has called its try failed and the retry is in flight.

Not done with the fix because it needs a new fault kind and its own invariants. To close it: add a fault that delivers a try's result after the limit and the retry, and assert the retry stays in flight and the run still ends in step.

While there: a simulator whose results never match their requests hangs the device run and no case fails (seen with its numbering broken on purpose, Stick 4K, 2026-11-11). It takes its number from `syncPlayGroup.nextRequestId` now, so that cannot happen by a reordering, but a run that cannot end should fail a case with a step limit, not hang.

#### Details-mode backdrop: no RTA test for Back from an item straight to Home `[fid: backdrop-details-home-back-untested]` `[captured 2026-11-12]`

`tests/rta/specs/backdrop-mode.spec.js` checks that Back from an item clears the backdrop in the Backdrop Images `details` mode on the library grid and on search, but not on Home. The direct check was dropped in `test(backdrop): Open the details-mode item from the poster grid, not a Home Latest row`: the public demo account excludes every library from Latest (`LatestItemsExcludes`, checked 2026-11-12), so Home has no stable row that opens an item, and reaching Home through the grid is vacuous because the grid clears the backdrop first.

Not now: it needs a Home row that opens an item on every run, which the shared demo server does not promise. Closes when such a row is available (for example a Continue Watching fixture the spec seeds itself) and the spec opens an item from Home, presses Back, and waits for `#imageFader.uri` to read empty.

#### The SyncPlay simulator never lets a seek fall back to the stream's start, so the reload that answers it has no sweep `[fid: syncplay-sim-seek-never-falls-back]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

The unit simulator (`tests/source/shared/syncPlaySim.bs`) lands every seek at or near its target. On a device, a catch-up seek on a transcode a few seconds old comes back playing from the stream's start (decision `syncplay-catch-up-fell-back-reloads`, traced 2026-11-15 on a Streaming Stick 4K), and the rule that answers it (`catchUpFellBack` in `syncPlayConverge.bs`) is covered only by three direct unit cases and a device run of `item-step, lost-seek, lost-seek`.

Why not now: the fix was proven on the device, and teaching the simulator a stream's start and age is its own change with its own sweep to re-baseline.

Closes when the simulator can make a seek fall back to the stream's start on a young transcode, and its sweep shows the member brought in by the reload.

#### An RTA run prints an uncaught `read ECONNRESET` and its source is unknown `[fid: rta-run-uncaught-econnreset]` `[captured 2026-11-16]`

An RTA run printed an uncaught `read ECONNRESET` (`node:internal/process/promises`, `triggerUncaughtException`) right after the RTA build deployed, then ran on and passed 400 of 400 steps (`syncplay-stress` mix, Stick 4K, 2026-11-16). Which process or socket raised it was not found: a promise in the runner or a library with no catch. A rejection nothing handles can end a run that should go on. Close it by rerunning `npm run test:rta -- syncplay-stress` with a short `RTA_STRESS_STEPS` list, finding where the rejection comes from, and catching it there. Not fixed now: the source is unknown and needs a reproduction first.

#### Upstream watch: drop the `roku-test-automation` closed-socket patch once upstream guards it `[fid: rta-closed-socket-patch-upstream]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

Watching upstream: `roku-test-automation` releases after 2.2.2 (including the 3.0.0-alpha line) that guard `handleClientSocketEvent()` and `closeSocket()` in `device/components/RTA_OnDeviceComponentTask.brs` against a client socket already closed.

Both functions call a method on `m.clientSockets[id]` without checking it, and `closeSocket()` deletes that id, so an event delivered for a socket already closed stops the app in the debugger with `runtime error &hec`. Every RTA case after it then times out. Seen once in a full `library-tabs` run on 2026-11-15, and reproduced on a device by opening and closing connections to port 9000 rapidly. 3.0.0-alpha.4 still has the unguarded code. `patches/roku-test-automation+2.2.2.patch` adds the two checks, and `tests/scripts/unit/rta-closed-socket-guard.test.js` pins them.

Not reported upstream yet: a report is a public post, so it waits for the operator's yes.

When it moves: if the release guards both functions, delete the patch and its test. Otherwise regenerate the patch on any version bump with `npx patch-package roku-test-automation`, since its filename pins 2.2.2. A bump the patch still applies to only warns about the mismatch. One it no longer applies to fails `npm ci` on CI, while a local install prints the error and carries on without the patch, and `rta-closed-socket-guard.test.js` then fails in `test:scripts`.

#### RTA specs still clear the settings they wrote by hand, which the signed-in seeds now reset `[fid: rta-hand-setting-cleanups-redundant]` `[captured 2026-11-18]`

Since the settings-reach-screen work (2026-11-18), `seedHome` and `seedHomeWithSavedServers` in `tests/rta/lib/seed.js` put the user back on default settings before each seed: every field of `JellyfinUserSettings.xml` in its registry section, and the `display.*` keys. Before that, specs cleaned up the settings they wrote by hand, and four still do: `dialogs.spec.js` (`PLAYBACK_SETTING_KEYS`, `seedPlaybackSettings(null)` in `afterAll`), `theme.spec.js` (`clearThemeKeys`, before each seed and in `afterAll`), `still-watching.spec.js` (an `afterAll` deleting `playbackPlayNextEpisode`) and `playback-advance.spec.js` (one deleting it and `playbackCinemaMode`).

They were kept because a spec after them that lands through a seed that signs nobody in (`seedUserSelect`, `seedServerSelect`) and then signs in through the UI would still see the setting. To close: check which specs after these four land that way and whether any reads a playback or theme setting; if none does, remove the hand cleanups (and the comments explaining them), then run the full `npm run test:rta` to prove the seed reset carries them. Not done in the same change because it widens a fix PR into four unrelated specs.

#### Delete the Rooibos patch when a brighterscript release carries its PR #1998 `[fid: rooibos-position-patch-delete-on-bsc-fix]` `[captured 2026-11-24]` `[waits-on: after:2026-12-15]`

`patches/rooibos-roku+6.0.0-alpha.58.patch` holds one fix: each statement keeps its position while Rooibos inserts coverage calls, so a local that shares its name with a namespace member is not transpiled as that member (rooibos#462). The fix in the compiler is brighterscript PR #1998, merged to its `v1` branch on 2026-11-20 and not in 1.0.0-alpha.58.

When a brighterscript release carries it (expected in the next alpha): bump, delete the patch, and run `npm run test:scripts`. `rooibos-coverage-local-names.test.js` must pass with no patch; if it fails, the release does not fix it, so regenerate the patch with `npx patch-package rooibos-roku`. Then update the patch-package paragraph in `docs/architecture/build-and-tooling.md` and the header of that test. Not now: the release does not exist yet.

#### `test:complete` stalls in the SyncPlay simulation spec, and two cases fail only under coverage `[fid: test-complete-stalls-in-syncplay-sim]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

`npm run test:complete` stalls in `tests/source/unit/syncplay/syncPlayConvergeSim.spec.bs`, at "with no faults, every run ends in step with no forbidden write". Measured 2026-11-24 on a Streaming Stick 4K: the case takes 21 s without coverage, and with coverage it printed nothing for 15 minutes (`ROKU_TEST_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS=900000`), on rooibos-roku 6.0.0-alpha.56 and alpha.58 alike. The spec arrived in #1355, after the coverage build last ran clean. Not checked: whether the case is very slow under coverage or never ends.

With the spec left out of the build, the coverage run finishes with one failure, and which one differs by Rooibos version: `ItemDetailsEpisode.spec.bs` "omits community and critic rating nodes when Disable Community Rating for Episodes is on" on alpha.58 (twice), a dialog focus case ("leaves focus alone when a dialog opened by its result handler...") on alpha.56. Both pass without coverage. The `ItemDetailsEpisode` case resets its settings right after writing `itemContent`, which an observer handles, so a timing dependence is likely and not proven.

To close: decide how the simulation suite and coverage coexist (a tag that `bsconfig-tests-complete.json` leaves out, as it does `measurement`, or a smaller seed range under coverage), then make the two timing-dependent cases wait for the state they assert. Run `npm run test:complete` to a full pass. Not now: it is broken on `main` already and needs a design choice. Findings are on issue #945.

#### A full RTA run whose first tests all fail runs for an hour instead of stopping `[fid: rta-run-no-stop-when-all-fail]` `[captured 2026-11-24]`

A full `npm run test:rta` on 2026-11-24 failed every test it reached (183 failed, 0 passed) for an hour, each waiting out its 10 to 20 second timeout. It started in the minute a Rooibos coverage run ended on the same device, and a spec from it passed alone right after. The cause is not established. `scripts/rta-run.js` has no guard for this shape, so the run costs its full length and the ledger gets an hour of noise.

To close: stop the run, with an outcome that is not `failed`, when the first N tests all fail (pick N from the ledger: a healthy run has never opened with that many), and say what the device answered at that point. Add a script test for the guard. Also worth checking then: whether a Rooibos test build left resident makes the next RTA deploy start in a bad state. Not now: it needs a threshold chosen from ledger data and a test. Findings are on issue #945.

### docs

#### Add the observed-field clause to `async.md`'s rendezvous cost model — but measure the grid path first. `[fid: async-md-observed-field-clause]` `[captured 2026-08-10]`

That section prices a crossing by how often you cross and how much you carry. Measured on Home 2026-08-09 (`.177`, 11 rows, n=10): a crossing into a field the receiving thread OBSERVES also costs that observer's **entire callback body**. Proven with a dose-response, not a contrast — three writes in one build differing only in what observed them cost **0.8 ms/row** (unobserved), **1.8 ms/row** (observed, empty callback) and **40.1 ms/row** (observed, callback sleeping a known 40 ms); slope 1.00, intercept ~1 ms. Matches `rokudev/dev-doc` v2.0 `DEVELOPER/core-concepts/threads.md` ("the response appears synchronous to the invoking thread"; "all observer callbacks on the fields are executed in the Render thread"). This is an **ADDITION, not a correction** — the doc's existing framing is confirmed by the same session's `appendChildren` result (176 crossings → 11 cut attach 849→328 ms), and its "expect a busy render thread to slow the Task down" warning correctly predicted a failed prototype. **GATE before editing anything:** run the same `xform`/`append`/`notify` split on `LoadItemsTask2` first. `decisions.md`'s `genre-skeletons-batched-not-per-row` attributes #779's `emit` 220→734 ms purely to crossing count, and blocking-on-the-receiver's-callback fits that data equally well — the two explanations prescribe different fixes (fewer crossings vs. less work in the receiver). Do NOT edit `async.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `components/CLAUDE.md` or the decision record until the grid path is measured; the evidence today is Home only.

#### A `dictionary.txt` word ending a sentence, followed by a non-text node, fails `lint:spelling`. `[fid: dictionary-word-before-non-text-node]` `[captured 2026-08-10]`

The trailing period is glued into the token (`handoff.`), and the personal-dictionary lookup is exact-match, so it misses. Measured 2026-08-10 by bisecting shapes — it is NOT specific to `decisions.md`, and NOT specific to headings, which is what an earlier note claimed. FAILS: word + `.` + heading / list / blockquote / thematic break / inline `code` span. PASSES: word + `.` + more plain text; word + `,` + anything; no trailing period; the word not last in its sentence; any base-dictionary word (`device.` is fine, `sideload.` is not). Bold/emphasis is irrelevant. Hit twice in one session: appending to `decisions.md`, and `...before the sideload.` followed by a code span in `testing.md`. **Fix:** a ~30-line `scripts/lint/spellcheck.cjs` that reads `dictionary.txt`, writes a derived word-list adding the `word.` form of each entry to a temp file, and execs `spellchecker-cli` with `-d` on both (the flag accepts multiple files). No committed generated artifact, no drift surface, unit-testable — and it moves the unreadable 12-flag `lint:spelling` one-liner out of `package.json`. Check whether `?` and `!` glue the same way; only `.` was measured. Do NOT adopt the earlier "add a word to the sentence" workaround — it is folklore for a bug with a deterministic fix.

#### The public docs document ONE maintainer's LAN topology as if it were the project's. `[fid: public-docs-one-maintainer-lan-topology]` `[captured 2026-08-12]`

`docs/dev/rta-tests.md`'s device-lock section carries a three-Roku table keyed on `.177` / `.178` / `.200`, and `docs/architecture/testing.md`'s agent paragraph tells the reader that "pointing a local run at CI's `.200` also needs `.200`'s password" — addresses on one contributor's home network, in a public repo, written as instructions to whoever clones it. Nobody else has a `.177`. Caught 2026-08-12 while writing a NEW baseline section in the same voice ("run the baseline on `.177`"); that section was rewritten in device **roles** before it shipped — "your device" (`ROKU_IP` in `.env`) vs "the CI device" (org secret) — which turned out to read *better* than the addresses, because the roles are what generalize and the reasoning (one device you can reach, one you cannot) is identical for every contributor. The pre-existing copies were left alone under isolate-the-fix. **Two kinds, and they are not equally wrong:** *instructions* keyed on an address are broken for every reader but one, while *provenance* ("measured at 13.6 min on `.177`") is a legitimate record of where a number came from — though even there the device MODEL serves an outsider better than the address. **Fix shape:** convert instructional uses to roles; leave provenance, optionally naming the device model instead. Not a security issue — private LAN addresses behind NAT — a usability one.

#### The LAN-topology sweep above has four more sites, and its inventory names none of them. `[fid: lan-topology-sweep-four-more-sites]` `[captured 2026-08-15]`

That entry lists `docs/dev/rta-tests.md` and `docs/architecture/testing.md` and explicitly leaves the pre-existing copies alone under isolate-the-fix — so the *fix shape* is settled but the *scope* was never enumerated, which is how a sweep half-lands. A 2026-08-15 scan (prompted by the maintainer mid-session, while measuring against their own server: *"careful about documenting my local env in this public repo"*) found the same **instructional** pattern in `docs/dev/home-first-paint-performance.md` — three `--server` command examples carrying the maintainer's SERVER address, ~lines 194 / 435 / 436 — plus one in `README.md` (~line 70). Note this is a different address from the `.177` / `.178` / `.200` DEVICE addresses the parent entry is about: it is the Jellyfin server, and it appears inside commands a reader is told to run, so it is squarely the "broken for every reader but one" kind rather than provenance. **The two code sites found in the same scan are already fixed** — `scripts/measure.js`'s usage header and `tests/scripts/unit/measurement-guard.test.js`'s fixtures were converted to the RFC 5737 documentation range (`192.0.2.x`) in `aaa9600c`, because that branch was already touching both files; the four docs sites were deliberately NOT swept in. **Fix shape:** same as the parent — roles, not addresses — and `home-first-paint-performance.md` is due a rewrite in Phase 3 of the measurement-platform work anyway, so folding it in there costs nothing extra. Filed separately from the parent only because the parent's inventory is what a fixer would work from, and these are not in it.

#### [`home-first-paint-performance.md`](dev/home-first-paint-performance.md)'s `size recompute` subtree has reached the markdown heading FLOOR — the next finding on that thread has nowhere to nest. `[fid: first-paint-doc-heading-floor]` `[captured 2026-08-26]`

It runs `h3` → `h4` → `h5` → `h6` (`size recompute` → `size recompute by` → `row removed` → `What row removed read`), and `h6` is the last level markdown has; a further sub-result would have to be filed under a heading that lies about its depth or as an unlabeled paragraph. The subtree is now ~370 of the file's ~890 lines, and the doc's own title is a HOW-TO ("Measuring orchestrator wait-vs-emit on device") — what has accumulated under it is a campaign log for one question, which is a different genre with a different reader. **Fix shape, in order of increasing diff:** promote `row removed` to its own `h2` or `h3` so the subtree flattens by two levels; or split the Home first-paint campaign history into its own doc and leave the method here, updating `related-files` on both and the topic map in [`architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md). Deliberately NOT folded into `perf/home-row-removal-phase-probe` (2026-08-25): a 350-line move would have buried that branch's substantive corrections in a restructure diff, and the thread it documents is PARKED, so the restructure is not urgent.

#### Prune `dictionary.txt` from [`build-and-tooling.md`](architecture/build-and-tooling.md)'s `related-files`. `[fid: prune-dictionary-from-build-tooling-related-files]` `[captured 2026-09-04]`

Same rule [ADR 0033](adr/0033-contextual-doc-freshness.md) applies to `en_US.json`: it is pure content data — one row per new spelling word, **42 commits in 90 days** — so it indicates no shape change, but it now fires the end-of-turn related-files reminder on every dictionary addition, and that reminder was just widened to `docs/dev/`. Measured while auditing the tracker, not folded into that branch under `isolate-the-fix` (the doc genuinely documents `package.json`, so the list needs a per-entry call rather than a blanket sweep — `package.json`'s 84 commits are mostly Renovate, but it also carries the npm scripts the doc describes).

#### Replace the free-text language-code settings with the existing `LanguagePicker` `[fid: free-text-language-codes-use-picker]` `[captured 2026-09-14]`

(#750 follow-up): `playbackAudioLanguageCustom` and `playbackSubtitleLanguageCustom` are `type: "alpha"` settings whose description literally reads *"Enter a 3-letter ISO 639-2 language code (e.g., eng, jpn, fra, deu, spa)"* — the user has to know ISO codes and type them on a remote. [`components/settings/LanguagePicker`](../components/settings/LanguagePicker.bs) already solves this (type-to-filter `DynamicMiniKeyboard` + `RadioButtonList`, 8 rows, matches on `name`/`nativeName`/`code`) but is wired to exactly one consumer: the sign-in-screen UI locale, with `settings.init()` hard-coding `m.languagePicker.languages = m.availableLanguages` from `locale/languages.json`. Fix: add a `languageSource` field to the setting definition so `settings.bs` can pick the list, and feed these two a media-language list (3-letter ISO 639-2 codes, matching `MediaStream.Language`) built from the local [`languages.bs`](../source/utils/languages.bs) map — no network call needed. #750 proves the second-consumer path by reusing the same component unchanged for the subtitle-search picker; this closes the loop so all three language pickers in the app look and behave identically. Kept out of #750 per `isolate-the-fix` — the subtitle feature doesn't need it.

#### Setting descriptions on the TV are outside the house voice, and the settings docs copy them from a second source that has drifted `[fid: in-app-settings-copy]` `[captured 2026-10-09]`

The setting text on the TV comes from `locale/custom/en_US.json` (each entry's `titleKey` and `descriptionKey`), but `docs/user/app-settings.md` is generated from the `description` copies in `settings/settings.json`. The two have drifted: on 2026-10-09, 2 of 74 descriptions differed (`uiTheme` and Custom Theme Colors). The docs-voice project left these strings alone, because changing them changes what users see in the app and what translators must redo.

To close it, as its own project:

- Add rules for TV text to `docs/dev/writing-style.md` (length, casing, tone), building on the casing convention in `locale/CLAUDE.md`.
- Rewrite the setting descriptions in `en_US.json`. Change values, not keys; to rename a key, use `npm run update-translations -- --rename Old=New`, which keeps its translations. Settings are saved under `settingName`, so a wording change needs no registry migration.
- Have `scripts/generate/settings-docs.cjs` read the English text from `en_US.json` through `descriptionKey`, so the docs show what the TV shows, and drop the duplicate `description` fields.
- Check the new text on a Roku for fit, and confirm what Weblate does to existing translations when an English value changes.

`settingName`, `type` and the generated-file note stay on the page: it serves users and contributors alike (decided 2026-10-09).

### ci

#### Commits from a cloud session can carry a Claude identity and add a Claude co-author on squash `[fid: cloud-commit-identity-anthropic]` `[captured 2026-11-01]`

Five commits on #1299's branch had `noreply@anthropic.com` as author and committer, with the user's name. GitHub showed them under the `claude` account. Squashing #1073, whose branch commits had that identity and no trailers, put `Co-authored-by: Claude <noreply@anthropic.com>` on `main` in `0deef5b2`. The `attribution` setting in `.claude/settings.json` controls trailers and session links, and nothing shows it changes the git identity.

**Options:** `gate`: fail a PR in CI when a commit's author or committer is `noreply@anthropic.com`, and name the commits to fix. `setup`: set the git identity in the cloud environment, if it allows that. `manual`: check before each merge.

**Recommended:** `gate`, because it holds wherever a commit was made.

**Not checked:** where cloud sessions get this identity; whether the environment can override it; whether GitHub adds the line on every squash, since #1073 is the only example found.

### Components

#### A SyncPlay previous step right after a transcoding item's Ready can leave the old stream playing `[fid: syncplay-previous-reload-never-swaps]` `[captured 2026-11-08]`

`syncplay-controls`' case "next and previous from the remote step the whole group" failed once at `2d6dac7b` (2026-11-07, Stick 4K, local Jellyfin 12.4.0 server), waiting 90 s for "the first movie playing again on this Roku". It is 1 failure in 25 full runs at committed heads (10 of 11 passed at `2d6dac7b`, 14 of 14 at `7e5f8a5d`), on a path that branch does not change (`PlayerHostView` untouched; the only `SyncPlayManager` change on it is the no-op `endUnansweredRefollow()` at the top of each queue fetch).

The server log: the Roku sent `PreviousItem` 1.2 s after its Ready on the second movie, whose stream was transcoding; it reported Buffering and posted `PlaybackInfo` for the first movie, but never started that stream, and its progress reports for the second movie's play session went on for 35 s until the server's 30 s wait for Ready gave up. So the reload in place (`PlayerHostView.skipToCurrentQueueItem`, `destroyPlayer` then `mountPlayer`) apparently never swapped the stream.

Not checked: the Roku console for the failed run (the next run overwrote it); why the reload did not swap; whether `main` can fail the same way (5 passing runs there, too few to rule out a 1-in-25 failure).

Next time it fails: read the kept `console-failed-<time>.log` beside the run's `console.log` (`.device-runs/rta/runs/<device key>/`) around the `PreviousItem` frame, and the server log for the same minute, then run /snag on what they show.

### locale

#### en_US.json has no punctuation rule by string role, which blocks seeding keys whose punctuation differs `[fid: punctuation-standard-by-string-role]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

`en_US.json` has no punctuation rule by string role. Setting descriptions end with a period, but toasts, badges, buttons and status phrases are mixed, and `locale/CLAUDE.md` covers casing only. Since `/translation-import` batch 1, punctuation counts as shape when mapping (`.claude/skills/translation-import/SKILL.md`, `docs/dev/translations.md`), so a key whose punctuation differs from a source's can't be seeded.

The case that surfaced it: `LabelRecordingScheduled` ("Recording scheduled", the toast in `source/utils/liveTv.bs`). the web client's `RecordingScheduled` is the same toast but reads "Recording scheduled." with a period, so batch 1 left it unmapped (about 53 cells).

To close: decide the punctuation for each role, write it in `locale/CLAUDE.md` beside the casing rule, then map `LabelRecordingScheduled` if its punctuation now matches. Not done now because it needs the owner's call on each role, and changing an English string means re-review for any seed map entry that matched it.

#### Mapped count keys seed nothing until their Zero forms are mapped `[fid: count-keys-need-zero-forms]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

The first `/translation-import` batch mapped 11 count keys (`LabelAlbumCountMany`, `LabelEpisodeCountOne` and the like), but they seed nothing: `planSeed()` fills a plural form only when its locale gets all three forms, and no source has the `Zero` strings ("No songs", "No albums" …) as exact text. `LabelAlbumCountOne` also has an exact candidate (the web client's `ValueOneAlbum`).

To close: find the `Zero` forms in the sources by meaning (a manual search, since `suggest` matches English text only), map them with `LabelAlbumCountOne`, and check the dry run fills the count sets.

#### Three seed candidates from translation-import batch 1 need a person's call `[fid: translation-import-batch-1-leave-list]` `[captured 2026-11-15]`

The first `/translation-import` batch left these candidates for a person, writing no entry:

- `LabelPlaybackErrorPlayMethod`: the web client's `LabelPlayMethod` matches by name and values, but has no use in its code.
- `LabelPlaybackErrorSectionMedia`: every candidate names the media-libraries section; ours heads the playing file's details (pt "Multimédia").
- `ButtonContinueWatching`: the iOS client's `continueWatching` has no use in its code, so whether it is a heading or a button is unclear.

To close: decide each, then map it or add a `reject:` line in `locale/seed/keymap.yml`.
